Do not enable it in production deployments. Use either of the following only for separate validation in an isolated environment:

```bash
# Direct node installation
sudo deploy/node/install-gpu-fault-collector.sh \
  <other required parameters> \
  --enable-node-log-collector

# deploy/hyperpod/deploy.sh or a single-node HyperPod Installer Job
export GPU_FAULT_ENABLE_NODE_LOG_COLLECTOR=true
```

#### NODE-5. Node Verification

The output of `verify` starts each line with `PASS`/`WARN`/`FAIL`/`SKIP`, with a final line `SUMMARY pass=<n> warn=<n> fail=<n>`;
the exit code is determined only by `FAIL`. GPU health checks (persistence mode per GPU, whether the DCGM exporter
returns metrics) only report `WARN`, the installation does not roll back because of them, and the installer writes
`/var/lib/gpu-fault/installer-degraded-gpu.json` (`observed_at` plus the
index/uuid/persistence_mode/error of each abnormal GPU); software integrity checks (systemd unit `is-active`, wheel digest,
Node Agent readiness probe, metrics reaching the control plane within 60 s) still `FAIL` and roll back. `nvidia-smi -L` enumerating
no GPU at all is still `FAIL`. A node installed with `WARN` should be handled as a lost card (usually REPLACE), and the Node Agent refuses
VERIFY/RESET for GPUs whose device node cannot be resolved: the error string
`cannot resolve device node for <uuid> (<reason>)` has three reasons, each handled differently:
`the driver reports no minor number for it` (`nvidia-smi -q -x` gave no minor for that card; the card is lost;
go the REPLACE route), `the driver reports device node /dev/nvidiaN for more than one GPU` (two cards
report the same minor; the driver state is abnormal; reboot the node first and look again), `no GPU on this node reports this UUID`
(the UUID carried by the workflow does not belong to this node: a stale plan or sent to the wrong node; check the incident's node binding).
Do not retry the reset in any of the three cases. A VERIFY without an explicit UUID now takes all GPUs of this node as targets, and if any of them cannot resolve
a device node the whole node refuses, rather than scanning only the resolvable ones and passing; when `nvidia-smi -L` enumerates none
it refuses likewise. Also affected are `UPDATE_SOFTWARE_FIRMWARE` and `REMEDIATE_DRIVER`: they VERIFY with the event's
GPU list (possibly empty), and on a node that lost a card these two steps are now refused as well and pushed towards REPLACE.

Before restarting `gpu-fault-node-agent` during an upgrade the installer first waits for `IN_PROGRESS` commands in the ledger to finish, and this wait
happens **before the first unit/env file rewrite** (waiting again before stopping and before restarting), so a wait that times out does not leave
a half-upgraded node with "new unit + old wheel", and it precedes the quiesce state recovery, so it does not pull up mid-way the GPU
services held by an in-flight command. The cap of the wait before writing is the smaller of 1900 s (matching the unit's `TimeoutStopSec=1900`) and
"remaining installation Job budget - 120 s - installation reserve": the Job's `activeDeadlineSeconds` (default 840) and
start time are passed into the chroot via `INSTALLER_ACTIVE_DEADLINE_SECONDS` / `INSTALLER_STARTED_EPOCH`, and the installation reserve
`INSTALLER_INSTALL_RESERVE_SECONDS` defaults to 420 s (measured installation plus verify duration plus margin), so under the default Job budget
it waits at most 300 s; a standalone EC2 installation without these variables uses 1900 s. If there are still in-flight commands at the cap, or the remaining budget after the wait is below
the installation reserve, the installer exits with
`node agent still has an in-flight command; retry later: <command ids>`, the Job is Failed,
and the reconciler retries with the existing exponential backoff; an unreadable ledger is treated as still having in-flight commands, not as empty.
The kernel collector's shutdown drain has its own upper bound: each record refused by the outbox gets one bounded delivery,
12 s plus one HTTP timeout (default 10 s, i.e. 22 s), with the unit's `TimeoutStopSec=30`; raising
`GPU_FAULT_COLLECTOR_HTTP_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` to 18 s or above requires raising that unit's stop timeout in step.
The unit uses `KillMode=mixed`: at stop only the main process receives SIGTERM, and a running `nvidia-smi --gpu-reset`
child process runs to completion before being reaped, so one `systemctl stop`/node shutdown may wait up to 1900 s, and when that exceeds
the REBOOT step's 600 s grace the control plane judges the step as timed out. The three reinstall steps (`REMEDIATE_DRIVER`,
`UPDATE_SOFTWARE_FIRMWARE`, `REMEDIATE_EFA_DRIVER`) are not bound by these 600 s; they have their own set of bounds:

- The per-step wait cap is given separately by `GPU_FAULT_WORKFLOW_INSTALL_STEP_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (default 1900, aligned with this unit's
  `TimeoutStopSec`), otherwise a 20-minute driver installation would be judged "over the per-step cap" on the control plane.
- A workflow containing a reinstall step raises, on the claim that stamps the execution deadline, the deadline floor to
  "reinstall cap + `GPU_FAULT_WORKFLOW_INSTALL_CONTAINMENT_SECONDS`" (default 600, 0 allowed), then caps it at the node
  workflow lifetime (default 3600 s), i.e. 2500 s by default; only the execution deadline is raised, the lifetime is untouched. The 600 s is reserved for the quiesce +
  `VERIFY_NO_GPU_CLIENTS` before installation (measured at about 300 s). On an ACTIVE node the SXID driver path and
  `REMEDIATE_EFA_DRIVER` first run `STOP_WORKLOADS`, which can itself use up the 600 s per-step cap, leaving only
  2500 - 600 - 300 = 1600 s < 1900 s for the installation: sites that expect to reinstall on active nodes should raise the value (for example 1200).
  When the lifetime falls in "reinstall cap <= lifetime < cap + margin", the control plane refuses at startup, and the error string names the three variables.
- The executor's execution limit (`GPU_FAULT_CLUSTER_EXECUTOR_MAX_EXECUTION_SECONDS`, default 1800) does **not** span the installation:
  in the regional architecture every poll of a reinstall step by the control plane is a separate short remote command that returns WAITING within seconds, and the executor cap
  constrains only a single command. The two bounds above are what really matter.
- Node side: the installation subprocess itself has an 1800 s cap (`INSTALL_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` in
  `gpu_fault.node_agent.operations.remediation`; the EFA "installation" is a 30 s `modprobe`). When the subprocess is killed on timeout, or the post-installation verification
  probe cannot run, the Agent returns FAILED with `retryable=false`, details carrying `outcome_unknown: true` and
  `manual_confirmation_required: true`, and an error string stating
  "node state is unknown and manual confirmation is required before any retry"; the control plane folds these two flags together with
  `failed_nodes`/`node_failures` into the step record, the escalation classification goes straight to the operator (phase `manual_confirmation_required`),
  and `REMEDIATE_EFA_DRIVER` does not climb to REBOOT via the step-level `failure_escalation_action` either. Only failures **before the installer starts**
  (the executable cannot start, the pre-installation `nvidia-smi` probe times out) remain retryable FAILED; the installer exiting cleanly with a non-zero code
  is a definite failure, non-retryable as before and without the two flags.
- The ledger schema is `user_version=3`: the `results` table gains a nullable audit column `agent_generation` (the Agent's own live
  generation at the time the command was accepted; rows written before v3 and commands accepted before the first successful heartbeat read back NULL). Old ledgers are migrated in place on open; v2 only does
  `ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN` without rerunning the v1->v2 table move.

DESTR-018-style time window compression is derived automatically by `scripts/e2e/regional/control_plane_env_window.py` from the lifetime
for the reinstall cap and margin, without manual synchronised lowering (see the eight-variable table of acceptance case DESTR-018).

```bash
sudo /opt/gpu-fault/verify
sudo systemctl status \
  gpu-fault-kernel-collector \
  gpu-fault-metrics-collector \
  gpu-fault-host-collector \
  gpu-fault-fabric-manager-collector \
  gpu-fault-node-agent
sudo journalctl -u gpu-fault-node-agent -n 200 --no-pager
curl --fail --silent --show-error \
  --cacert /etc/gpu-fault/node-agent.crt \
  "https://$(hostname -I | awk '{print $1}'):9099/healthz"
```

By default also confirm the NodeLogCollector is neither enabled nor running:

```bash
sudo systemctl is-enabled gpu-fault-log-collector.service  # expect disabled
sudo systemctl is-active gpu-fault-log-collector.service   # expect inactive
```

Uninstall:

```bash
sudo /opt/gpu-fault/uninstall
```

<a id="8-控制面参数"></a>
## 6. Control-Plane Parameters

<a id="81-启动与存储"></a>
### 6.1 Startup and Storage

| Environment variable | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `GPU_FAULT_EXECUTOR_MODE` | `simulation` | Only `active` really executes |
| `GPU_FAULT_STORE_URL` | Empty | Required in active; SQLite or PostgreSQL URL |
| `GPU_FAULT_EXECUTION_TOKEN` | Empty | Required in active, at least 32 characters |
| `GPU_FAULT_PROCESSOR_REPLAY_SECRET` | Empty | Required for the queued processor, at least 32 characters; must differ from the execution token, and replay accepts loopback sources only |
| `GPU_FAULT_ALLOWED_OPERATIONS` | Empty | Required in active, comma-separated |
| `GPU_FAULT_EXECUTOR_ID` | Automatic | Manual setting is not recommended with multiple replicas |
| `GPU_FAULT_WORKFLOW_LEASE_DURATION_SECONDS` | 180 | Minimum 30 |
| `GPU_FAULT_WORKFLOW_INSTALL_STEP_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` | 1900 | Per-step wait cap for the three reinstall steps `REMEDIATE_DRIVER` / `UPDATE_SOFTWARE_FIRMWARE` / `REMEDIATE_EFA_DRIVER`, replacing the generic 600 s; must not exceed the node workflow lifetime |
| `GPU_FAULT_WORKFLOW_INSTALL_CONTAINMENT_SECONDS` | 600 | Execution deadline floor of a workflow containing a reinstall step = reinstall cap + this value (2500 s by default, capped by the lifetime); >= 0, compression drills may set 0; sites that run `STOP_WORKLOADS` first on active nodes should raise it (e.g. 1200) |
| `GPU_FAULT_ENABLE_WORKFLOW_DISPATCHER` | true | Effective in active |
| `GPU_FAULT_ENABLE_WORKFLOW_PREEMPTION` | true | Allows a higher-tier workflow on the same node with the same attempt, or with neither side having a job, to set the lower-tier predecessor to `SUPERSEDED` at a safe step boundary; on by default, can be explicitly disabled during emergency troubleshooting |
| `GPU_FAULT_WORKFLOW_POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS` | 5 | Fallback scan interval of persistent workflows; remote completion, GPU metrics and host telemetry actively wake this Pod's dispatcher after being stored |
| `GPU_FAULT_WORKFLOW_BATCH_SIZE` | 100 | Per-batch cap |
| `GPU_FAULT_WORKFLOW_DISPATCHER_WORKERS` | 8 | Number of dispatcher threads executing different incidents in parallel; at most one workflow per incident per round |
| `GPU_FAULT_XID_CORRELATION_POLL_SECONDS` | 1 | Period for scanning expired XID 45 |
| `GPU_FAULT_XID_CORRELATION_LEASE_SECONDS` | 30 | HA final-processing lease; minimum 5 seconds |
| `GPU_FAULT_DEFAULT_RESTART_BUDGET` | 1 | Maximum automatic restarts of the same job when the Pod has no annotation |
| `GPU_FAULT_ACKNOWLEDGE_NO_ALERT_CHANNEL` | false | Emergency compatibility switch. Must not be set in production by default; startup fails when active and SES/dispatcher do not form an outbound channel |
| `GPU_FAULT_REQUIRED_AGENT_ARTIFACT_SHA256` | Required | Must be a non-empty 64-character SHA-256 when the Agent Registry is enabled |
| `GPU_FAULT_REQUIRED_AGENT_CONFIG_DIGEST` | Required | Must be a non-empty 64-character SHA-256 when the Agent Registry is enabled |
| `GPU_FAULT_REQUIRED_NODE_ACTION_KEY_VERSION` | 2 | Mandatory version after the whole fleet completes the node-specific key migration |
| `GPU_FAULT_POSTGRES_BOOTSTRAP_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` | 540 | Total cap for one bootstrap of the schema Job |
| `GPU_FAULT_POSTGRES_BOOTSTRAP_LOCK_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` | 60 | Cap on bootstrap waiting for relation/advisory locks |
| `GPU_FAULT_REMOTE_COMMAND_CLAIM_DEADLINE_SECONDS` | 900 | Dead-letter timeout of `PENDING` remote commands nobody claims; 0 disables |
| `GPU_FAULT_EXECUTOR_READINESS_MAX_UNCLAIMED_SECONDS` | 300 | Oldest unclaimed backlog age at which `/v1/regional/executors/readiness` judges not ready |
| `GPU_FAULT_EXECUTOR_READINESS_MAX_CLAIM_AGE_SECONDS` | 300 | Same as above: cap on the age of the executor's last successful claim |
| `GPU_FAULT_AGENT_ENDPOINT_ALLOWED_PORTS` | `9099` | Whitelist of ports a heartbeat may announce (comma-separated) |
| `GPU_FAULT_AGENT_ENDPOINT_ALLOWED_HOST_SUFFIXES` | Empty | Additional endpoint domain suffixes allowed beyond the node's own name/private address |
| `GPU_FAULT_AGENT_ENDPOINT_ALLOWED_CIDRS` | Empty | Single-cluster/Canary compatibility path only; regional production uses `agent_endpoint_allowed_cidrs` of each cluster registration |

Recommended phased allowlist:

```text
# Phase 1: evidence and isolation only
FREEZE_EVIDENCE,MARK_UNSCHEDULABLE,QUARANTINE

# Phase 2: add training control and validation
FREEZE_EVIDENCE,MARK_UNSCHEDULABLE,QUARANTINE,CHECKPOINT_WORKLOADS,STOP_WORKLOADS,RESTART_WORKLOAD,VALIDATE_GPU,VALIDATE_HOST,VALIDATE_FABRIC,RESTORE_SCHEDULING

# Phase 3: add after the node Agent drill
QUIESCE_GPU_SERVICES,VERIFY_NO_GPU_CLIENTS,RESET_GPU,RESTORE_GPU_SERVICES
```

Do not add operations that have no Adapter yet to the allowlist in an attempt to force execution.

The companion window of XID 45 is fixed by the current NVIDIA Catalog's
`companionWindowSeconds=30`. The first report returns `PENDING_CORRELATION`, with no
incident/workflow at that point. You can query the window deadline, lease and final
decision via `GET /v1/gpu-events/xid/<event-id>/correlation`. HA deployments must use PostgreSQL; SQLite is only for a single replica.

<a id="82-adapter-与-owner"></a>
### 6.2 Adapters and Owners

| Environment variable | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `GPU_FAULT_ENABLE_KUBERNETES_ADAPTER` | false | Kubernetes mutation |
| `GPU_FAULT_KUBERNETES_OWNER` | `gpu-fault-kubernetes-adapter` | Must match the profile |
| `GPU_FAULT_ENABLE_NODE_ACTION_ADAPTER` | false | Calls the Node Agent |
| `GPU_FAULT_NODE_ACTION_OWNER` | `gpu-fault-node-agent` | Must match the profile |
| `GPU_FAULT_NODE_ACTION_SECRET` | Empty | Same as on the nodes |
| `GPU_FAULT_NODE_AGENT_ENDPOINTS` | `{}` | Static JSON fallback; the registry takes precedence |
| `GPU_FAULT_EVIDENCE_OWNER` | `gpu-fault-control-plane` | Evidence step owner |
| `GPU_FAULT_VALIDATION_OWNER` | `gpu-fault-validation-adapter` | validation owner |
| `GPU_FAULT_VALIDATION_REQUIRE_RDMA` | false | Set true on RDMA clusters |
| `GPU_FAULT_POST_ACTION_VALIDATION_MAX_SAMPLE_AGE_SECONDS` | 420 | Validation window after actions such as RESET/REBOOT; must be no less than `GPU_FAULT_GPU_METRICS_SILENT_AFTER_SECONDS`; the default covers one 300 s edge-filter period plus transport jitter |
| `GPU_FAULT_MANAGED_RECOVERY_OWNERS` | The two HyperPod owners | Comma-separated |

<a id="83-fleet-compatibility"></a>
### 6.3 Fleet compatibility

| Environment variable | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `GPU_FAULT_ENABLE_AGENT_REGISTRY` | false | Enables the heartbeat registry |
| `GPU_FAULT_AGENT_REGISTRATION_SECRET` | node secret | Can be set independently |
| `GPU_FAULT_REQUIRED_AGENT_VERSION` | Unrestricted | Pinning 0.10.0 recommended |
| `GPU_FAULT_REQUIRED_AGENT_ARTIFACT_SHA256` | Unrestricted | Pinning the wheel SHA recommended |
| `GPU_FAULT_REQUIRED_POLICY_VERSION` | Unrestricted | Pinning the mapping version recommended |
| `GPU_FAULT_REQUIRED_RUNTIME_PROFILE_VERSION` | Unrestricted | Pinning recommended |
| `GPU_FAULT_REQUIRED_AGENT_CONFIG_DIGEST` | Unrestricted | Pin after rollout |
| `GPU_FAULT_AGENT_MAX_HEARTBEAT_AGE_SECONDS` | 90 | Should be 2-3 times the heartbeat interval |
| `GPU_FAULT_AGENT_MAINTENANCE_WINDOW_SECONDS` | 420, 30-3600 | After a quiesce, allows the same generation to continue reset/restore while heartbeats are paused; must not exceed the node fail-safe |

### 6.4 Diagnostics, Training and Evidence

| Environment variable | Default | Description |
|---|---:|---|
| `GPU_FAULT_ENABLE_TRAINING_HEALTH_MONITOR` | false | Not yet fully implemented and tested as a deliverable capability; must stay false |
| `GPU_FAULT_TRAINING_HEALTH_SCAN_SECONDS` | 15 | Scan interval |
| `GPU_FAULT_TRAINING_HEARTBEAT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` | 120 | Minimum 15 |
| `GPU_FAULT_TRAINING_STARTUP_GRACE_SECONDS` | 300 | Minimum 15 |
| `GPU_FAULT_TRAINING_MAX_STEP_LAG` | 20 | Minimum 1 |
| `GPU_FAULT_TRAINING_MIN_THROUGHPUT_RATIO` | 0.5 | 0-1 |
| `GPU_FAULT_EVIDENCE_RETENTION_HOURS` | 24 | Short-term evidence TTL |
| `GPU_FAULT_EVIDENCE_MAX_RECORDS_PER_NODE` | 10000 | Per-node cap |

<a id="85-gpu-阈值"></a>
### 6.5 GPU Thresholds

| Environment variable | Default |
|---|---:|
| `GPU_FAULT_GPU_TEMP_WARNING_C` | 85 |
| `GPU_FAULT_GPU_TEMP_CRITICAL_C` | 90 |
| `GPU_FAULT_MEMORY_TEMP_WARNING_C` | 90 |
| `GPU_FAULT_MEMORY_TEMP_CRITICAL_C` | 95 |
| `GPU_FAULT_GPU_TEMP_WARNING_MARGIN_C` | 5 |
| `GPU_FAULT_GPU_TEMP_SHUTDOWN_MARGIN_C` | 3 |
| `GPU_FAULT_MEMORY_TEMP_WARNING_MARGIN_C` | 5 |
| `GPU_FAULT_PCIE_REPLAY_RATE_WARNING_PER_MINUTE` | 8 |
| `GPU_FAULT_NVLINK_ERROR_DELTA_CRITICAL` | 1 |
| `GPU_FAULT_POWER_VIOLATION_DELTA_WARNING_US` | 1 |
| `GPU_FAULT_THERMAL_VIOLATION_DELTA_WARNING_US` | 1 |
| `GPU_FAULT_THERMAL_VIOLATION_DRAIN_CONSECUTIVE_SAMPLES` | 2 |
| `GPU_FAULT_TEMPERATURE_WARNING_GRACE_SECONDS` | 120; minimum 15 | Cool-down observation time after a temperature warning completes the DCGM diagnostic |
| `GPU_FAULT_DCGM_CORRELATION_WINDOW_SECONDS` | 45; 15-300 | Event time window for correlated analysis |
| `GPU_FAULT_DCGM_COMPOSITE_CONSECUTIVE_SAMPLES` | 2; 2-10 | Consecutive count for the thermal composite to escalate to DRAIN |
| `GPU_FAULT_DCGM_POWER_LIMIT_RATIO` | 0.95; 0-1 | Ratio of power usage to the enforced limit |
| `GPU_FAULT_DCGM_POWER_CORRELATION_MIN_UTILIZATION_PERCENT` | 80; 0-100 | Minimum GPU utilisation for the power correlation rule |
| `GPU_FAULT_TRANSIENT_GPU_WARNING_GRACE_SECONDS` | 120; minimum 15 | Observation window for corrected-memory Warnings |
| `GPU_FAULT_DCGM_ECC_SBE_DELTA_WARNING` | 1; positive | SBE counter delta threshold per sample |
| `GPU_FAULT_DCGM_RETIRED_PAGES_SBE_DELTA_WARNING` | 1; positive | Threshold for newly corrected retired pages |
| `GPU_FAULT_DCGM_RETIRED_PAGES_DBE_DELTA_CRITICAL` | 1; positive | Threshold for newly uncorrectable retired pages |
| `GPU_FAULT_DCGM_ROW_REMAP_CORRECTABLE_DELTA_WARNING` | 1; positive | Threshold for new correctable remaps |
| `GPU_FAULT_DCGM_CORRECTABLE_MEMORY_DRAIN_CONSECUTIVE_SAMPLES` | 3; 2-10 | Count for the corrected-memory composite to escalate to DRAIN |

Rule sources and default handling are as follows:

| Signal | Default behaviour | Source |
|---|---|---|
| PCIe replay rate `>8/min` | Warning, run GPU diagnostics | NVIDIA DCGM Health `DCGM_FR_PCI_REPLAY_RATE` |
| NVLink error counter delta `>=1` | Critical, stop affected jobs, isolate and analyse the GPU | NVIDIA DCGM Health `DCGM_FR_NVLINK_ERROR_CRITICAL` |
| Volatile DBE non-zero or increased | Critical, stop affected jobs, isolate and analyse the GPU | NVIDIA DCGM Health `DCGM_FR_VOLATILE_DBE_DETECTED` |
| Row-remap pending | Stop jobs, reset the GPU in a controlled window, then validate | NVIDIA GPU Memory Error Management |
| Row-remap failure | Stop jobs and isolate; RMA can be judged only after running the NVIDIA Field Diagnostic | NVIDIA GPU Memory Error Management RMA Policy |
| Aggregate DBE/uncorrectable remap counter increased | Warning, run diagnostics | Site conservative policy; the first scrape of a cumulative value only establishes a baseline |
| GPU temperature | Prefers the device slowdown/max-operating/shutdown limits; warning runs diagnostics, reaching the operating limit executes `DRAIN` | Site action derived from NVIDIA device limits |
| HBM temperature | Prefers the device memory max-operating limit; warning runs diagnostics, reaching the limit executes `DRAIN` | Site action derived from NVIDIA device limits |
| GPU/HBM fallback temperature | Uses 85/90 and 90/95 when the device exposes no limits | Site fallback |
| Power violation increased | Warning, run diagnostics | NVIDIA DCGM Health signal; the delta allows site override |
| Thermal violation increased | First Warning with diagnostics; continued growth escalates to `DRAIN` on the 2nd by default | NVIDIA DCGM Health signal plus site automated escalation |

NVIDIA references:

- [DCGM Background Health Checks](https://docs.nvidia.com/datacenter/dcgm/latest/user-guide/feature-overview.html#background-health-checks)
- [Row Remapping](https://docs.nvidia.com/deploy/a100-gpu-mem-error-mgmt/row-remapping.html)
- [GPU DRAM Memory RMA Policy](https://docs.nvidia.com/deploy/a100-gpu-mem-error-mgmt/rma-policy-thresholds-for-row-remapping.html)

When the PCIe, NVLink, power or thermal thresholds override the official defaults, the finding/incident's
`policy_source` is recorded as `SITE_OVERRIDE_NVIDIA_DCGM_HEALTH` and no longer masquerades as a pure
NVIDIA rule. `policy_reference`, `official_action` and the final `effective_action` are all
kept.

The old parameters `GPU_FAULT_PCIE_REPLAY_DELTA_WARNING` and
`GPU_FAULT_NVLINK_ERROR_DELTA_WARNING` are no longer used. PCIe must be normalised to a per-minute rate by adjacent sample times,
so that a change in collection period does not produce different results for the same behaviour.

The node collector reads and caches each GPU's slowdown, shutdown, GPU max-operating and memory max-operating temperatures using structured `nvidia-smi -q -x`.
The warning threshold is the device limit minus the configured margin; on reaching the device operating limit it stops the affected training, forbids scheduling, isolates and validates the GPU.
Only when the command fails, an XML field is missing, or the target GPU does not support the field does it use the fixed fallback values. Device limits are used only to derive
site actions, and the incident's `policy_source` is recorded as
`SITE_NVIDIA_DEVICE_LIMIT_DERIVED`, not masquerading as an NVIDIA-mandated automatic recovery action.

A temperature warning has the Node Agent run `dcgmi diag -r 1 -j`. Before deployment confirm that
`dcgmi` exists in the node image and that `GPU_FAULT_NODE_ALLOWED_OPERATIONS` also contains
`RUN_DCGM_DIAGNOSTIC`. Diagnostic output is saved in
`GPU_FAULT_DIAGNOSTIC_OUTPUT_DIR`. When a regional site extends the cooling observation period, change
`GPU_FAULT_TEMPERATURE_WARNING_GRACE_SECONDS` in the source Manifest
(`deploy/control-plane/base/control-plane-deployment.yaml` or the regional patch), render it via the renderer into
`regional/generated/gpu-fault-*-config-core.yaml` and release it with the checked release;
do not `set env` on the live Deployment:

```bash
# After changing GPU_FAULT_TEMPERATURE_WARNING_GRACE_SECONDS in the source manifest to the reviewed value (e.g. 180):
make PYTHON=.venv/bin/python deployment-contracts-update
# Build, verify signatures and release per section 4; do not edit the generated ConfigMaps directly.
gpu-fault-admin deploy --state-dir /secure/gpu-fault
```

Only the old single-cluster compatibility path distributes the same-named variable via Appendix A's `deploy/hyperpod/deploy.sh deploy`.

When the diagnostic returns `FAIL/INCONCLUSIVE`, or the warning persists after the observation period ends, the control plane executes
`DRAIN` and keeps the node isolated; this path does not automatically restore scheduling or restart training jobs.

After the diagnostic completes, an email with the fixed template `dcgm-diagnostic-zh-v2` is sent. The email contains the node, DCGM test,
status, entity, error code, a digest of the raw message, evidence URI, control-plane action and fixed remediation guidance.
The email does not use a large language model. Common action codes:

| Action code | Administrator handling |
|---|---|
| `THERMAL_COOLING_INSPECTION` | Inspect airflow, fans, heatsinks, ambient temperature and device limits |
| `PCIE_AER_INSPECTION` | Inspect the PCIe link, AER, replay counters and physical connections |
| `NVLINK_NVSWITCH_INSPECTION` | Inspect NVLink/NVSwitch, topology and Fabric Manager |
| `GPU_MEMORY_FIELD_DIAGNOSTIC` | Inspect ECC/row-remap and run the NVIDIA Field Diagnostic |
| `DRIVER_DCGM_REMEDIATION` | Inspect driver/DCGM/CUDA compatibility and services |
| `DEEP_DIAGNOSTIC_REVIEW` | Unrecognised result; review the complete evidence and run deep diagnostics |
| `DCGM_EXECUTION_REVIEW` | Inspect dcgmi/hostengine and stderr, then re-run the diagnostic |

Production should configure `GPU_FAULT_DIAGNOSTIC_S3_URI`, otherwise the `file://` URIs in the email can only be read locally on the
faulty node. The S3 bucket should have encryption, versioning, lifecycle and a bucket policy writable only by the control-plane/node roles.

Correlated analysis requires the exporter to additionally expose:

```text
DCGM_FI_DEV_CLOCK_THROTTLE_REASONS
DCGM_FI_DEV_POWER_MGMT_LIMIT
DCGM_FI_DEV_POWER_USAGE
DCGM_FI_DEV_GPU_UTIL
DCGM_FI_DEV_SM_CLOCK
DCGM_FI_DEV_MEM_CLOCK
```

Upgrade note: this version adds `DCGM_FI_DEV_CLOCK_THROTTLE_REASONS` and
`DCGM_FI_DEV_POWER_MGMT_LIMIT` as required DCGM core fields. The bundled
`deploy/dataplane/dcgm-counters.csv` already contains both; when the `existing` mode reuses a customer exporter,
missing fields refuse the upgrade at the deployment validation phase, and the exporter counters should be updated first. Use of
`NvidiaSmiMetricsCollector` is currently disabled by default. An explicitly enabled `nvidia-smi` fallback cannot reliably
obtain the full throttle bitmask or PCIe/NVLink counters,
the related correlated rules are not enabled, and missing signals must not be interpreted as healthy.

When the thermal composite is first Warning, GPU validation puts the temperature, thermal violation,
thermal throttle and `THERMAL_STRESS` together into the
`GPU_FAULT_TEMPERATURE_WARNING_GRACE_SECONDS` cool-down window. Within the window it returns `WAITING`
and does not `DRAIN` early because a throttle finding is still active. When later samples reach the configured consecutive count and
escalate to Critical it no longer waits and executes `DRAIN` immediately.

When tuning the correlation window and consecutive counts, the following are example configuration values for the source manifests, not an online environment override entry point.
Regional sites must release via `make deployment-contracts-update` -> build release -> `gpu-fault-admin deploy --state-dir`;
for the old single-cluster path see Appendix A:

```bash
export GPU_FAULT_DCGM_CORRELATION_WINDOW_SECONDS=60
export GPU_FAULT_DCGM_COMPOSITE_CONSECUTIVE_SAMPLES=3
export GPU_FAULT_DCGM_POWER_LIMIT_RATIO=0.90
export GPU_FAULT_DCGM_POWER_CORRELATION_MIN_UTILIZATION_PERCENT=75
export GPU_FAULT_TRANSIENT_GPU_WARNING_GRACE_SECONDS=180
export GPU_FAULT_DCGM_ECC_SBE_DELTA_WARNING=1
export GPU_FAULT_DCGM_RETIRED_PAGES_SBE_DELTA_WARNING=1
export GPU_FAULT_DCGM_RETIRED_PAGES_DBE_DELTA_CRITICAL=1
export GPU_FAULT_DCGM_ROW_REMAP_CORRECTABLE_DELTA_WARNING=1
export GPU_FAULT_DCGM_CORRECTABLE_MEMORY_DRAIN_CONSECUTIVE_SAMPLES=3
```

These defaults are a conservative site policy. Before go-live they should be adjusted according to GPU model, training cycle, historical corrected-error
baseline and NVIDIA support advice; the defaults must not be described as official NVIDIA RMA thresholds.

<a id="86-hyperpod"></a>
### 6.6 HyperPod

| Environment variable | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `GPU_FAULT_ENABLE_HYPERPOD_ADAPTER` | false | Self-built lifecycle Adapter |
| `GPU_FAULT_HYPERPOD_CLUSTER` | Required | HyperPod name |
| `AWS_REGION/AWS_DEFAULT_REGION` | SDK default | Region |
| `GPU_FAULT_ALLOW_HYPERPOD_MUTATION` | Unset (application default false) | Legacy compatibility switch; may only fall back to reboot when the separate reboot switch is absent; never falls back to replace. Regional production deployments must not set this variable |
| `GPU_FAULT_ALLOW_HYPERPOD_REBOOT` | The bare application default inherits `GPU_FAULT_ALLOW_HYPERPOD_MUTATION` (false when unset); the executor manifest explicitly sets `true` | Allows calling the SageMaker HyperPod reboot API, still subject to the Profile, signing, maintenance window and pre-action gates; does not replace the node instance |
| `GPU_FAULT_ALLOW_HYPERPOD_REPLACE` | Always false (solution invariant of §2.2) | The process refuses to start when set to true; this solution never calls the SageMaker HyperPod replace API, and the warm-spare local switch does not depend on this switch |
| `GPU_FAULT_ALLOW_WITH_AUTOMATIC_NODE_RECOVERY` | false | Extremely high risk; normally must not be enabled |
| `GPU_FAULT_HYPERPOD_OWNER` | `gpu-fault-hyperpod-adapter` | profile owner |
| `GPU_FAULT_ENABLE_HYPERPOD_MANAGED_OBSERVER` | true | Effective once the cluster is configured |
| `GPU_FAULT_HYPERPOD_IDENTITY_REFRESH_SECONDS` | 20 | Identity refresh |
| `GPU_FAULT_HYPERPOD_MANAGED_RECOVERY_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` | 1800 | Managed recovery timeout; also the per-step wait cap of `REPLACE_NODE`/`RESTART_NODE` |
| `GPU_FAULT_HYPERPOD_POST_REBOOT_STABILIZATION_SECONDS` | 60 | Device plugin settle time after a node reboot |
| `GPU_FAULT_KUBERNETES_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` | 30 | Cap per Kubernetes API call; HyperPod REBOOT/REPLACE now require the Kubernetes adapter to be present and able to read the target node, otherwise the step is `FAILED{safety_rejection}` |
| `GPU_FAULT_ENABLE_HYPERPOD_SPARE_FAILOVER` | false | Automatic spare switch |
| `GPU_FAULT_HYPERPOD_SPARE_LABEL` | `gpu-fault.io/spare` | spare label |
| `GPU_FAULT_HYPERPOD_SPARE_LABEL_VALUE` | `true` | spare label value |
| `GPU_FAULT_SPARE_HEALTH_SCAN_SECONDS` | 30 | spare scan |
| `GPU_FAULT_SPARE_HEALTH_FAILURE_THRESHOLD` | 2 | Consecutive failure threshold |
| `GPU_FAULT_SPARE_UNAVAILABLE_RECHECK_SECONDS` | 3600 | Periodic re-check interval of `UNAVAILABLE` spares; after a passing re-check it first enters `RECHECKING` and returns to `HEALTHY` after confirmation in the next round |
| `GPU_FAULT_SPARE_UNAVAILABLE_ALERT_SECONDS` | 86400 | Repeated administrator alert interval while a spare stays unavailable |
| `GPU_FAULT_MULTI_NODE_AGGREGATION_WINDOW_SECONDS` | 5 | Aggregation window for multi-node replacement of the same training attempt and cross-type DCGM metric/XID/SXID aggregation |
| `GPU_FAULT_MULTI_NODE_AGGREGATION_WINDOW_MAX_SECONDS` | 30 | Cap of the aggregation window after logarithmic scaling by the attempt's node count |
| `GPU_FAULT_PROCESSOR_DRAIN_MAX_WAIT_SECONDS` | 30 | Bounded wait during which PENDING/LEASED processor requests of the same cluster block aggregated workflow execution |
| `GPU_FAULT_PROCESSOR_WORKERS` | deploy.sh 24; bare application default 4 | Number of concurrent lanes per active consumer process; control events are serialised within a cluster, periodic telemetry is parallel per node. Under the role split it belongs only to `gpu-fault-control-worker`; setting it on the ingress tier has no effect (see the connection and thread budget above) |
| `GPU_FAULT_PROCESSOR_FAULT_IDLE_BACKOFF_MAX_SECONDS` | 0.5 | Polling backoff cap for a genuinely empty fault stream when not connected to PostgreSQL LISTEN |
| `GPU_FAULT_PROCESSOR_FAULT_BUSY_BACKOFF_MAX_SECONDS` | 0.1 | Fault claim cap when there is local in-flight work or a lane-blocked backlog is detected |
| `GPU_FAULT_PROCESSOR_NOTIFICATION_FALLBACK_SECONDS` | 5 | Fallback polling cap while LISTEN/NOTIFY is healthy; an arriving notification clears the backoff immediately |
| `GPU_FAULT_PROCESSOR_CONSUMER_PROCESSES` | 24 | Total consumer processes = control-worker replicas x uvicorn processes per Pod (6 x 4); the renderer writes it together with `NOTIFICATION_SHARDS`, and the runtime refuses a shard count below it |
| `GPU_FAULT_PROCESSOR_NOTIFICATION_SHARDS` | 24 | Number of advisory-lock owner shards for queue notifications; the default equals 6 worker Pods x 4 processes, so each process holds at most 1 shard |
| `GPU_FAULT_PROCESSOR_COMPLETION_CLUSTER_CONCURRENCY` | 1 | Number of cluster transactions that may be committed in parallel within one completion batch; production default 1, canary allows only 2/4 and must verify deadlock=0, consistent counters and no fencing regression |
| `GPU_FAULT_PROCESSOR_ROUTINE_STARVATION_SECONDS` | 30 | After priority 100 has waited longer than this in the GPU/Host main queue it is claimed at effective priority 49, avoiding permanent starvation by priority 50 |
| `GPU_FAULT_PROCESSOR_FAULT_PRESSURE_EVIDENCE_WORKERS` | 1 | Cap on execution workers each GPU/Host evidence pool retains while this process has priority 0; must be at least 1 |
| `GPU_FAULT_PROCESSOR_REQUEST_LEASE_SECONDS` | ingress/spool 30; worker 150; bare application default 120 | Active lane lease cap; the worker's long value covers the 120 s handler deadline, and the actual renewal deadline never crosses the request's hard deadline |
| `GPU_FAULT_PROCESSOR_REQUEST_RENEW_SECONDS` | ingress/spool 5; worker 10; bare application default 5 | Renewal interval during normal processing on an active lane |
| `GPU_FAULT_PROCESSOR_REQUEST_MAX_EXECUTION_SECONDS` | ingress/spool 20; worker 120; bare application default 30 | Request hard deadline; a timed-out result is not committed and the current Pod stops claiming |
| `GPU_FAULT_PROCESSOR_RETRYABLE_RESPONSE_MAX_AGE_SECONDS` | 300 | Bounded retry age for internally replayed 408/425/429/5xx; over-age requests are terminated to prevent an infinite poison queue |
| `GPU_FAULT_PROCESSOR_RETRY_BACKOFF_SECONDS` | 1 | Initial delay of a retryable replay; `not_before` is written after the request releases the lane, avoiding an immediate re-claim |
| `GPU_FAULT_PROCESSOR_RETRY_BACKOFF_MAX_SECONDS` | 30 | Cap of the retryable exponential backoff; STRICT requests still block the same lane while waiting, REORDERABLE requests may be safely overtaken |
| `GPU_FAULT_PROCESSOR_GPU_INVENTORY_STALE_SECONDS` | 180 | Stale PENDING GPU inventory is fast-terminated and does not enter policy processing |
| `GPU_FAULT_PROCESSOR_HEALTH_SUMMARY_STALE_SECONDS` | 420 | Stale GPU/Host summaries without abnormal edges/errors are fast-terminated; Faults are never dropped by this |
| `GPU_FAULT_PROCESSOR_OBSERVATION_STALE_SECONDS` | 120 | Evicts old Observations only when a newer attempt snapshot already exists |
| `GPU_FAULT_PROCESSOR_TRAINING_PROGRESS_STALE_SECONDS` | 120 | Evicts old Training Progress only when a newer rank heartbeat already exists |
| `GPU_FAULT_FAULT_ACTION_MAX_AGE_SECONDS` | 900 | Fault events are kept for audit, but over-age/previous-boot events must not automatically execute destructive actions |
| `GPU_FAULT_COLLECTOR_STARTUP_SPREAD_SECONDS` | 15 | Maximum stagger window for the first collection of ordinary Collectors; `TRIGGER_HEALTH_SNAPSHOT` bypasses this phase via the request file |
| `GPU_FAULT_PROCESSOR_EXIT_ON_DEADLINE` | true | Exit the process after a deadline fires and let Kubernetes recreate it |
| `GPU_FAULT_PROCESSOR_EXIT_GRACE_SECONDS` | 5 | Wrap-up seconds before an unhealthy process hard-exits |
| `GPU_FAULT_PROCESSOR_MAX_QUEUE_DEPTH` | Regional manifest 65536; bare application default 10000 | Regional processor queue admission cap |
| `GPU_FAULT_PROCESSOR_MAX_CLUSTER_QUEUE_DEPTH` | Regional manifest 4096 (configured by administrators via `processor.maxClusterQueueDepth` in `admin-config.yaml`); bare application default 1000 | Single-cluster processor queue admission cap |
| `GPU_FAULT_PROCESSOR_FAULT_RESERVED_QUEUE_DEPTH` | Regional manifest 8192; bare application default 2/5 of the total depth | Global reserved capacity for priority=0 fault events |
| `GPU_FAULT_PROCESSOR_FAULT_RESERVED_CLUSTER_DEPTH` | Regional manifest 512; bare application default 2/5 of the single-cluster depth | Single-cluster reserved capacity for priority=0 fault events |
| `GPU_FAULT_PROCESSOR_RETRY_AFTER_SECONDS` | 2 | Suggested retry interval after a capacity rejection |
| `GPU_FAULT_PROCESSOR_MAX_REQUEST_BYTES` | 16777216 | Hard cap on the processor request body |
| `GPU_FAULT_TELEMETRY_SPOOL` | ingress false, worker false, spool-worker true (but 0 replicas by default) | The regional base manifests do not write new telemetry into the spool by default; when enabling the pressure-relief path you must make ingress write to the spool and scale up the dedicated spool-worker at the same time, not just scale replicas |
| `GPU_FAULT_TELEMETRY_SPOOL_WORKERS` | 8 | Normal replay threads of a single spool-worker process; automatically drops to 1 with a priority 0 backlog. With spool enabled it validates `workers × REPLAY_BATCH_MAX_BYTES ≤ MAX_IN_FLIGHT_BYTES` (production 8 × 8 MiB = 64 MiB exactly), otherwise the last free slot cannot hold the head-of-queue batch and the claim repeatedly gives up and re-fetches (E-6). When unset on the worker role it is derived from the processor budget as 12, which does not error only because it does not start the spool consumer -- to enable spool on the worker it must be set explicitly to 8 |
| `GPU_FAULT_TELEMETRY_SPOOL_FAULT_PRESSURE_WORKERS` | 1 | Per-process replay cap while a priority 0 backlog exists |
| `GPU_FAULT_TELEMETRY_SPOOL_FAULT_PRESSURE_POLL_SECONDS` | 0.5 | Sampling period for the priority 0 backlog |
| `GPU_FAULT_TELEMETRY_SPOOL_MAX_ITEM_BYTES` | 4194304 | Cap for a single routine telemetry item to enter the spool; larger items stay in the processor main queue |
| `GPU_FAULT_TELEMETRY_SPOOL_REPLAY_BATCH_MAX_BYTES` | 8388608 | Byte cap of a single internal replay JSON; must hold the largest item plus envelope |
| `GPU_FAULT_TELEMETRY_SPOOL_MAX_IN_FLIGHT_BYTES` | 67108864 | Total byte cap of all replay futures of a single spool-worker process; the production value equals 8 maximum batches |
| `GPU_FAULT_TELEMETRY_SPOOL_MAX_DEPTH` | 65536 | Global spool admission depth cap |
| `GPU_FAULT_TELEMETRY_SPOOL_MAX_CLUSTER_DEPTH` | 1024 | Single-cluster spool admission depth cap |
| `GPU_FAULT_TELEMETRY_SPOOL_LEASE_SECONDS` | 60 | spool claim lease; after a process crash it expires and other replicas can re-claim |
| `GPU_FAULT_TELEMETRY_SPOOL_RETRY_BACKOFF_SECONDS` | 1 | Retry backoff after a real replay failure; local scheduling re-queues do not consume retry attempts |
| `GPU_FAULT_TELEMETRY_SPOOL_ADMISSION_PARTITIONS` | Regional manifest 8; bare application default 4 | Number of admission stripes across which consecutive full batches rotate |
| `GPU_FAULT_TELEMETRY_SPOOL_STORE_IO_WORKERS` | Regional manifest 8; bare application default 16 | Store I/O threads dedicated on ingress to cross-cluster spool admission; at most one in-flight flush per stripe |
| `GPU_FAULT_TELEMETRY_SPOOL_STORE_IO_MAX_IN_FLIGHT` | 256 | Cap on spool admissions waiting or executing in a single ingress process |
| `GPU_FAULT_PROCESSOR_CLEANUP_INTERVAL_SECONDS` | 60 | Whichever Pod obtains the separate `processor-cleanup` task lease periodically batch-cleans completed processor requests |
| `GPU_FAULT_PROCESSOR_COMPLETED_RETENTION_SECONDS` | 600 | Retention time of completed processor responses |
| `GPU_FAULT_PROCESSOR_CLEANUP_BATCH_SIZE` | 1000 | Per-run cleanup cap |
| `GPU_FAULT_POSTGRES_POOL_MIN_SIZE` | Regional manifest 2; bare application default 1 | Minimum Aurora PostgreSQL connections per uvicorn process |
| `GPU_FAULT_POSTGRES_POOL_MAX_SIZE` | Regional manifest ingress 40 / control-worker 24 / spool-worker 12; bare application default 8 | Maximum Aurora PostgreSQL connections per uvicorn process (the total must also be multiplied by `--workers` and the replica count) |
| `GPU_FAULT_POSTGRES_POOL_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` | 2 | pool checkout timeout |
| `GPU_FAULT_POSTGRES_POOL_MAX_IDLE_SECONDS` | 300 | Idle connection reclamation time |
| `GPU_FAULT_POSTGRES_POOL_MAX_LIFETIME_SECONDS` | 3600 | Maximum connection lifetime |
| `GPU_FAULT_FLEET_DEPLOYMENT_RETENTION_SECONDS` | 604800 | Retention of terminal fleet deployments; the periodic task deletes in batches |
| `GPU_FAULT_POSTGRES_AUTO_SCHEMA_INIT` | Bare application default false; regional manifest false | Only development environments explicitly set true to run schema DDL automatically; in regional production DDL may only be executed by the standalone ensure Job, and business processes only validate read-only |
| `GPU_FAULT_NVSWITCH_TOPOLOGY_MAX_AGE_SECONDS` | 300 | Acceptable topology freshness for UNKNOWN SXID scope |
| `GPU_FAULT_WORKLOAD_CONTEXT_FRESHNESS_SECONDS` | 600 | When the collector reports `workload_state=UNKNOWN`, the control plane backfills `IDLE` only if the cluster has any attempt observation within this window **or one Completion Watcher coverage heartbeat** (`POST /v1/attempts/coverage`, sent by the watcher after every completed full pass with zero running managed Pods and zero active attempts, built-in at most one per 120 s; a busy cluster proves the watcher alive through the observation stream itself) and no observation hits that node; neither observation nor heartbeat within the window -> stays `UNKNOWN`, and destructive node-health plans compile to `BLOCKED NEEDS_OPERATOR` (loss of monitoring is Unknown, fail closed). **Troubleshooting**: when a plan is BLOCKED because of `workload state is UNKNOWN`, first check that cluster's `get_workload_coverage_heartbeat` / heartbeat age (`observed_at` relative to now; heartbeats with non-zero `watched_pods`/`watched_attempts` do not count as coverage) -- a stopped watcher or a non-Ready Pod means UNKNOWN, which is expected; fix the watcher instead of releasing the plan; only a fresh heartbeat with UNKNOWN persisting is a control-plane problem. Before the heartbeat was added, an idle cluster with no managed jobs had every XID plan BLOCKED after 10 minutes, which was a misjudgement |
| `GPU_FAULT_NVSWITCH_TOPOLOGY_COMMAND` | Empty | Optional NVSwitch port topology query command |
| `GPU_FAULT_SUPPORT_ESCALATION_OWNER` | `gpu-fault-support-escalation` | Owner of the fixed-template ticket record/email Adapter |
| `GPU_FAULT_SXID_DRIVER_REMEDIATION_CODES` | Empty | List of SXID integers allowed automatic driver remediation; empty means disabled |
| `GPU_FAULT_SXID_FIRMWARE_UPDATE_CODES` | Empty | List of SXID integers allowed automatic firmware update; empty means disabled |
| `GPU_FAULT_TARGET_DRIVER_BRANCH` | Empty | e.g. `575`; required when the driver SXID list is configured |
| `GPU_FAULT_TARGET_FIRMWARE_VERSION` | Empty | The exact version validated by the site; required when any firmware flow is enabled |

When automatic spare switching is enabled, the control plane only picks nodes from the pool carrying
`gpu-fault.io/spare=true`. Each candidate node must simultaneously satisfy HyperPod
`Running`, `sagemaker.amazonaws.com/node-health-status=Schedulable`, Kubernetes
`Ready`, independent Agent fleet-ready, no active fault markers/findings, and not reserved by another incident.
When the required number of healthy spares is insufficient the whole replacement transaction fails without partial activation; this path does not call
HyperPod `BatchReplaceClusterNodes`.

Reset-failure escalation using a warm spare must additionally satisfy:

1. HyperPod `NodeRecovery=None`, avoiding double writes by managed replacement and this controller.
2. `GPU_FAULT_ENABLE_HYPERPOD_SPARE_FAILOVER=true`.
3. The Runtime Profile's `nodeReplace` owner is `gpu-fault-hyperpod-adapter` and cannot be delegated to
   `hyperpod-managed-node-recovery`.
4. The replacement step generated by escalation always carries
   `replacement_strategy=HEALTHY_WARM_SPARE_ONLY`. When the spare coordinator is not enabled, healthy
   spares are insufficient, or provider recovery is Automatic, the workflow fails and enters the hardware offline/ticket phase.

The NVSwitch topology query command must output the following structure; `switch_id` and `port` must match the SXID events:

```json
{
  "ports": [
    {
      "switch_id": "nvidia-nvswitch0",
      "port": "18",
      "link_scope": "ACCESS",
      "peer_type": "GPU",
      "gpu_uuid": "GPU-...",
      "fabric_partition": "cluster/node/local-nvswitch"
    }
  ]
}
```

Only `ACCESS` or `TRUNK` is accepted. On query failure, invalid output, stale records or conflicts between several trusted sources it stays
`UNKNOWN` and isolates without executing a reset. Ordinary fatal SXIDs with an explicit port on H100/H200/H20 may
be resolved automatically to `ACCESS` using the "single baseboard without trunk" platform topology constraint; always-fatal ones do not qualify for
that local inference.

<a id="9-collector-和-watcher-参数"></a>
## 7. Collector and Watcher Parameters

<a id="91-collector-公共参数"></a>
### 7.1 Common Collector Parameters

| Environment variable | Required / default |
|---|---|
| `GPU_FAULT_CONTROL_PLANE_URL` | Required |
| `GPU_FAULT_CLUSTER_ID` | Required |
| `GPU_FAULT_RUNTIME_PROFILE_VERSION` | Recommended required |
| `GPU_FAULT_CONTROL_PLANE_TOKEN` | Optional |
| `GPU_FAULT_GPU_PRODUCT` | Optional fallback; must be filled in `disabled` mode |
| `GPU_FAULT_GPU_PRODUCT_DISCOVERY` | `auto`; optionally `required/disabled`. Under `auto` when `nvidia-smi` is unavailable: if `GPU_FAULT_GPU_PRODUCT` is configured the configured value is used, otherwise it **starts without a product** and logs a WARNING (host telemetry keeps being delivered, and timeouts are reported as collection errors); only `required` refuses to start |
| `GPU_FAULT_DRIVER_BRANCH` | Recommended |
| `GPU_FAULT_CUDA_VERSION` | Recommended |
| `GPU_FAULT_METRICS_INTERVAL_SECONDS` | DCGM 15, nvidia-smi 30 |
| `GPU_FAULT_HOST_INTERVAL_SECONDS` | 15 |
| `GPU_FAULT_LOG_INTERVAL_SECONDS` | 10 |
| `GPU_FAULT_FILESYSTEMS` | `/,/var,/tmp` |
| `GPU_FAULT_REQUIRED_INTERFACES` | Empty |
| `GPU_FAULT_TRAINING_LOG_PATHS` | Empty |

The node-local Collector runs by default at startup:

```bash
nvidia-smi \
  --query-gpu=index,uuid,name \
  --format=csv,noheader
```

The system reads all visible GPUs, not just the first card, and normalises names such as `NVIDIA H200 NVL` to
`H200`. All GPUs must have unique UUIDs and the same model. When the discovered model disagrees with
`GPU_FAULT_GPU_PRODUCT`, mixed models are returned, or the CSV is corrupt, the Collector refuses to start.

- `auto`: prefers `nvidia-smi`; the explicit configuration may be used when the CLI is unavailable.
- `required`: refuses to start when auto-discovery fails, with no fallback.
- `disabled`: runs no command and uses only the explicit configuration.

The legacy generic Kubernetes Collector manifests in the repository are not implemented as a delivered capability; their images and
`REPLACE_WITH_GPU_PRODUCT` placeholders cannot be used in production. Regional HyperPod data-plane nodes are installed via the
Installer/Reconciler or systemd, and GPU product discovery uses `auto` by default.

| Environment variable | Default |
|---|---|
| `GPU_FAULT_LOG_STATE_PATH` | `/var/lib/gpu-fault/log-collector-state.json` |
| `GPU_FAULT_DCGM_METRICS_URL` | `http://127.0.0.1:9400/metrics` |
| `GPU_FAULT_COLLECTOR_OUTBOX_PATH` | `/var/lib/gpu-fault/outbox/<subcommand>.ndjson` |
| `GPU_FAULT_COLLECTOR_GZIP_MIN_BYTES` | `4096` |

Collector-to-control-plane delivery has three behaviours directly related to IOPS and bandwidth that must be confirmed at deployment:

- **durable outbox**: when the control plane is unreachable, events first land in NDJSON and are replayed after recovery. The path is derived automatically by default
  from `gpu-fault-collector <subcommand>` as
  `/var/lib/gpu-fault/outbox/<subcommand>.ndjson`, with no per-deployment setting needed. Collector containers running in
  Kubernetes use a read-only root filesystem, so a writable volume **must** be mounted at
  `/var/lib/gpu-fault` (the sample manifest uses an emptyDir with `sizeLimit: 256Mi`);
  otherwise outbox writes fail and buffered events are silently dropped.
- **gzip**: request bodies reaching `GPU_FAULT_COLLECTOR_GZIP_MIN_BYTES` are compressed. Both the control-plane
  processor and cluster-token middlewares decompress, so the default 4 KiB is safe;
  raise it only when you have confirmed some chain does not pass through these two middlewares.
- **connection reuse**: the same thread reuses HTTP/1.1 keep-alive connections for the same `(scheme, host, port)`
  (at most 8 per thread, LRU eviction), retrying once when a reused connection meets a silent close by the peer.
  For verification see the `ss -tan` count in the "Node verification" section.

<a id="92-completion-watcher"></a>
### 7.2 Completion Watcher

| Environment variable | Default / constraint |
|---|---|
| `GPU_FAULT_CONTROL_PLANE_URL` | Required |
| `GPU_FAULT_CLUSTER_ID` | Required |
| `GPU_FAULT_WATCHER_ENVIRONMENT` | `hyperpod-eks` |
| `GPU_FAULT_WATCH_NAMESPACE` | Empty, watches all namespaces |
| `GPU_FAULT_WATCHER_POLL_SECONDS` | 5 |
| `GPU_FAULT_WATCHER_WATCH_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` | 30 |
| `GPU_FAULT_WATCHER_CLEANUP_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` | 120 |
| `GPU_FAULT_PASSIVE_STOP_FALLBACK_SECONDS` | 30; an empty value disables the emergency fallback |
| `GPU_FAULT_PUBLISH_WORKLOAD_OBSERVATIONS` | true |
| `GPU_FAULT_DISCOVER_POD_GPU_UUIDS` | true |
| `GPU_FAULT_DEFAULT_RESTART_BUDGET` | 1 |
| `GPU_FAULT_WORKLOAD_LOG_TAIL_LINES` | 2000 |
| `GPU_FAULT_WORKLOAD_LOG_MAX_BYTES` | 262144; cap of the Aurora bounded tail |
| `GPU_FAULT_WORKLOAD_LOG_S3_URI` | Optional; `s3://bucket/prefix` for fallback log snapshots |
| `GPU_FAULT_WORKLOAD_LOG_S3_MAX_BYTES` | 104857600; per-Pod S3 snapshot cap |
| `GPU_FAULT_WORKLOAD_LOG_ANNOTATION_TAIL_BYTES` | 8192; Pod annotation tail surviving Watcher restarts |
| `GPU_FAULT_COMPLETION_WATCHER_METRICS_PORT` | 9109; the same HTTP server serves `GET /metrics` (`gpu_fault_completion_controller_*` counters and outbox gauges) and `GET /healthz`. All three startup/liveness/readiness probes read `/healthz`, so setting 0 or a bind failure is no longer "just missing metrics": the process keeps running, but kubelet repeatedly kills the Pod, and the failure shows up in the restart count |
| `LOG_LEVEL` | INFO |

The `cleanup timeout` should exceed the graceful exit time of distributed jobs and be below the scheduler's hard timeout. For large jobs, stress-test starting from 120-600 seconds.

After a critical rank exits abnormally, the Watcher first calls
`POST /v1/attempts/failure-detected`. The control plane creates and executes a
`FREEZE_EVIDENCE -> STOP_WORKLOADS` workflow. If the FAILED/TIMED_OUT terminal state of `POST /v1/attempts/terminal`
arrives before failure-detected, the control plane creates the same containment workflow directly from the terminal fields and queues the recovery workflow
after it via `predecessor_workflow_id`; the terminal report gets a decision immediately and does not return 409. Only when that request keeps failing for more than
`GPU_FAULT_PASSIVE_STOP_FALLBACK_SECONDS` does the Watcher suspend the workload directly
and write `gpu-fault.io/passive-stop-mode=emergency-fallback`. Production should not configure that value
shorter than a normal control-plane rolling upgrade or transient network jitter; setting it empty disables the direct suspend entirely.

The Watcher ServiceAccount must also have:

```yaml
- apiGroups: [""]
  resources: ["pods/log"]
  verbs: ["get"]
```

When `GPU_FAULT_WORKLOAD_LOG_S3_URI` is configured, give the Watcher a separate IRSA that allows only
`s3:PutObject` on the target log prefix. Do not reuse the executor role carrying HyperPod reboot/replace permissions.
An unreachable control plane does not affect this independent S3 chain.

Ordinary `/v1/workload-observations` are sent directly first; only on send failure are they written latest-wins per attempt into
`gpu-fault-completion-watcher-outbox`, and the next reconcile replays first, so the healthy path adds no
ConfigMap write amplification. The steady state lives in another object, `gpu-fault-completion-watcher-outbox-active`:
`active-attempts.json` saves state only when the attempt structure changes (about 833 B per Pod, within about 1080 managed Pods),
the Watcher can recover after a restart and publish `STOPPED` for disappeared Pods; a rolling upgrade does not overwrite the two existing
ConfigMaps, and when upgrading to this version the attempt state in the old object is migrated once when the Watcher first reads it; the migration is one-way
(the `attempt:*` keys in the old object are deleted), and after rolling back to the previous watcher there is no restart memory and it can only be rebuilt from live Pods. The Role's
`resourceNames` must list both objects; when `-active` reads 403/404 the Watcher runs degraded and sets
`gpu_fault_completion_active_state_unavailable` to 1 (one ERROR per hour); delivery is unaffected,
but there is no attempt memory after a restart.
`GpuFaultStaleAttemptObservation` means the CPU side still has an active-phase
Observation not refreshed for more than 120 seconds; that record has been excluded from ownership. The alert fires only after persisting for 2 minutes; troubleshooting should check the corresponding training Pod,
Watcher logs/Ready and outbox, and must not delete database rows directly.
After a terminal event is saved, the Store monotonically turns the Observation terminal in the same attempt transaction and refuses
later `PENDING/RUNNING`; the periodic hot-state cleanup automatically repairs historical terminal/active contradictions.
Therefore the cleanup gate must see both the terminal workflow/event and the terminal Observation, and cannot rely on only one of them.

After a fallback log snapshot is written to S3, the Pod annotation keeps only the record ID, URI, SHA256,
truncation marker and at most an 8 KiB tail; the complete usable snapshot is not written into Kubernetes metadata. After the control plane recovers
that manifest enters `WORKLOAD_LOG` raw evidence, cleaned up automatically after 24 hours by default. Full training logs
should be archived continuously by Fluent Bit/CloudWatch/the training application itself; this solution does not replace the cluster logging platform.

When `GPU_FAULT_DISCOVER_POD_GPU_UUIDS=true` and the Pod has no
`gpu-fault.io/gpu-uuids` annotation, the Completion Watcher runs the fixed read-only command
`nvidia-smi --query-gpu=uuid --format=csv,noheader` in the running managed
training container. The result is cached by Pod UID and re-queried after a Pod is recreated. The Watcher ServiceAccount must have `get/create` on `pods/exec`;
the Python Kubernetes client uses WebSocket GET, while kubectl exec usually uses POST/create.
The feature uses no shell and accepts no command arguments from the workload. On query failure, empty result, duplicate UUIDs
or non-GPU/MIG UUIDs the allocation stays incomplete, and the restart guard does not guess the source GPU count.
An explicit `gpu-fault.io/gpu-uuids` annotation still has the highest priority.

Restart budget configuration priority:

1. Pod annotation `gpu-fault.io/restart-budget`.
2. Watcher environment variable `GPU_FAULT_DEFAULT_RESTART_BUDGET`.
3. Code default 1.

The budget is persisted keyed by `cluster_id + gpu-fault.io/job-id`. An established job budget may not be silently modified by later
attempts; to raise the budget the administrator should submit a new job ID.

When a Job, PyTorchJob or JobSet is restarted successfully, the system generates a deterministic new
`gpu-fault.io/attempt-id` from the workflow operation ID and writes the new attempt ID, restart budget and restart count into
the workload root metadata and all new Pod templates. A retry of the same operation yields the same attempt ID and does not
consume the budget twice.

The control-plane ServiceAccount also needs `get/list/patch` on managed Pods.
`STOP_WORKLOADS` writes
`gpu-fault.io/termination-initiator-incident-id` before suspending, preventing a second recursive recovery flow
when the training process exits non-zero because of the deliberate stop.

<a id="93-training-progress"></a>
### 7.3 Training Progress

The current solution does not use the `TrainingProgressCollector` for now. The control plane and all deployment manifests set by default:
Training Progress is not yet fully implemented and end-to-end tested as a deliverable production capability and currently cannot be used in production.

```text
GPU_FAULT_ENABLE_TRAINING_HEALTH_MONITOR=false
```

Therefore no training health background scan starts, and no
`training_hang` finding is generated because a training job has no progress reporter deployed. The interfaces and commands below are kept only as a capability for later validation; until training Pod
reporter/sidecar injection, the progress file protocol and end-to-end validation are complete, it must not be changed to `true` in production.

The training container updates via atomic rename:

```json
{
  "step": 1200,
  "samples_per_second": 845.5,
  "loss": 1.73,
  "numerical_error": false,
  "checkpoint_ref": "s3://bucket/checkpoints/step-1200"
}
```

Start the sidecar/same-container reporter:

```bash
gpu-fault-collector training-progress \
  --attempt-id "$GPU_FAULT_ATTEMPT_ID" \
  --rank "$RANK" \
  --progress-file /var/run/gpu-fault/progress.json \
  --interval-seconds 15
```

<a id="94-collector-上下文与-hma"></a>
### 7.4 Collector Context and HMA

| Environment variable | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `GPU_FAULT_AFFECTED_WORKLOAD_IDS` | Empty | Comma-separated workload IDs; usually completed automatically by the control-plane topology |
| `GPU_FAULT_WORKLOAD_STATE` | `UNKNOWN` | `UNKNOWN/IDLE/ACTIVE` |
| `GPU_FAULT_CHECKPOINT_MANIFEST_REF` | Empty | Checkpoint manifest of the current workload |
| `GPU_FAULT_GPU_UUIDS` | Empty | Comma-separated GPU UUIDs used by Training Progress |
| `GPU_FAULT_KMSG_PATH` | `/dev/kmsg` | Kernel Collector input |
| `GPU_FAULT_TRAINING_PROGRESS_FILE` | `/var/run/gpu-fault/progress.json` | Progress file |
| `GPU_FAULT_TRAINING_PROGRESS_INTERVAL_SECONDS` | 15 | Progress reporting period |

The Kubernetes HMA watcher and the CloudWatch/Lambda/SQS forwarding chain have been retired and can no longer be enabled via environment variables.
Kernel/Fabric Manager collection stays independent; the AWS built-in HMA and the provider health boundary are unchanged.
The Managed observer is not a replacement for HMA forwarding; under `NodeRecovery=None` recovery does not depend on it.

The Completion watcher only lists Pods with `gpu-fault.io/managed=true`; this selector has no switch:
the solution's contract is that every submitted training job carries that label; unlabelled Pods are not listed, are neither attempts
nor counted as running Pods in the heartbeat. The early "observe-only mode" (`GPU_FAULT_COMPLETION_OBSERVE_UNMANAGED`
together with `GPU_FAULT_COMPLETION_OBSERVATION_RUNTIME_PROFILE` and
`GPU_FAULT_COMPLETION_OBSERVATION_RETENTION_CYCLES`) has been removed; if the site configuration still sets these three
variables, the environment validation at process startup treats them as unknown `GPU_FAULT_*` variables and errors or warns per
`GPU_FAULT_UNKNOWN_ENV_POLICY`; just remove them. JobSet/PyTorchJob jobs, like ordinary Jobs,
are aggregated per attempt as long as the Pods carry the managed label and attempt-id; there is no longer a side path synthesising attempts by `jobset-name`.

The Completion watcher also carries the **idle coverage heartbeat**: after every pass with no running managed Pods, no active attempts
and no new reconcile failures, it reports "cluster idle" once to `POST /v1/attempts/coverage`,
from which the control plane reads `workload_state` as `IDLE` instead of `UNKNOWN`, so that XID plans on an idle cluster are no longer uniformly
BLOCKED. The premise is the solution's existing contract: **every submitted training job carries the `gpu-fault.io/managed=true` label**;
the heartbeat is responsible only for labelled Pods, unlabelled workloads are outside the protection scope, and the watcher has no switch to relax the selector
(the former observe-only mode has been removed). The 120 s heartbeat interval is built into
the watcher, and `GPU_FAULT_WORKLOAD_CONTEXT_FRESHNESS_SECONDS` (default 600) must not be lowered below 600,
otherwise `UNKNOWN` gaps appear.

After go-live, verify the collection plane once with the following command:

```bash
gpu-fault-config collector-readiness \
  --url "${GPU_FAULT_CONTROL_PLANE_URL}" \
  --cluster-id "${GPU_FAULT_CLUSTER_ID}" \
  --execution-token "${GPU_FAULT_EXECUTION_TOKEN}"
```

The returned matrix contains each node's Agent state, the five systemd collection units, and the freshness of the GPU
inventory/metrics, host, kernel, Fabric Manager and node-log channels.
Each item's `unit_state` and `unit_enabled` always return strings; a NodeLogCollector disabled by default policy or whose enabled state cannot be confirmed
does not enter the required set.
The CLI accepts the report only when the returned `cluster_id` matches the request, `ready` is a real boolean, and the node results are complete and consistent with
the summary; the string `"false"`, empty nodes claiming ready, or a response from another cluster are all treated as
read errors. Exit code `0` means healthy, `1` means not ready in a valid report, `2` means a credential, transport or
report format error. The cluster ID is encoded as a single URL path segment.

| Environment variable | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `GPU_FAULT_FABRIC_MANAGER_JOURNAL` | true | Whether to read journald |
| `GPU_FAULT_FABRIC_MANAGER_IDENTIFIERS` | `nvidia-fabricmanager,nv-fabricmanager` | journal identity allowlist |
| `GPU_FAULT_FABRIC_MANAGER_LOG_PATHS` | Auto-discovered | When `LOG_USE_SYSLOG=0`, reads the existing absolute paths from the NVIDIA configuration; can also be overridden explicitly |
| `GPU_FAULT_FABRIC_MANAGER_STATE_PATH` | `/var/lib/gpu-fault/fabric-manager-collector-state.json` | journal cursor and file inode/offset |

#### HMA Forwarding Chain Retirement and Upgrade

The `kubernetes-hma` and `sqs-hma` subcommands, the two dedicated Deployment manifests, the Lambda/CloudFormation
templates, the deployment scripts and the four `/v1/provider-events/hyperpod-hma/*` routes have been deleted. The old paths return 404,
and a new release cannot be used to drain requests that still need the old entry points.

The candidate preflight of the administrator deploy checks two historical Deployments before DDL and CPU application changes:
`gpu-fault-hma-watcher`, `gpu-fault-hma-cloudwatch-consumer`. As long as the object still exists (including
replicas=0), or the query fails/returns an indeterminate state, the upgrade is refused. The check is read-only and does not delete
resources on the administrator's behalf. Environments that never deployed these two chains pass directly.
The same check also runs in the cluster-context preflight, covering the first deployment and application-layer rebuilds.

Historical environments must complete the following in a separately approved maintenance window while still running the old release:

1. Inventory the two solution-owned Deployments, their ServiceAccount/RBAC, CloudWatch Subscription,
   Lambda, CloudFormation stack, SQS/DLQ, IAM and artefact references, saving the resource registry and audit records.
2. Stop new forwarding, and under the old API drain or archive the queues, collector outbox and
   processor requests according to the approved data retention policy. Check or export the outbox before deleting Pods; do not throw away the only copy in an emptyDir.
   Keep unprocessable events in the DLQ as evidence; do not blindly replay or purge.
3. After stopping the flow and handling the evidence, decommission the two Deployments above and their dedicated RBAC/ServiceAccounts.
   Do not delete the AWS-managed `aws-hyperpod/health-monitoring-agent`.
4. Remove the HMA enable, queue URL and node prefix/regex parameters from the old configuration, then rerun the checked deploy.

The cleanup inventory keeps six historical Kubernetes resource identities for discovery and uninstall; undiscovered objects are not
fabricated as installed; the HMA collector/evidence/source values in old databases still decode. Removing the source does not delete
already existing AWS stacks, SQS/DLQ, IAM or logs; their retention and final destruction need a separate audit/approval.
This change does not modify the AWS HMA health labels, Node Agent quiesce container coordination, warm-spare `Schedulable`
check, `NodeRecovery=None` or the always-disabled provider replace constraint.

#### XID 154 Evidence and Correlation Acceptance

The Kernel Collector does not guess semantics from the action code; it accepts only the bracketed
labels explicitly listed by the NVIDIA Catalog. The write below only verifies the software chain and does not create a hardware fault:

```bash
printf '%s\n' \
  '<3>NVRM: Xid (PCI:0000:b9:00): 154, GPU recovery action changed from 0x0 (None) to 0x2 (Node Reboot Required)' \
  | sudo tee /dev/kmsg >/dev/null
```

During acceptance first write an XID 144-150 sample decodable as `XID_154_EVAL`, then within 30 seconds write XID
154; both must have the same node, GPU UUID/PCI BDF and boot ID. The expected primary decision has
`policy_source=NVIDIA_XID_154`, `official_action=RESTART_BM`, the XID 154's
`correlated_event_id` points at the primary, and the two events share exactly one incident/workflow. Removing the bracketed
label or changing it to an unknown label must yield `BLOCKED_MISSING_EVIDENCE` and must not execute reset/reboot.

The NVIDIA kernel message of XID 144-150 must carry the complete seven-register payload:

```text
NVRM: Xid (PCI:0000:BB:DF): <144-150> <sub-component> <fatality> \
<cross-contain> <injected> <link> \
(<intrInfo> <errorStatus> <debug0> <debug1> <debug2> <debug3> <debug4>)
```

The Collector sets `intr_info` from the first value inside the brackets and `error_status` from the second, and the control plane then
selects the V1/V2 Catalog pattern by the R575 boundary. Acceptance must check that the evidence keeps the complete original text,
that both fields in the normalized XID event are integers, and that the decision contains `decoded_subcode` and
`matched_decode_rules`. A simplified sample with only two registers should yield
`BLOCKED_MISSING_EVIDENCE` and cannot be used to verify real recovery actions. Samples that may decode to reset/reboot
may only be injected on already isolated B200/B300 test nodes.

<a id="95-node-agent-完整参数"></a>
### 7.5 Complete Node Agent Parameters

The installation script generates these variables; when deploying the Agent by hand you must configure them yourself:

| Environment variable | Default / constraint | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `GPU_FAULT_NODE_ACTION_SECRET` | Required | With version 2, the node-specific HMAC key derived by `cluster_id/node_id`; the node must not keep the fleet master |
| `GPU_FAULT_NODE_ACTION_KEY_VERSION` | Regional installation writes 2 explicitly; the process defaults to 1 when unset | `1` is only for rolling migration of old Agents; production must ultimately be node-level `2` |
| `GPU_FAULT_NODE_ALLOWED_OPERATIONS` | Generated by the installation script, containing `RUN_DCGM_DIAGNOSTIC` | The node's final allowlist |
| `GPU_FAULT_NODE_ALLOW_GPU_RESET` | CLI false; regional production true | reset master switch |
| `GPU_FAULT_NODE_ALLOW_FABRIC_RESET` | CLI false; regional production true | Separate master switch for all-GPU/NVSwitch reset |
| `GPU_FAULT_NODE_ALLOW_FABRIC_MANAGER_RESTART` | CLI false; regional production true | Separate master switch for Fabric Manager restart |
| `GPU_FAULT_NODE_ALLOW_SERVICE_QUIESCE` | CLI false; regional production true | quiesce/restore master switch |
| `GPU_FAULT_DIAGNOSTIC_OUTPUT_DIR` | `/var/lib/gpu-fault/diagnostics` | root-only local diagnostic bundle directory |
| `GPU_FAULT_DIAGNOSTIC_S3_URI` | Empty | Optional `s3://bucket/prefix` persistent evidence location |
| `GPU_FAULT_NODE_ALLOW_DRIVER_REMEDIATION` | false | Node master switch for driver remediation |
| `GPU_FAULT_DRIVER_REMEDIATION_COMMAND` | Empty | Absolute-path command; arguments may contain `{target}` |
| `GPU_FAULT_DRIVER_REMEDIATION_SHA256` | Empty | SHA-256 of the driver remediation executable |
| `GPU_FAULT_TARGET_DRIVER_BRANCH` | Empty | Integer branch exactly matching the control plane |
| `GPU_FAULT_NODE_ALLOW_FIRMWARE_UPDATE` | false | Node master switch for firmware updates |
| `GPU_FAULT_FIRMWARE_UPDATE_COMMAND` | Empty | Absolute-path command; arguments may contain `{target}` |
| `GPU_FAULT_FIRMWARE_UPDATE_SHA256` | Empty | SHA-256 of the firmware update executable |
| `GPU_FAULT_TARGET_FIRMWARE_VERSION` | Empty | Exact version exactly matching the control plane |
| `GPU_FAULT_FIRMWARE_VERIFY_COMMAND` | Empty | Absolute-path command whose stdout prints only the current exact version |
| `GPU_FAULT_FIRMWARE_VERIFY_SHA256` | Empty | SHA-256 of the firmware verification executable |
| `GPU_FAULT_QUIESCE_SERVICES` | Generated by the installation script, containing `nvidia-persistenced`, `gpu-fault-gpu-persistence`, DCGM/FM/collector/kubelet | Restore order; stop order is the reverse |
| `GPU_FAULT_QUIESCE_PROCESSES` | The set of NVIDIA/DCGM processes | Process names for exact TERM |
| `GPU_FAULT_QUIESCE_FAILSAFE_SECONDS` | 420, 30-3600 | Delay of the first automatic recovery |
| `GPU_FAULT_QUIESCE_RETRY_SECONDS` | 60, 10-600 | Automatic recovery retry period |
| `GPU_FAULT_QUIESCE_SETTLE_SECONDS` | 2, 0-60 | Wait after stopping services |
| `GPU_FAULT_QUIESCE_STATE_DIR` | `/var/lib/gpu-fault/quiesce` | root-only persistent recovery state |
| `GPU_FAULT_NODE_ACTION_DB` | `/var/lib/gpu-fault/node-actions.db` | Idempotency ledger |
| `GPU_FAULT_NODE_AGENT_HOST` | Node private address | The installer writes the InternalIP; it no longer listens on all interfaces by default |
| `GPU_FAULT_NODE_AGENT_PORT` | 9099 | Listening port |
| `GPU_FAULT_NODE_AGENT_ALLOW_PLAINTEXT` | false | Must be set explicitly to true without TLS; allowed only in an already isolated node network |
| `GPU_FAULT_NODE_CONTROL_PLANE_URL` | Conditionally required | Required when heartbeat is enabled |
| `GPU_FAULT_NODE_CLUSTER_ID` | Conditionally required | Required when heartbeat is enabled |
| `GPU_FAULT_NODE_ARTIFACT_SHA256` | Conditionally required | wheel SHA-256 |
| `GPU_FAULT_NODE_RUNTIME_PROFILE_VERSION` | Conditionally required | Agent profile |
| `GPU_FAULT_NODE_ADVERTISE_URL` | FQDN:9099 | URL reachable by the control plane |
| `GPU_FAULT_NODE_HEARTBEAT_INTERVAL_SECONDS` | 30, minimum 5 | Heartbeat period |
| `GPU_FAULT_NODE_INSTANCE_ID` | Node UID / instance ID | Fencing across node replacement |
| `GPU_FAULT_NODE_ACTION_RESULT_SIGNATURE_REQUIRED` | true | `GET /v1/node-actions/result` must carry `issued_at`+`signature` (same HMAC secret). Set false only during a migration window where the control plane has not yet rolled |
| `GPU_FAULT_NODE_ACTION_RESULT_SIGNATURE_SKEW_SECONDS` | 300 | Replay window of the result query signature |
| `GPU_FAULT_NODE_AGENT_TLS_CERT` | Empty | Listens on HTTPS when set; must be set together with KEY, and the advertise URL must be `https://` |
| `GPU_FAULT_NODE_AGENT_TLS_KEY` | Empty | Certificate private key |
| `GPU_FAULT_NODE_AGENT_TLS_KEY_PASSWORD` | Empty | Private key passphrase (optional) |
| `GPU_FAULT_NODE_AGENT_TLS_CLIENT_CA` | Empty | When set, **requires** client certificates verified with this CA, further constraining which control-plane callers may connect to 9099 |

If only one of the control-plane URL or cluster ID is set, the Agent refuses to start.

Authentication of the result query is by signature, not by port reachability: `command_id` is derived from the workflow idempotency key and
guessable, so an unsigned query is refused with 401 (`INVALID_SIGNATURE`). The signing side and the verifying side share the same protocol contract between
the control plane and the Node Runtime but use separate wheels; **the rollout order must be control plane first,
node bundle second**: an un-rolled Agent ignores the two extra query parameters, while an un-rolled control plane cannot
emit the signature. The physical artifact SHAs need not be the same; the protocol version and the compatibility window must match.

The endpoint announced by the heartbeat becomes the target URL of the control plane's POSTed signed commands, so it is restricted to
"the node's own name, or a private address, or an EC2 private DNS name (`ip-a-b-c-d...`)", the port must
fall within `GPU_FAULT_AGENT_ENDPOINT_ALLOWED_PORTS`, and no path, query or
userinfo is allowed. The current regional installation requires `https://<node-private-ip>:9099`, and the signed heartbeat
carries the node certificate for the control plane to pin.

On 2026-08-16 this was probed item by item on real machines (hitting the production control plane's
`/v1/fleet/agents/heartbeat` with the node's own cluster token, 10 malicious endpoints): the metadata service
`169.254.169.254`, loopback, public addresses, an attacker domain, with userinfo, with path, with
query, and the non-whitelisted port 8080 were all rejected with 409/422; only `172.20.0.1` (the ClusterIP of the Kubernetes API)
**passed the endpoint validation** and was finally stopped only by the unrelated incarnation lease.
The reason is that HyperPod's node_id does not encode an address, so the check is left with only "is it private". Therefore
regional production now additionally reads the node subnets from the
`RegionalClusterRegistration.agent_endpoint_allowed_cidrs` of the authenticated `cluster_id`; the same
`172.20.0.1` is rejected with `outside GPU_FAULT_AGENT_ENDPOINT_ALLOWED_CIDRS`, and an EC2 private
DNS name cannot bypass it either. Two clusters may declare the same CIDR, but requests are still bound to their own registration by the cluster token and node key.

At go-live set this value to the actual subnet CIDRs of the GPU nodes and run a set of positive and negative checks: legitimate node endpoints
must keep advancing heartbeats; the Service CIDR, a neighbouring VPC or addresses outside the whitelist must return 409
`outside GPU_FAULT_AGENT_ENDPOINT_ALLOWED_CIDRS`. The fix is to update `agentEndpointAllowedCidrs` of the corresponding
`site.yaml` cluster and go through the formal release-deploy; do not widen the control-plane environment variable online.

The current Node Action key version 2 derives the initial node-specific key with
`HMAC-SHA256(fleet_master, "gpu-fault/node-action/v1" || cluster_id || node_id)`.
The fleet master is kept only in the CPU control-plane Secret and in a short-lived 0600 file on the trusted deployment host;
the GPU cluster's `gpu-fault-node-action-keys`, the Cluster Executor and the
installer Job hold only per-node keys. A key leaked from one node cannot produce a legitimate heartbeat, command or result-query signature for another
node_id of the same fleet. During a rolling upgrade the control plane accepts versions 1/2 simultaneously according to the
`node_action_key_version` in the heartbeat; after all nodes are upgraded,
`GPU_FAULT_REQUIRED_NODE_ACTION_KEY_VERSION` must be pinned to `2`. Endpoint CIDR validation and
the Agent's own `TARGET_NODE_MISMATCH` remain as additional lines of defence. When probing this with a synthetic node_id,
the one that passes really creates an
ACTIVE record, and the cleanup must drain and then revoke (`revoke_agent` requires the same `transition_id`;
a bare revoke gets 409).

To rotate a single node, do not recompute the whole fleet:

```bash
export ROTATE_NODE='<target node_id>'
FLEET_MASTER_FILE="$(mktemp)"
kubectl --kubeconfig "${CPU_KUBECONFIG}" -n "${NAMESPACE}" \
  get secret gpu-fault-control-plane-active \
  -o jsonpath='{.data.node-action-secret}' |
base64 -d > "${FLEET_MASTER_FILE}"
chmod 0600 "${FLEET_MASTER_FILE}"

GPU_FAULT_KUBECTL_CONTEXT="${GPU_EKS_CONTEXT}" \
GPU_FAULT_NAMESPACE="${NAMESPACE}" \
GPU_FAULT_CLUSTER_ID="${CLUSTER_ID}" \
GPU_FAULT_HYPERPOD_CLUSTER="${HYPERPOD_CLUSTER_NAME}" \
GPU_FAULT_FLEET_MASTER_FILE="${FLEET_MASTER_FILE}" \
GPU_FAULT_ROTATE_NODE_ACTION_KEY="${ROTATE_NODE}" \
GPU_FAULT_CONTROL_PLANE_KUBECONFIG="${CPU_KUBECONFIG}" \
  deploy/node/provision-node-action-keys.sh
shred -u "${FLEET_MASTER_FILE}"

# The signers on both sides reload the key map; other Node Agents are not restarted.
kubectl --kubeconfig "${CPU_KUBECONFIG}" -n "${NAMESPACE}" \
  rollout restart deployment/gpu-fault-control-worker \
  deployment/gpu-fault-api-ha
kubectl --context "${GPU_EKS_CONTEXT}" -n "${NAMESPACE}" \
  rollout restart deployment/gpu-fault-cluster-executor

GPU_FAULT_KUBECTL_CONTEXT="${GPU_EKS_CONTEXT}" \
GPU_FAULT_NAMESPACE="${NAMESPACE}" \
GPU_FAULT_CLUSTER_ID="${CLUSTER_ID}" \
GPU_FAULT_HYPERPOD_CLUSTER="${HYPERPOD_CLUSTER_NAME}" \
GPU_FAULT_CONNECTION_MODE=regional \
GPU_FAULT_INSTALLER_ARTIFACT_SHA256="$(
  kubectl --kubeconfig "${CPU_KUBECONFIG}" -n "${NAMESPACE}" \
    get configmap gpu-fault-release-metadata \
    -o jsonpath='{.data.required-agent-artifact-sha256}'
)" \
  deploy/node/run-hyperpod-installer-job.sh --node "${ROTATE_NODE}"
```

When the required pin is `2`, the compatibility window is already closed: the FleetRegistry rejects
version 1 heartbeats before signature verification, and the NodeAction adapter also refuses to sign commands for historical version 1 records with the fleet
master. Only explicitly lowering the required pin to empty or `1` reopens the migration path; this must not be used
as a routine fault workaround.
When S3 upload is enabled, the node IAM role must have `s3:PutObject` on the target prefix; a local
`file://` reference cannot be read directly by the control plane. Remediation commands must be pre-installed by the image or configuration management;
event fields must not be assembled into shell commands, and the control plane must not distribute arbitrary commands.

GPU node installation creates and enables:

```text
gpu-fault-gpu-persistence.service
```

This oneshot runs after `nvidia-persistenced.service`:

```bash
nvidia-smi -pm 1
```

Both the installer and the verifier require `persistence_mode=Enabled` on every GPU, otherwise node installation fails.
The service also belongs to the quiesce/restore set: stopped before reset, driver or firmware maintenance, and run again after
`nvidia-persistenced` is restored. Check commands:

```bash
systemctl is-active gpu-fault-gpu-persistence.service
nvidia-smi --query-gpu=index,persistence_mode --format=csv,noheader
```

<a id="96-fleet-cli-transport-变量"></a>
### 7.6 Fleet CLI transport Variables

When `gpu-fault-fleet run-deployment` executes the transport command for each node, besides substituting the command template placeholders it also injects the `GPU_FAULT_FLEET_*` environment variables, including node, cluster, deployment, Agent version and artifact SHA. Transport scripts should read values from the environment variables, avoiding concatenating unescaped node data into shell.

The Fleet CLI's read-only `agents`, `readiness` and `deployment` also require
`--execution-token`; the deployment loop's status reads, next-wave requests and final readiness all carry the same credential.
Authentication required by the current API must not be omitted just because the interface is a read operation. The credential must not enter ordinary output or evidence.
`--wave-timeout-seconds` is timed from before the wave request and covers the local transport wait and the subsequent heartbeat wait;
a transport timeout stops subsequent waves and reports the remote completion state as unknown, which does not mean the remote installation or its sub-tasks were cancelled.
A single HTTP request still uses a separate 30-second timeout. Check the actual remote state before continuing.

<a id="10-邮件通知"></a>
## 8. Email Notifications
