A site has exactly one email channel. The default channel is the site SNS topic (`site.yaml` `spec.notifications.channel: sns`):
it carries both the AMP Alertmanager metric alerts and the business notifications sent by the control plane itself; the administrator confirms only one SNS subscription email,
with no SES identity to verify and no sandbox restriction. The cost is the medium: SNS emails are plain text, the sender is fixed as
`no-reply@sns.amazonaws.com`, and the subject must be a single line of ASCII of at most 100 characters -- notifications convert Chinese subjects to ASCII by
a deterministic rule (each run of non-ASCII replaced by one `?`, truncated to 99 characters) for the Subject, put the full subject as
the first line of the body, and the rest of the body is verbatim identical to the SES version. The control-plane role holds only `sns:Publish` on that topic.
Sites that need rich-text email, a custom sender or multiple recipients declare `channel: ses` in `site.yaml`; the SES description below
applies only to such sites. The release engine renders the channel and topic into the environment variables of the three control-plane roles:

| Environment variable | Description |
|---|---|
| `GPU_FAULT_NOTIFICATION_CHANNEL` | `sns` (default) / `ses` / `disabled`; when undeclared, inferred as `sns` with a topic ARN, `ses` with an SES address pair |
| `GPU_FAULT_SNS_TOPIC_ARN` | The site topic ARN when `channel=sns` (from `spec.health.snsTopicArn`) |
| `GPU_FAULT_EMAIL_SENDER` | `channel=ses`: the verified sender |
| `GPU_FAULT_EMAIL_RECIPIENTS` | `channel=ses`: administrator addresses, several separated by ASCII commas |
| `GPU_FAULT_ALLOW_EMAIL` | Master switch: `sns` defaults to `true` (a confirmed subscription is the authorisation), `ses` defaults to `false`, sending happens only when set to `true` |
| `GPU_FAULT_NOTIFICATION_ASYNC_DELIVERY` | `true`; business requests only write to the Aurora outbox |
| `GPU_FAULT_NOTIFICATION_DISPATCHER_ENABLED` | Usually the same as `GPU_FAULT_ALLOW_EMAIL` |
| `GPU_FAULT_NOTIFICATION_LEASE_SECONDS` | dispatcher claim lease, default 120 seconds |
| `GPU_FAULT_NOTIFICATION_MAX_ATTEMPTS` | Maximum attempts of the exponential backoff, default 8 |
| `GPU_FAULT_NOTIFICATION_BACKLOG_GRACE_SECONDS` | Watermark look-back window, default 300 seconds; backlog older than it is treated as history |
| `GPU_FAULT_NOTIFICATION_DELIVER_BACKLOG` | `false`; set `true` to send the entire historical backlog on first enablement |
| `GPU_FAULT_HOST_NOTIFICATION_COOLDOWN_SECONDS` | Cooldown of node resource trend emails, default 3600 seconds |
| `AWS_REGION` | SES region |
| `GPU_FAULT_SES_CONFIGURATION_SET` | Optional SES configuration set name, from `spec.notifications.sesConfigurationSet` (effective only with `channel: ses`; empty when undeclared) |

Regional sites declare the channel and notification fields only in `spec.notifications` of `site.yaml` (`channel`, `allowEmail`, `emailSender`,
`emailRecipients`, `emailSubjectPrefix`, `sesConfigurationSet`) and release via
the bound `gpu-fault-admin deploy`. deploy validates the address format; SES sender and recipients must
be given together; before any gate or build the preflight checks the sender's `VerifiedForSendingStatus` via SES v2
(sending one verification email and giving the rerun command when unverified), the account sending permission, and the addresses actually in the Secret.
Addresses are rendered into the `gpu-fault-system/gpu-fault-email` Secret and are not written in plaintext into ConfigMaps or Deployments;
the channel, sending switch and configuration set are rendered by the same renderer into the
`…-config-notification.yaml` ConfigMaps of the three CPU roles. These values must not depend on environment variables inherited in the deployment shell.

`sesConfigurationSet` accepts 1 to 64 ASCII letters, digits, hyphens or underscores. Once declared it must already exist,
with a matching name and `SendingOptions.SendingEnabled=true`; unknown or sending-disabled are both refused. deploy does not create that resource on your behalf,
and the executing identity needs `ses:GetConfigurationSet`. When undeclared no configuration-set check is added and the old
notification digest is kept; rollback restores the actually captured old ConfigMap and environment and does not write the candidate configuration set into the old version.
The validation entry point is `src/gpu_fault_release/regional_notifications.py::check_notification_channel`.

bootstrap grants the CPU Pod Identity role the least privilege of the selected channel: for SES an
`ses:SendEmail` restricted by `ses:FromAddress`, for SNS `sns:Publish` on the site topic. The SES preflight additionally needs
`ses:GetEmailIdentity`, `ses:GetAccount`; SNS sites need no SES identity.

When SES is needed, modify only the notification block of the generated site, not the identity, credentials, allowlist or release pins:

```yaml
# <state-dir>/site.yaml
spec:
  notifications:
    channel: ses
    allowEmail: true
    emailSender: gpu-alerts@example.com
    emailRecipients:
      - admin1@example.com
      - admin2@example.com
    # emailSubjectPrefix: "[PROD]"
    # sesConfigurationSet: gpu-fault-alerts   # optional: SES v2 configuration set name
```

First check the notification content with the preview API, then apply in the approved release window (while the SES identity is still awaiting email confirmation
you can add `--wait-for-email-confirmation <minutes>` to poll instead of rerunning repeatedly):

```bash
gpu-fault-admin deploy --state-dir /secure/gpu-fault
```

Production must keep sending and the dispatcher enabled; a drill configuration with sending disabled must not be treated as production-ready.
Only old single-cluster environments use Appendix A's `RUN_E2E=false deploy/hyperpod/deploy.sh deploy` to distribute the same set of
`GPU_FAULT_EMAIL_*` parameters.
With `GPU_FAULT_NOTIFICATION_ASYNC_DELIVERY=true`, notifications generated while sending is off keep being written to the
outbox. On its first run the dispatcher first establishes a watermark: undelivered backlog older than
`GPU_FAULT_NOTIFICATION_BACKLOG_GRACE_SECONDS` (default 300 seconds) is marked
`SKIPPED` / `DEAD` and is not sent, so first enablement does not dump the history on administrators in one go. The watermark is written by
the first dispatcher and persisted; later replicas and restarts follow the same line, and notifications generated afterwards are delivered as usual.
`suppressed_backlog` in the `dispatch` response gives the number suppressed. When one really needs to be resent,
call `POST /v1/advisory-notifications/{id}/send` to re-enqueue it -- but it only enqueues notifications with no delivery row or still
`PENDING`; rows already backed off by the dispatcher (`RETRY`) stay where they are, and dead-lettered ones (`DEAD`) return
`FAILED` with a reason containing `requeue it explicitly to try again` (control-plane review 2026-09-08, F-2:
the collection-silence scan repeating `send` every 60 s once made the retry budget unbounded and `dead_lettered_total` +1 every minute). Reviving
`RETRY`/`DEAD` only goes through the service layer `AdvisoryNotificationService.requeue`: it resets the attempt budget and reopens the
shelf life, and already `SENT` ones return `DUPLICATE` without resending. To resend the entire history set
`GPU_FAULT_NOTIFICATION_DELIVER_BACKLOG=true`, but confirm the backlog size first. The SES call itself is bounded:
connect timeout 5 s, read timeout 20 s, at most 2 attempts.

With `channel: ses` while SES is still in the sandbox, the sender and every administrator recipient must be verified in the same region;
production mode usually requires only the sender identity to be verified. The actual restrictions follow the target AWS account and SES region; `channel: sns`
has none of these restrictions.
HyperPod managed advisories, `NOT_APPLICABLE` XIDs, GPU count changes, restart budget
exhaustion and successful `RESTART_WORKLOAD` submission notifications share this configuration. Automatic restart notifications use the fixed
`restart-workload-zh-v1` template, recording the incident, job, original/new attempt, GPU count before and after the restart
and restart budget usage. That email means the Kubernetes restart operation was submitted successfully, not that
the training has passed health checks. The restart budget exhaustion notification is sent by the control plane's claim preflight
(`reserve_restart_budgets` -> `fail_restart_preflight`), no longer by the data-plane
Kubernetes Adapter: the workflow goes straight to `FAILED`, the incident to `ESCALATED`,
no adapter is called, and the details of the corresponding step carry `notification_id`. A
`RESTART_RESERVATION_MISSING` at dispatch (reserved at claim but the reservation is gone at dispatch) is a broken invariant and sends no email:
the workflow goes straight to `FAILED`, the incident to `ESCALATED`, surfacing only via the workflow ESCALATED alert
(`GpuFaultIncidentsAwaitingOperator`). `NOT_APPLICABLE` notifications use the fixed
`xid-not-applicable-zh-v2` template, filling only the XID event, policy decision, isolation workflow and evidence fields,
without generating administrator advice. Event ingestion only writes idempotently into the Aurora notification outbox; the standalone
dispatcher calls SNS (or SES) after claiming with owner, epoch and lease. Send failures are retried with exponential backoff
and do not change the result of event handling, GPU reset or workload restart. A notification result of `FAILED` is a terminal
semantic: written only when `GPU_FAULT_NOTIFICATION_MAX_ATTEMPTS` is exhausted or an inline send fails; deliveries under retry
show only in the delivery record's `attempts`/`last_error` and do not count as `FAILED`. The same incident, XID and
policy version use a fixed deduplication key, so repeated events are not sent twice.

Every XID that completes correlation also generates a fixed `xid-investigatory-zh-v1` administrator notification,
returning the notification ID in the decision's `investigatory_notification_id` field. The template fills only the XID,
product, Immediate Action, Effective Action, NVIDIA Investigatory Action, workflow
and evidence fields; the system does not execute the Investigatory Action automatically, and the administrator decides whether to investigate.
When the Catalog defines no Investigatory Action the field is `NONE`. Events in the companion correlation
window are sent after the decision completes. The deduplication key is composed of the Event ID and the template version, so a re-submission of the same event does not send
twice. `NOT_APPLICABLE` includes the field in its original notification without sending a second investigation email; a HyperPod
managed advisory and the per-XID investigation notification serve different purposes and may each produce one email.

Every SXID that completes a policy decision generates and queues the fixed
`sxid-event-zh-v1` administrator notification, unconditionally on classification, disposition or the existence of a recovery
workflow. The notification records the Collector source, SXID, classification, trusted link scope,
switch/port/PCI BDF, Official/Effective/Safety/Investigatory Action, incident,
workflow and evidence references, and returns the notification ID in the decision's `investigatory_notification_id`.
The deduplication key is composed of the Event ID and the template version; a re-submission of the same event does not send twice. Subsequent
`CHECK_MECHANICALS`, reset, reboot, training restart and other actions are notified separately with their own fixed templates.
A successful single-GPU `RESET_GPU` uses `gpu-reset-zh-v1`; a successfully submitted
`RESTART_WORKLOAD` uses `restart-workload-zh-v1`. When a workflow containing only forensic/diagnostic/validation steps
FAILs (the diagnostic reached no conclusion, the incident turns `RECOVERED` rather than `ESCALATED`, and the reason contains
`diagnostic inconclusive`) it uses `diagnostic-inconclusive-zh-v1`, category `ADVISORY`,
with deduplication key `<cluster>/<incident>/diagnostic-inconclusive/<workflow>`. The regional executor only executes actions and writes the
notification ID/action result back to Aurora; notifications are always sent by the regional control-plane dispatcher, and the GPU
cluster executor needs no SNS or SES permission.

When HostTelemetry finds `rdma_link_down`, aggregated RDMA errors, EFA
RNR/retry/CQ errors, or EFA traffic entering `SPIKE`, `DROP`, `ZERO_WARNING` or
`HUNG_SUSPECTED`, it generates and queues the fixed `efa-rdma-event-zh-v1` administrator notification.
The email records the node, metric, device/port, current value, traffic state and baseline, job/attempt,
diagnostic workflow and evidence references, without a large language model. Notifications are created only on health-state or traffic-state transitions;
the deduplication key is composed of the Event ID and the template version, so continuous abnormal samples and re-submission of the same event do not send twice.
`notification_ids` in the HostTelemetry API response returns the notification IDs created or matched this time.

When no notification channel is configured (`GPU_FAULT_NOTIFICATION_CHANNEL=disabled`) or the dispatcher is off, notifications stay in the Aurora outbox and can be queried via
`/v1/advisory-notifications/{id}`. The API returning a notification ID or `QUEUED` only means
enqueue succeeded; acceptance must wait for the result to be `SENT` with a non-empty `provider_message_id`. Historical notifications without
delivery records from before the upgrade are not enqueued automatically, avoiding replay of old alerts; administrators can re-enqueue a given notification ID
individually via `/v1/advisory-notifications/{id}/send`. If sender and
recipients are not provided on redeployment, the script deletes the `gpu-fault-email` Secret and keeps sending off.

<a id="101-gpu-数量变化审批"></a>
### 8.1 GPU Count Change Approval

Before an automatic restart the system compares:

- Source count: the number of unique GPU UUIDs in the failed attempt allocation.
- Target count: the total of `nvidia.com/gpu` or MIG resources in the Job/PyTorchJob/JobSet template.

The target count can also be declared explicitly on the workload metadata:

```yaml
metadata:
  annotations:
    gpu-fault.io/target-gpu-count: "4"
```

If it changes from 8 GPUs to 4 GPUs, the workflow stays `WAITING` and sends an administrator notification. The administrator needs to check
world size, data-parallel/model-parallel strategy, gradient accumulation, global batch size, learning
rate and checkpoint compatibility. After confirming, set the exact approval value on the original workload metadata:

```bash
kubectl -n <namespace> annotate job <job-name> \
  gpu-fault.io/approve-gpu-count-change='8:4' --overwrite
```

The Dispatcher re-reads the template on its next scan; it continues only when the approval value exactly matches the current actual source/target counts.
When the source or target count is unknown, automatic approval via annotation is not possible; a new job must be submitted manually.

Query the budget:

```bash
curl -fsS \
  http://<control-plane>/v1/restart-budgets/<cluster-id>/<job-id>
```

<a id="102-spare-不足时管理员手动降级恢复"></a>
### 8.2 Administrator Manual Degraded Recovery When Spares Are Insufficient

<a id="1021-适用范围和系统行为"></a>
#### 8.2.1 Scope and System Behaviour

This procedure applies to the following scenario:

- Several nodes of the same training attempt need replacement;
- The number of healthy, compatible warm spares is less than the number of faulty nodes;
- The administrator accepts reducing the number of training nodes and total GPUs;
- The training framework and checkpoints support resuming after a world-size change.

The current automatic flow requires a one-to-one correspondence between faulty nodes and healthy spares and does not support automatic partial replacement. For example, when the original job uses
3 nodes with 8 GPUs each, 2 nodes fail and only 1 healthy spare exists, the automatic flow does not replace only
one of them. The `REPLACE_NODE` step fails, the faulty nodes stay isolated, and the subsequent
`RESTART_WORKLOAD` does not execute.

This scenario must be handled as a new manual training attempt. Do not merely set
`gpu-fault.io/approve-gpu-count-change` on the old workload; that annotation can only release the GPU count gate of a workflow that has already reached
the `RESTART_WORKLOAD` step, and cannot make a failed `REPLACE_NODE` workflow continue.
A manual submission does not automatically increase the restart count in the control plane either, but later automatic recovery of the new attempt is still bound by
the original job's restart budget.

The operation does not write partial `node_rebindings` into the original workflow, nor does it turn the original workflow into a success.
From the control plane's perspective the original automatic recovery still failed; the new attempt, the manual reservation and the operations ticket together form
the subsequent audit record.

The following examples assume:

```bash
export CONTROL_PLANE=http://<control-plane>
export AWS_REGION='<aws-region>'
export CLUSTER_ID='<hyperpod-cluster-id>'
export NAMESPACE='<training-namespace>'
export JOB_ID='<original-job-id>'
export OLD_ATTEMPT='<original-attempt-id>'
export INCIDENT_ID='<incident-id-from-email>'
export WORKFLOW_ID='<workflow-id-from-incident>'
export BAD_NODE_1='<failed-node-1>'
export BAD_NODE_2='<failed-node-2>'
export SPARE_NODE='<healthy-spare-node>'
export CHANGE_ID="manual-${INCIDENT_ID}"
```

<a id="1022-第一步确认自动流程已经停止"></a>
#### 8.2.2 Step 1: Confirm the Automatic Flow Has Stopped

Query the incident and the workflow:

```bash
curl -fsS \
  "${CONTROL_PLANE}/v1/incidents/${INCIDENT_ID}"

curl -fsS \
  "${CONTROL_PLANE}/v1/workflows/${WORKFLOW_ID}"
```

You must confirm:

- The incident contains all the expected faulty nodes;
- The failed step of the workflow is `REPLACE_NODE`;
- `blocked_reasons` or the step error shows that healthy compatible spares are insufficient;
- `RESTART_WORKLOAD` has not completed;
- No other workflow is controlling the same workload.

List the Pods of the original attempt:

```bash
kubectl -n "${NAMESPACE}" get pods \
  -l "gpu-fault.io/attempt-id=${OLD_ATTEMPT}" \
  -o wide
```

The original attempt must have stopped with no Running or Terminating Pods. If Pods remain, first investigate
`STOP_WORKLOADS` and the training Operator's suspend state; do not start the reduced attempt directly, otherwise old and new
ranks may access the same checkpoint, output directory or rendezvous endpoint at the same time.

<a id="1023-第二步保持故障节点隔离"></a>
#### 8.2.3 Step 2: Keep the Faulty Nodes Isolated

Cordoning all faulty nodes again is idempotent:

```bash
kubectl cordon "${BAD_NODE_1}" "${BAD_NODE_2}"

kubectl get nodes "${BAD_NODE_1}" "${BAD_NODE_2}" \
  -o custom-columns='NAME:.metadata.name,UNSCHEDULABLE:.spec.unschedulable,READY:.status.conditions[?(@.type=="Ready")].status'
```

Do not uncordon faulty nodes that have not been replaced, and do not delete the incident, fencing or quarantine
information written by the control plane. Manual degraded recovery does not repair those nodes; it only removes them from the new training allocation.

<a id="1024-第三步复核并人工预留健康-spare"></a>
#### 8.2.4 Step 3: Re-check and Manually Reserve a Healthy Spare

First confirm the candidate node really belongs to the warm-spare pool and check the Kubernetes and HyperPod health state:

```bash
aws sagemaker list-cluster-nodes \
  --cluster-name "${CLUSTER_ID}" \
  --region "${AWS_REGION}" \
  --include-node-logical-ids

kubectl get node "${SPARE_NODE}" \
  -o custom-columns='NAME:.metadata.name,SPARE:.metadata.labels.gpu-fault\.io/spare,HYPERPOD_HEALTH:.metadata.labels.sagemaker\.amazonaws\.com/node-health-status,READY:.status.conditions[?(@.type=="Ready")].status,UNSCHEDULABLE:.spec.unschedulable,RESERVATION:.metadata.annotations.gpu-fault\.io/spare-reservation,POOL_STATE:.metadata.annotations.gpu-fault\.io/spare-pool-state,SPARE_HEALTH:.metadata.annotations.gpu-fault\.io/spare-health'
```

The candidate node must simultaneously satisfy (the first two are reported item by item by the read-only check of `gpu-fault-admin config spare --state-dir ... --node <spare>`; declaring and releasing also go through that command, see "Adding an independently repaired and re-accepted node to the spare pool" below):

- `gpu-fault.io/spare=true`;
- HyperPod state `Running`;
- `sagemaker.amazonaws.com/node-health-status=Schedulable`;
- Kubernetes `Ready=True`;
- Currently `unschedulable=true`;
- `gpu-fault.io/spare-health=HEALTHY`, or the field not yet written but the latest spare health
  controller scan explicitly found no health reasons;
- No other `gpu-fault.io/spare-reservation`;
- GPU SKU, GPUs per node, EFA count, driver, firmware and topology satisfy the reduced YAML.

`gpu-fault.io/spare-health=UNAVAILABLE` does not stay silent forever. The controller re-checks every 1 hour by default;
a persistent fault alerts repeatedly every 24 hours. After a healthy re-check the state transitions as:

```text
UNAVAILABLE -> RECHECKING -> HEALTHY
```

Operators must not manually delete
`gpu-fault.io/spare-health-unavailable-at` or
`gpu-fault.io/spare-health-last-alert-at`; the controller cleans them up automatically when the node returns to `HEALTHY`.
Invalid states, counts or stale incident annotations are handled in single-node isolation and do not block the scan of other spares.

Check the Node Action Agent readiness:

```bash
curl -fsS -X POST \
  "${CONTROL_PLANE}/v1/fleet/readiness" \
  -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  -d "{
    \"cluster_id\": \"${CLUSTER_ID}\",
    \"node_ids\": [\"${SPARE_NODE}\"]
  }"
```

Continue only when the response has `ready=true`. Then write the manual reservation first and make the node schedulable afterwards:

```bash
kubectl annotate node "${SPARE_NODE}" \
  "gpu-fault.io/spare-reservation=${CHANGE_ID}"

kubectl annotate node "${SPARE_NODE}" \
  gpu-fault.io/spare-pool-state=ALLOCATED --overwrite

kubectl uncordon "${SPARE_NODE}"
```

The first command deliberately does not use `--overwrite`. If the node has already been reserved by another incident or administrator, the command should
fail; the original reservation must not be overwritten. Once the reservation is written, the automatic spare allocator does not assign the node to
other incidents, and the spare health controller does not modify it during manual use.

<a id="1025-第四步修改训练-yaml-和训练参数"></a>
#### 8.2.5 Step 4: Modify the Training YAML and Training Parameters

Create a new file from the original, version-controlled YAML; do not modify the already failed Kubernetes object directly:

```bash
cp pytorchjob.yaml pytorchjob-reduced.yaml
```

Modify at least the following:

1. Use a new `metadata.name` to avoid overwriting the old PyTorchJob, Job or JobSet.
2. Adjust the replica count to the number of healthy retained nodes plus manually reserved spares.
3. Keep each Pod's GPU/EFA requests and limits consistent with the actual nodes.
4. Add required node affinity allowing scheduling only onto the retained healthy training nodes and the reserved spare.
5. Keep Pod anti-affinity so every training Pod lands on a different node.
6. Set a new checkpoint path or an explicit checkpoint to resume from.
7. Modify world size, data parallelism, tensor parallelism, pipeline parallelism and rendezvous parameters.
8. Recompute gradient accumulation, global batch size, learning rate and scheduler.
9. Confirm that optimizer state, sampler state and checkpoint shards support the new world size.

For example, when shrinking from 3 8-GPU nodes to 2 8-GPU nodes:

```yaml
metadata:
  name: training-reduced-a002
  annotations:
    gpu-fault.io/target-gpu-count: "16"
spec:
  pytorchReplicaSpecs:
    Master:
      replicas: 1
      template:
        spec:
          affinity:
            nodeAffinity:
              requiredDuringSchedulingIgnoredDuringExecution:
                nodeSelectorTerms:
                  - matchExpressions:
                      - key: kubernetes.io/hostname
                        operator: In
                        values:
                          - <retained-healthy-node>
                          - <healthy-spare-node>
    Worker:
      replicas: 1
      template:
        spec:
          affinity:
            nodeAffinity:
              requiredDuringSchedulingIgnoredDuringExecution:
                nodeSelectorTerms:
                  - matchExpressions:
                      - key: kubernetes.io/hostname
                        operator: In
                        values:
                          - <retained-healthy-node>
                          - <healthy-spare-node>
```

The affinity above must be merged into the original YAML; the existing EFA, shared memory, container resource and Pod
anti-affinity configuration must not be removed. If the framework cannot resume from a 24-GPU checkpoint onto 16 GPUs, stop this procedure and wait for
enough spares or start a new logical job from a compatible checkpoint/initial weights.

The change record should keep at least the following comparison:

| Item | Original attempt | New attempt |
|---|---:|---:|
| Node count | 3 | 2 |
| GPUs per node | 8 | 8 |
| Total GPUs | 24 | 16 |
| world size | 24 | 16 |
| Data/model/pipeline parallelism | Record the original value | Record the new value |
| gradient accumulation | Record the original value | Record the new value |
| global batch size | Record the original value | Record the new value |
| learning rate | Record the original value | Record the new value |
| checkpoint | Original manifest | Manifest used by the new attempt |

<a id="1026-第五步生成并审核新的-attempt"></a>
#### 8.2.6 Step 5: Generate and Review the New Attempt

Reuse the original `JOB_ID`, but you must use a new, never-used attempt ID. The restart budget must match the value
already established for that job:

`gpu-training-submit` is recommended for the formal resubmission. If an external training platform can generate and review the complete
managed YAML, a direct `kubectl apply` is also possible; what is forbidden is directly submitting raw YAML lacking
`gpu-fault.io/managed`, job/attempt IDs, role, expected ranks, runtime profile,
training container or restart budget. Missing these fields prevents the Completion Watcher, fault
attribution, restart budget and later automatic recovery from reliably correlating to the training job.

```bash
export NEW_ATTEMPT="${JOB_ID}-a002"

gpu-training-submit --site "${SITE_FILE}" pytorchjob-reduced.yaml \
  --job-id "${JOB_ID}" \
  --attempt-id "${NEW_ATTEMPT}" \
  --restart-budget <original-restart-budget> \
  --namespace "${NAMESPACE}" \
  --dry-run > /tmp/pytorchjob-reduced.managed.yaml
```

Review the rendered result before submitting:

```bash
kubectl apply --dry-run=server \
  -n "${NAMESPACE}" \
  -f /tmp/pytorchjob-reduced.managed.yaml

grep -nE \
  'name:|replicas:|gpu-fault.io/(job-id|attempt-id|expected-critical-ranks|target-gpu-count)|nvidia.com/gpu|vpc.amazonaws.com/efa' \
  /tmp/pytorchjob-reduced.managed.yaml
```

Here `kubectl apply --dry-run=server` only verifies that the rendered resource is accepted by Kubernetes and the training
Operator; it does not create or update any workload. After the review passes, the complete managed YAML may be formally submitted with
`kubectl apply` without `--dry-run`.

Confirm the new attempt, expected ranks, total GPUs, EFA count, checkpoint and all parallelism parameters before submitting:

```bash
gpu-training-submit --site "${SITE_FILE}" pytorchjob-reduced.yaml \
  --job-id "${JOB_ID}" \
  --attempt-id "${NEW_ATTEMPT}" \
  --restart-budget <original-restart-budget> \
  --namespace "${NAMESPACE}"
```

`gpu-training-submit` internally hands the fully injected YAML to `kubectl apply` via standard input, so the formal
submission is still managed by the unified tool while keeping Kubernetes server-side validation and audit behaviour.

Do not reuse the old attempt ID, do not overwrite the old workload, and do not manually change the original failed workflow to
`SUCCEEDED`. The original incident/workflow must remain as audit evidence of the fault and the manual takeover.

<a id="1027-第六步验证缩容-attempt"></a>
#### 8.2.7 Step 6: Verify the Reduced Attempt

Confirm there are only the expected number of Pods and that they are scheduled only onto the allowed healthy nodes:

```bash
kubectl -n "${NAMESPACE}" get pods \
  -l "gpu-fault.io/attempt-id=${NEW_ATTEMPT}" \
  -o wide
```

Verify item by item:

- Pod count, expected ranks and world size agree;
- No Pod is on a faulty node;
- Every Pod gets the expected GPU and EFA resources;
- NCCL uses EFA RDMA and all ranks join the same process group successfully;
- The checkpoint loads successfully with no shard, optimizer or sampler incompatibility;
- The collective canary/all-reduce result matches the new world size;
- loss, throughput, global batch size and learning rate match the manual change record;
- The Completion Watcher has observed the new `job-id/attempt-id/allocation`;
- No new XID, ECC, row-remap, NVLink or EFA errors within the observation window.

You can query the restart budget to confirm the manual submission itself did not unexpectedly increase the automatic restart count:

```bash
curl -fsS \
  "${CONTROL_PLANE}/v1/restart-budgets/${CLUSTER_ID}/${JOB_ID}"
```

Write `INCIDENT_ID`, `WORKFLOW_ID`, `CHANGE_ID`, the old and new attempt IDs, the node mapping, the YAML Git
revision, the checkpoint manifest and the parameter change table into the same operations ticket.

<a id="1028-失败回滚和-spare-释放"></a>
#### 8.2.8 Failure Rollback and Spare Release

If the new workload has not started or the submission verification failed, re-cordon the spare immediately:

```bash
kubectl cordon "${SPARE_NODE}"
```

After confirming no new training Pods are on that node, clear the manual reservation and restore the spare pool state:

```bash
kubectl get pods -A \
  --field-selector "spec.nodeName=${SPARE_NODE}"

kubectl annotate node "${SPARE_NODE}" \
  gpu-fault.io/spare-reservation-

kubectl annotate node "${SPARE_NODE}" \
  gpu-fault.io/spare-pool-state=AVAILABLE --overwrite
```

Only after the node is `unschedulable=true` again, the HyperPod/Kubernetes/Agent health checks pass and there are no
active fault markers/findings may it serve again as an available spare. Do not clear the
reservation while the reduced training is still running; that spare counts as allocated training capacity until the current attempt ends and the pool-exit checks above are done.

<a id="103-checkmechanicals-后由管理员提交明确处置"></a>
### 8.3 Administrator Submits an Explicit Remediation After CHECK_MECHANICALS

`CHECK_MECHANICALS` is an investigative action; it does not automatically cordon, stop workloads, reset or reboot. After sending the fixed notification the workflow
waits for administrator confirmation, with the wait capped at
`GPU_FAULT_OPERATOR_ACKNOWLEDGEMENT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (default 86400). The only formal entry point for the confirmation and the subsequent remediation
is one command; the former procedure of hand-assembling the annotation value and two JSON documents and calling the endpoints separately with the execution token and
cluster token is no longer used:

```bash
gpu-fault-admin submit-remediation \
  --state-dir /secure/gpu-fault \
  --incident-id "${CHECK_INCIDENT_ID}" \
  --disposition inspected \
  --reference CHG-12345
```

`--disposition` values: `inspected` (mechanical inspection complete, no further hardware action), `reset-gpu`,
`reboot-node`, `quarantine`, `restore` (not an answer to CHECK_MECHANICALS but the exit of a QUARANTINED
incident: after the node is repaired manually, creates a validated restore workflow under the same incident, see item 8),
`confirm-node-action` (likewise not an answer to CHECK_MECHANICALS: records a manual confirmation for a node action with unknown outcome on a BLOCKED workflow;
needs `--node`, see item 9).

1. **Applicability**: the incident's workflow is in `WAITING` at `CHECK_MECHANICALS`; the incident
   and workflow have the same fencing token (otherwise it is a stale generation); the node set of the waiting step matches
   the incident; the workflow's `execution_deadline`/`lifetime_deadline_at` have not passed; the nodes are still in
   the cluster and not isolated by another incident. If any condition fails, the command refuses and writes nothing. When several
   active attempts are observed on the same node it likewise refuses, without guessing which one to terminate.
2. **Scope of impact**: `inspected` writes only the node annotation
   `gpu-fault.io/mechanical-inspection-complete=<incident>:<fencing token>`; the annotation value is derived from the record
   rather than typed by hand, and the original workflow then completes on its own and restores the workload. A hardware remediation first writes the same annotation, then with
   the nodes, GPU UUIDs and job/attempt/workload from the record (taken from the current AttemptObservation; an idle node
   uses the separate job/attempt ID `operator-<incident>` with empty `workload_ids`) submits a trusted
   marker (`marker-operator-<incident>-<disposition>`, source `operator-change`, incident
   `inc-operator-<incident>-<disposition>`) and an operator terminal trigger, and the completion
   service compiles a new fenced workflow; the original incident is neither rewritten nor reused. When the node has active training
   the system compiles `STOP_WORKLOADS -> hardware action -> RESTART_WORKLOAD`, so a hardware remediation may only be submitted after the change ticket has approved stopping that
   attempt.
3. **Read-only check**: add `--plan` to print the annotation value, marker, terminal and subsequent steps that would be written, without writing any
   object. `reset-gpu` requires the incident record to carry GPU UUIDs, otherwise it refuses already at the `--plan` stage.
4. **Run the command**: as above. The marker and the terminal were originally received by endpoints authenticated with the execution token and the target cluster token
   respectively; the command calls the completion service directly through the exec channel of a CPU ingress Pod, and the
   deployment host holds no token and no token appears on the command line.
5. **Success criteria**: `message` in the output JSON gives the workflow and step order to be executed next; for a hardware remediation
   `submission.decision.matched_marker_ids` must contain this marker (otherwise the command exits non-zero and
   explains: the compiled plan does not carry the administrator's remediation). Only after confirming the
   `fencing_token`, target nodes/GPUs and step order of `submission.workflow` may the change ticket be marked as in progress.
6. **Rollback**: neither the annotation nor the terminal decision can be withdrawn. Running the same `--disposition` again is idempotent:
   nodes already annotated are not rewritten, and a terminal decision with the same `cluster_id + attempt_id` has the control plane return
   the original decision (output `no_op=true`). Do not fabricate a new attempt ID to bypass idempotency; if the existing
   decision does not carry this marker, the command refuses, and you should first check whether that decision/workflow already has the required remediation.
7. **Evidence**: `<state-dir>/submit-remediation/<incident-id>/<disposition>-<time>.json`
   records the operator ARN, `--reference`, the complete plan, the annotation result and the decision/plan/workflow IDs.
8. **`restore` (validated restore after a QUARANTINED node is repaired)**:
   `gpu-fault-admin submit-remediation --state-dir ... --incident-id <QUARANTINED incident>
   --disposition restore --reference CHG-xxxx [--plan]`. All preconditions are checked before writing, refusing if any fails:
   incident `state=QUARANTINED`; the incident has no PENDING/RUNNING/SAFETY_PENDING
   workflow; every node is in the cluster; the incident and its last workflow have the same fencing token (otherwise
   a stale generation); at least one node is still isolated by this incident. Isolation ownership is judged per node and written into the
   `node_decisions` of `--plan`: nodes carrying a `gpu-fault.io/quarantined` taint whose value equals `quarantine_taint_value(<incident>)`,
   or carrying this incident's `gpu-fault.io/incident-id` annotation (taint/cordon removed by hand, annotation
   still present) enter the restore; already clean nodes and nodes isolated by another incident (named -- that isolation is released by its
   own restore) are skipped; when no node is still isolated by this incident it refuses, and you close with node evidence via the
   QUARANTINED branch of Administrator Operations §9.1. Effect: through the exec channel of a CPU ingress Pod the command,
   under the product identity, constructs with `gpu_fault.orchestration.validated_restore` and writes in one transaction
   the workflow `workflow-validated-restore-<uuid>` (official_action `RESTORE_SCHEDULING`, steps
   `VALIDATE_GPU -> VALIDATE_HOST -> VALIDATE_FABRIC -> RESTORE_SCHEDULING`, the first three
   `gpu-fault-validation-adapter`, the last `gpu-fault-kubernetes-adapter`, node_ids taken from the
   incident, GPU scope taken from the incident record, fencing_token equal to the incident's) and the updated incident
   (`ACTION_PENDING`, `workflow_request_id` pointing at the new workflow, reasons appended with
   `operator restore requested by <operator> (<reference>)`), then wakes the dispatcher. `--plan`
   prints the steps, owners, nodes, GPU scope and fencing token to be created, writing no object. Idempotent: while that restore
   workflow is still PENDING/RUNNING a rerun returns `no_op=true` with the existing id. The evidence file is
   `<state-dir>/submit-remediation/<incident-id>/restore-<time>.json`. The restore of the acceptance script
   `warm_spare_fixture.py` goes through the same constructor, so the script and the product do not evolve separately.
   The arrows here denote validation phases; a complete, unambiguous inventory can expand multi-node GPU/Fabric validation into per-node steps,
   but every event GPU must be validated and all validation precedes restoring scheduling. A missing inventory does not authorise dropping UUIDs or treating an empty intersection
   as node health; a wrong scope of an old incident needs checked reconciliation and cannot be rewritten automatically from a missing inventory. When only
   some nodes of the incident are restored (`restore_node_ids` is a proper subset of `node_ids`), the steps do not carry the incident's GPU scope but do full per-node
   validation: the GPUs named by the incident may be on already restored sibling nodes.
9. **`confirm-node-action` (manual confirmation of a node action with unknown outcome)**:
   `gpu-fault-admin submit-remediation --state-dir ... --incident-id <incident>
   --disposition confirm-node-action --node <node_id> --reference CHG-xxxx [--plan]`. Applies when
   a node action step on a BLOCKED / NEEDS_OPERATOR workflow FAILED with details carrying
   `outcome_unknown: true, manual_confirmation_required: true` (the executor got no receipt within the step cap).
   The command-line side computes the decision from the Pod's inspect result (incident, BLOCKED workflow, fleet Agent record, the remote command's evidence keys,
   GPU inventory) and the node object read via the GPU kubeconfig, and `--plan` prints it; at write time the in-Pod script uses only
   store primitives that exist in the running release: it re-reads the incident/workflow, compares the fencing token, execution epoch,
   merge revision, state and node set, compares the confirmed step's status/adapter operation/details verbatim,
   re-reads the Agent record to re-check ACTIVE, the lease, a boot id consistent with kubelet and different from the pre-action boot id, then writes with
   `save_workflow(expected=)` and appends `OPERATOR_RECONCILED` / `NODE_ACTION_CONFIRMED` events.
   Evidence rules: the record is BLOCKED, has no owner, its lease has expired, has no WAITING step and no open remote command; the node is in the incident's
   node list and that record really has a pending node action on this node. `RESTART_NODE` requires the node Ready, the Agent ACTIVE
   with a heartbeat after the action started, kubelet and the Agent reporting the same boot id which differs from the boot id recorded before the action
   (`agent_baselines` / `stop_reboot_authorization_v1` / the single-node incident's `source_boot_id`;
   refused when none exists); other node actions require the Agent's terminal answer (`node_results`) on the remote command, or a later validated restore of the same incident
   having completed `VALIDATE_GPU` and `RESTORE_SCHEDULING` on that node and made the incident
   RECOVERED. Effect: the step's uncertainty flags are set to false, `operator_confirmed` records the evidence verbatim, the step stays FAILED,
   and the record stays BLOCKED; afterwards the restore of item 8 makes the incident RECOVERED and produces a verified restore successor,
   and `workflow-reconcile` closes out the record (Administrator Operations §10.2). Idempotent: rerunning for an already confirmed node gives `no_op=true`.
   The evidence file is `<state-dir>/submit-remediation/<incident-id>/confirm-node-action-<time>.json`.

<a id="104-手工删除无-ttl-的-incident-审计记录"></a>
<a id="84-手工删除无-ttl-的-incident-审计记录"></a>
### 8.4 Manually Deleting Incident Audit Records Ahead of Time

The archive-first automatic retention of the production control plane is **enabled by default**, retaining 30 days; it is disabled only when `site.yaml` explicitly declares
`spec.retention.controlRecordRetentionDays: 0`. Configuration ownership, site.yaml fields and IAM are in
`docs/管理员容量配置.md` (Administrator Capacity Configuration) §10. The values the release engine renders to the control plane by default:

```text
GPU_FAULT_CONTROL_RECORD_RETENTION_DAYS=30
GPU_FAULT_CONTROL_RECORD_ARCHIVE_S3_URI=s3://gpu-fault-control-records-<account>-<region>/<site>/control-record-archive
GPU_FAULT_CONTROL_RECORD_ARCHIVE_INTERVAL_SECONDS=600
GPU_FAULT_CONTROL_RECORD_ARCHIVE_BATCH_SIZE=200
```

`archiveS3Uri` may be omitted; `src/gpu_fault/admin/site.py::default_control_record_archive_s3_uri`
derives the default target from account, Region and site. bootstrap creates the bucket automatically and converges encryption, versioning and Block Public
Access; the CPU role obtains only `s3:PutObject` on the target prefix and is granted no read or delete permission on the archived objects
(the archiver only writes; it neither reads nor deletes objects). The running process still refuses to start with `RETENTION_DAYS>0` but no URI, but
the release engine always renders the URI. The system first archives the complete audit package and re-checks the SHA within the transaction, then deletes the live table records. The throughput cap
is 200 records / 10 minutes; `gpu_fault_control_record_archive_archived_total` should keep growing, and growth of
`gpu_fault_control_record_archive_errors_total{reason}` is handled per
`GpuFaultControlRecordArchiveErrors`. Retention rule: `incident`/`workflow` and every kind without a dedicated
cleanup job (event dedup links, group links, etc.) are archived and deleted only together with the incident -- **as long as the incident
exists, they are kept**; inactive markers, terminal notifications, completion decisions and expired registry
members are reclaimed by `processor-cleanup` according to their own `*_RETENTION_SECONDS`, and those referenced by an incident are likewise kept
(see `docs/详细设计-v2.md` §2.3.1).

Do not run single-table `DELETE`s separately: the incident is also referenced by event dedup links,
attempt/node group links, notification dedup links and predecessor workflows. Once dangling
links are left behind, replay of the same event or scheduling of a successor workflow may fail.

The repository provides the following transactional scripts:

- `scripts/postgres/incident-audit-preview.sql`: previews object counts, workflow states, open
  commands and successor references.
- `scripts/postgres/incident-audit-export.sql`: exports the deletion scope as NDJSON.
- `scripts/postgres/incident-audit-purge.sql`: transactional deletion with terminal-state guards and double incident ID confirmation.

This procedure is only for incidents that have ended and exceeded the organisation's audit retention period. After deletion, the same event ID
is no longer protected by the original `incident_by_event` dedup record, and a replay from the event source may create a new incident, so you must also
confirm that the collector cursor, completion watcher and external event sources will not replay that historical event.

First set the variables and create a PostgreSQL admin Pod that exists only during the maintenance window. The Pod reads the DSN from the existing Secret;
the password does not appear on the command line or in the Pod YAML:

```bash
export CONTROL_KUBECONFIG=/tmp/gpu-fault-control-plane.kubeconfig
export NS=gpu-fault-system
export INCIDENT_ID=inc-replace-with-terminal-incident
export STAMP="$(date -u +%Y%m%dT%H%M%SZ)"
export SQL_CONFIGMAP="gpu-fault-incident-purge-${STAMP}"
export ADMIN_POD="gpu-fault-db-admin-${STAMP,,}"
export BACKUP_DIR="${HOME}/gpu-fault-audit-purge/${STAMP}"

install -d -m 0700 "${BACKUP_DIR}"
umask 077

kubectl --kubeconfig "${CONTROL_KUBECONFIG}" -n "${NS}" \
  create configmap "${SQL_CONFIGMAP}" \
  --from-file=scripts/postgres/incident-audit-preview.sql \
  --from-file=scripts/postgres/incident-audit-export.sql \
  --from-file=scripts/postgres/incident-audit-purge.sql

cat <<YAML | kubectl --kubeconfig "${CONTROL_KUBECONFIG}" \
  -n "${NS}" apply -f -
apiVersion: v1
kind: Pod
metadata:
  name: ${ADMIN_POD}
  labels:
    app: gpu-fault-db-admin
spec:
  restartPolicy: Never
  containers:
    - name: psql
      image: postgres:16
      command: ["/bin/sh", "-c", "sleep 3600"]
      env:
        - name: DATABASE_URL
          valueFrom:
            secretKeyRef:
              name: gpu-fault-aurora
              key: postgres-url
      securityContext:
        allowPrivilegeEscalation: false
        capabilities:
          drop: ["ALL"]
      volumeMounts:
        - name: sql
          mountPath: /sql
          readOnly: true
  volumes:
    - name: sql
      configMap:
        name: ${SQL_CONFIGMAP}
YAML

kubectl --kubeconfig "${CONTROL_KUBECONFIG}" -n "${NS}" \
  wait --for=condition=Ready "pod/${ADMIN_POD}" --timeout=5m
```

Preview first. In the following output the open remote commands and external successor workflows must be empty,
and all workflows must be `SUCCEEDED`, `FAILED` or `BLOCKED`:

```bash
kubectl --kubeconfig "${CONTROL_KUBECONFIG}" -n "${NS}" \
  exec "${ADMIN_POD}" -- sh -ec \
  'psql "$DATABASE_URL" -X -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 \
    -v incident_id="$1" \
    -f /sql/incident-audit-preview.sql' \
  sh "${INCIDENT_ID}"
```

Export the complete deletion scope to the administrator's machine. The file contains email bodies and fault metadata and must be protected
as a sensitive operations record:

```bash
BACKUP_FILE="${BACKUP_DIR}/${INCIDENT_ID}.ndjson"

kubectl --kubeconfig "${CONTROL_KUBECONFIG}" -n "${NS}" \
  exec "${ADMIN_POD}" -- sh -ec \
  'psql "$DATABASE_URL" -X -qAt -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 \
    -v incident_id="$1" \
    -f /sql/incident-audit-export.sql' \
  sh "${INCIDENT_ID}" > "${BACKUP_FILE}"

test -s "${BACKUP_FILE}"
sha256sum "${BACKUP_FILE}" |
  tee "${BACKUP_FILE}.sha256"
wc -l "${BACKUP_FILE}"
```

All Aurora writers must be stopped during deletion, to avoid another replica merging the same incident just as the event dedup link is deleted.
The writers in regional mode are ingress, control-worker and a non-zero-replica
spool-worker; the writer in transitional single-cluster mode is `gpu-fault-control-plane`. Record each one's original replica
count and set a recovery trap that runs on any failure:

```bash
: "${PURGE_WRITER_STATE:=${BACKUP_DIR}/writer-replicas.tsv}"
: > "${PURGE_WRITER_STATE}"

if kubectl --kubeconfig "${CONTROL_KUBECONFIG}" -n "${NS}" \
  get deployment gpu-fault-api-ha >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  PURGE_DEPLOYMENT_MODE=regional
  WRITER_DEPLOYMENTS=(
    gpu-fault-api-ha
    gpu-fault-control-worker
    gpu-fault-telemetry-spool-worker
  )
elif kubectl --kubeconfig "${CONTROL_KUBECONFIG}" -n "${NS}" \
  get deployment gpu-fault-control-plane >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  PURGE_DEPLOYMENT_MODE=single-cluster
  WRITER_DEPLOYMENTS=(gpu-fault-control-plane)
else
  echo "no supported control-plane writer Deployment found" >&2
  exit 1
fi

for deployment in "${WRITER_DEPLOYMENTS[@]}"; do
  if kubectl --kubeconfig "${CONTROL_KUBECONFIG}" -n "${NS}" \
    get deployment "${deployment}" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    replicas="$(
      kubectl --kubeconfig "${CONTROL_KUBECONFIG}" -n "${NS}" \
        get deployment "${deployment}" \
        -o jsonpath='{.spec.replicas}'
    )"
    printf '%s\t%s\n' "${deployment}" "${replicas:-0}" \
      >> "${PURGE_WRITER_STATE}"
  fi
done
chmod 0600 "${PURGE_WRITER_STATE}"
test -s "${PURGE_WRITER_STATE}"
cat "${PURGE_WRITER_STATE}"

restore_incident_purge_writers() {
  local deployment
  local replicas
  local -a restore_order
  if [[ "${PURGE_DEPLOYMENT_MODE}" == regional ]]; then
    restore_order=(
      gpu-fault-telemetry-spool-worker
      gpu-fault-control-worker
      gpu-fault-api-ha
    )
  else
    restore_order=(gpu-fault-control-plane)
  fi

  for deployment in "${restore_order[@]}"; do
    replicas="$(
      awk -v name="${deployment}" '$1==name{print $2}' \
        "${PURGE_WRITER_STATE}"
    )"
    test -n "${replicas}" || continue
    kubectl --kubeconfig "${CONTROL_KUBECONFIG}" -n "${NS}" \
      scale "deployment/${deployment}" --replicas="${replicas}"
    if [[ "${replicas}" -gt 0 ]]; then
      kubectl --kubeconfig "${CONTROL_KUBECONFIG}" -n "${NS}" \
        rollout status "deployment/${deployment}" --timeout=10m
    fi
  done
}
trap restore_incident_purge_writers EXIT

# The array order guarantees that regional mode stops ingress first, refusing new requests, then the two consumers.
while IFS=$'\t' read -r deployment replicas; do
  kubectl --kubeconfig "${CONTROL_KUBECONFIG}" -n "${NS}" \
    scale "deployment/${deployment}" --replicas=0
  if [[ "${replicas}" -gt 0 ]]; then
    kubectl --kubeconfig "${CONTROL_KUBECONFIG}" -n "${NS}" \
      wait --for=delete pod -l "app=${deployment}" --timeout=10m
  fi
done < "${PURGE_WRITER_STATE}"

while IFS=$'\t' read -r deployment _; do
  test "$(
    kubectl --kubeconfig "${CONTROL_KUBECONFIG}" -n "${NS}" \
      get pod -l "app=${deployment}" --no-headers 2>/dev/null |
    wc -l
  )" -eq 0
done < "${PURGE_WRITER_STATE}"
```

Preview again, then run the transactional deletion with double ID confirmation:

```bash
kubectl --kubeconfig "${CONTROL_KUBECONFIG}" -n "${NS}" \
  exec "${ADMIN_POD}" -- sh -ec \
  'psql "$DATABASE_URL" -X -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 \
    -v incident_id="$1" \
    -f /sql/incident-audit-preview.sql' \
  sh "${INCIDENT_ID}"

kubectl --kubeconfig "${CONTROL_KUBECONFIG}" -n "${NS}" \
  exec "${ADMIN_POD}" -- sh -ec \
  'psql "$DATABASE_URL" -X -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 \
    -v incident_id="$1" \
    -v confirm_incident_id="$1" \
    -f /sql/incident-audit-purge.sql' \
  sh "${INCIDENT_ID}"
```

The script rolls back and exits when any of the following holds:

- The incident does not exist;
- There are `PENDING`, `RUNNING` or `SAFETY_PENDING` workflows;
- There are `PENDING`, `WAITING` or `LEASED` remote commands;
- A workflow of another incident still uses this incident's workflow as predecessor.

Restore by the original replica counts. Regional mode restores the consumers first and ingress last; the function internally waits for every
non-zero-replica Deployment to complete its rollout:

```bash
restore_incident_purge_writers
trap - EXIT

kubectl --kubeconfig "${CONTROL_KUBECONFIG}" -n "${NS}" \
  exec "${ADMIN_POD}" -- sh -ec \
  'psql "$DATABASE_URL" -X -At -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 \
    -v incident_id="$1" \
    -c "SELECT count(*) FROM gpu_fault_objects
        WHERE kind='\''incident'\'' AND key=:'\''incident_id'\'';"' \
  sh "${INCIDENT_ID}"
```

The last command must print `0`. After confirming the control plane has recovered, delete the temporary Pod and ConfigMap:

```bash
kubectl --kubeconfig "${CONTROL_KUBECONFIG}" -n "${NS}" \
  delete pod "${ADMIN_POD}"
kubectl --kubeconfig "${CONTROL_KUBECONFIG}" -n "${NS}" \
  delete configmap "${SQL_CONFIGMAP}"
```

The transaction does not delete the following data:

- Raw evidence still cleaned up by the 24-hour TTL;
- Job-level restart budgets and GPU health baselines;
- The Node Agent's node-local SQLite ledger;
- Email copies already delivered to administrator mailboxes and in SES/CloudWatch logs;
- Historical pages in Aurora automated backups and snapshots. After deletion from the live database, the records in backups remain until
  the Aurora backup retention expires.

To satisfy regulatory deletion requirements, besides the live database operation above, the exported
NDJSON, mailboxes/mail archives, logging systems and manual Aurora snapshots must also be handled according to the organisation's process.

<a id="11-数据迁移"></a>
## 9. Data Migration

The migration command requires the target database to be empty and no writer during the migration window. First determine the deployment mode and record
the original replica counts; the state file must be kept in a controlled directory for the recovery session after a disconnect:

```bash
: "${CONTROL_KUBECONFIG:?the control-plane kubeconfig must be given explicitly}"
: "${NS:=gpu-fault-system}"
: "${MIGRATION_STATE:=/secure/gpu-fault/migration-replicas.tsv}"

install -d -m 0700 "$(dirname "${MIGRATION_STATE}")"
: > "${MIGRATION_STATE}"

if kubectl --kubeconfig "${CONTROL_KUBECONFIG}" -n "${NS}" \
  get deployment gpu-fault-api-ha >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  DEPLOYMENT_MODE=regional
  WRITER_DEPLOYMENTS=(
    gpu-fault-api-ha
    gpu-fault-control-worker
    gpu-fault-telemetry-spool-worker
  )
else
  DEPLOYMENT_MODE=generic
  WRITER_DEPLOYMENTS=(gpu-fault-control-plane)
fi

for deployment in "${WRITER_DEPLOYMENTS[@]}"; do
  if kubectl --kubeconfig "${CONTROL_KUBECONFIG}" -n "${NS}" \
    get deployment "${deployment}" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    replicas="$(
      kubectl --kubeconfig "${CONTROL_KUBECONFIG}" -n "${NS}" \
        get deployment "${deployment}" \
        -o jsonpath='{.spec.replicas}'
    )"
    printf '%s\t%s\n' "${deployment}" "${replicas:-0}" \
      >> "${MIGRATION_STATE}"
  fi
done
chmod 0600 "${MIGRATION_STATE}"
cat "${MIGRATION_STATE}"
```

In regional mode stop ingress first to block new requests; keep the consumers processing the already accepted queue. Then wait from
the control-worker's `/metrics` until the processor queue and in-flight are both zero:

```bash
if [[ "${DEPLOYMENT_MODE}" == regional ]]; then
  kubectl --kubeconfig "${CONTROL_KUBECONFIG}" -n "${NS}" \
    scale deployment/gpu-fault-api-ha --replicas=0
  kubectl --kubeconfig "${CONTROL_KUBECONFIG}" -n "${NS}" \
    wait --for=delete pod -l app=gpu-fault-api-ha --timeout=10m

  for _ in {1..120}; do
    WORKER_POD="$(
      kubectl --kubeconfig "${CONTROL_KUBECONFIG}" -n "${NS}" \
        get pod -l app=gpu-fault-control-worker \
        --field-selector=status.phase=Running \
        -o jsonpath='{.items[0].metadata.name}'
    )"
    test -n "${WORKER_POD}"
    METRICS="$(
      kubectl --kubeconfig "${CONTROL_KUBECONFIG}" -n "${NS}" \
        exec "${WORKER_POD}" -- python -c \
        'import urllib.request; print(urllib.request.urlopen("http://127.0.0.1:8081/metrics", timeout=10).read().decode())'
    )"
    QUEUE_DEPTH="$(
      awk '$1=="gpu_fault_processor_queue_depth"{print $2}' \
        <<<"${METRICS}"
    )"
    IN_FLIGHT="$(
      awk '$1=="gpu_fault_processor_in_flight"{print $2}' \
        <<<"${METRICS}"
    )"
    if [[ "${QUEUE_DEPTH:-1}" == 0 &&
      "${IN_FLIGHT:-1}" == 0 ]]; then
      break
    fi
    sleep 5
  done
  test "${QUEUE_DEPTH:-1}" = 0
  test "${IN_FLIGHT:-1}" = 0

  SPOOL_REPLICAS="$(
    awk '$1=="gpu-fault-telemetry-spool-worker"{print $2}' \
      "${MIGRATION_STATE}"
  )"
  if [[ "${SPOOL_REPLICAS:-0}" -gt 0 ]]; then
    for _ in {1..120}; do
      SPOOL_POD="$(
        kubectl --kubeconfig "${CONTROL_KUBECONFIG}" -n "${NS}" \
          get pod -l app=gpu-fault-telemetry-spool-worker \
          --field-selector=status.phase=Running \
          -o jsonpath='{.items[0].metadata.name}'
      )"
      SPOOL_METRICS="$(
        kubectl --kubeconfig "${CONTROL_KUBECONFIG}" -n "${NS}" \
          exec "${SPOOL_POD}" -- python -c \
          'import urllib.request; print(urllib.request.urlopen("http://127.0.0.1:8082/metrics", timeout=10).read().decode())'
      )"
      SPOOL_DEPTH="$(
        awk '$1=="gpu_fault_telemetry_spool_depth"{print $2}' \
          <<<"${SPOOL_METRICS}"
      )"
      SPOOL_LEASED="$(
        awk '$1=="gpu_fault_telemetry_spool_leased"{print $2}' \
          <<<"${SPOOL_METRICS}"
      )"
      if [[ "${SPOOL_DEPTH:-1}" == 0 &&
        "${SPOOL_LEASED:-1}" == 0 ]]; then
        break
      fi
      sleep 5
    done
    test "${SPOOL_DEPTH:-1}" = 0
    test "${SPOOL_LEASED:-1}" = 0
  fi

  for deployment in \
    gpu-fault-control-worker \
    gpu-fault-telemetry-spool-worker; do
    replicas="$(
      awk -v name="${deployment}" '$1==name{print $2}' \
        "${MIGRATION_STATE}"
    )"
    if [[ "${replicas:-0}" -gt 0 ]]; then
      kubectl --kubeconfig "${CONTROL_KUBECONFIG}" -n "${NS}" \
        scale "deployment/${deployment}" --replicas=0
      kubectl --kubeconfig "${CONTROL_KUBECONFIG}" -n "${NS}" \
        wait --for=delete pod -l "app=${deployment}" --timeout=10m
    fi
  done
else
  # The generic single-tier deployment both receives requests and consumes the queue. First stop traffic at the external entry point and confirm
  # gpu_fault_processor_queue_depth / in_flight are both 0 before scaling down.
  kubectl --kubeconfig "${CONTROL_KUBECONFIG}" -n "${NS}" \
    scale deployment/gpu-fault-control-plane --replicas=0
  kubectl --kubeconfig "${CONTROL_KUBECONFIG}" -n "${NS}" \
    wait --for=delete pod -l app=gpu-fault-control-plane \
    --timeout=10m
fi
```

Confirm that no writer Pods exist:

```bash
while IFS=$'\t' read -r deployment _; do
  test "$(
    kubectl --kubeconfig "${CONTROL_KUBECONFIG}" -n "${NS}" \
      get pod -l "app=${deployment}" --no-headers 2>/dev/null |
    wc -l
  )" -eq 0
done < "${MIGRATION_STATE}"
```

The status query, backfill, cleanup and logical data copy entry points only validate the existing schema and no longer implicitly initialise tables or indexes by constructing a
`PostgresStore`. Source and target databases must already have gone through the checked schema release process;
on a structural mismatch handle the schema first; `status` or the data migration command must not be used to repair it implicitly.
An explicit `--ensure-schema` is still executed by the standalone schema ensure Job.
When target connection initialisation fails or the close is abnormal, the copier still closes the source connection; the legacy SQLite compatibility import uses an encoded
read-only URI and does not treat characters such as `?` in the file name as connection options. The production control plane still uses PostgreSQL.

The logical copy here is not a complete PostgreSQL backup: when data exists in the dedicated telemetry tables of source or target, the command refuses before writing
and requires the reviewed native PostgreSQL backup/restore process instead. The check must not be bypassed by deleting data or changing the production
storage mode. Hot state in old SQLite or legacy objects is backfilled into the dedicated tables within the same target transaction,
so that the dedicated reader can read the just-imported data after success returns.

Then run one kind of stop-write migration:

```bash
# SQLite -> PostgreSQL
GPU_FAULT_STORE_URL='postgresql://target/...' \
  gpu-fault-store-migrate \
  --sqlite-path /var/lib/gpu-fault/control-plane.db

# or PostgreSQL -> PostgreSQL
GPU_FAULT_STORE_URL='postgresql://target/...' \
  gpu-fault-store-migrate \
  --source-postgres-url 'postgresql://source/...'
```

After the migration completes, first start the minimal closed-loop verification. Regional mode must have one consumer and one ingress at the same time;
do not start only ingress:

```bash
if [[ "${DEPLOYMENT_MODE}" == regional ]]; then
  kubectl --kubeconfig "${CONTROL_KUBECONFIG}" -n "${NS}" \
    scale deployment/gpu-fault-control-worker --replicas=1
  kubectl --kubeconfig "${CONTROL_KUBECONFIG}" -n "${NS}" \
    rollout status deployment/gpu-fault-control-worker --timeout=10m
  kubectl --kubeconfig "${CONTROL_KUBECONFIG}" -n "${NS}" \
    scale deployment/gpu-fault-api-ha --replicas=1
  kubectl --kubeconfig "${CONTROL_KUBECONFIG}" -n "${NS}" \
    rollout status deployment/gpu-fault-api-ha --timeout=10m
else
  kubectl --kubeconfig "${CONTROL_KUBECONFIG}" -n "${NS}" \
    scale deployment/gpu-fault-control-plane --replicas=1
  kubectl --kubeconfig "${CONTROL_KUBECONFIG}" -n "${NS}" \
    rollout status deployment/gpu-fault-control-plane --timeout=10m
fi
```

After health, schema version and one non-destructive read/write smoke all pass, restore the original replicas from the state file.
Regional mode restores the consumers first and ingress last:

```bash
restore_replicas() {
  local deployment
  local replicas
  if [[ "${DEPLOYMENT_MODE}" == regional ]]; then
    for deployment in \
      gpu-fault-telemetry-spool-worker \
      gpu-fault-control-worker; do
      replicas="$(
        awk -v name="${deployment}" '$1==name{print $2}' \
          "${MIGRATION_STATE}"
      )"
      test -n "${replicas}" || continue
      kubectl --kubeconfig "${CONTROL_KUBECONFIG}" -n "${NS}" \
        scale "deployment/${deployment}" --replicas="${replicas}"
      if [[ "${replicas}" -gt 0 ]]; then
        kubectl --kubeconfig "${CONTROL_KUBECONFIG}" -n "${NS}" \
          rollout status "deployment/${deployment}" --timeout=10m
      fi
    done
    deployment=gpu-fault-api-ha
    replicas="$(
      awk -v name="${deployment}" '$1==name{print $2}' \
        "${MIGRATION_STATE}"
    )"
    kubectl --kubeconfig "${CONTROL_KUBECONFIG}" -n "${NS}" \
      scale "deployment/${deployment}" --replicas="${replicas}"
    kubectl --kubeconfig "${CONTROL_KUBECONFIG}" -n "${NS}" \
      rollout status "deployment/${deployment}" --timeout=10m
  else
    read -r deployment replicas < "${MIGRATION_STATE}"
    kubectl --kubeconfig "${CONTROL_KUBECONFIG}" -n "${NS}" \
      scale "deployment/${deployment}" --replicas="${replicas}"
    kubectl --kubeconfig "${CONTROL_KUBECONFIG}" -n "${NS}" \
      rollout status "deployment/${deployment}" --timeout=10m
  fi
}

restore_replicas
```

Do not delete `${MIGRATION_STATE}` when the migration or verification fails; after repairing the database, set
`CONTROL_KUBECONFIG`, `NS`, `MIGRATION_STATE` and `DEPLOYMENT_MODE` again in a new session and call the same
`restore_replicas` logic. Delete the state file only after confirming all original replicas are Ready.

<a id="12-分阶段启用破坏性能力"></a>
## 10. Enabling Destructive Capabilities in Phases

### Phase A: Observation

- The executor stays in simulation, or active with only forensics enabled.
- Deploy collectors, the Watcher and the profile.
- Verify XIDs, metrics, allocation, Agent heartbeats and evidence.

### Phase B: Isolation and Training Control

- Enable `MARK_UNSCHEDULABLE/QUARANTINE/STOP_WORKLOADS/RESTART_WORKLOAD`.
- Use a GPU-free E2E to verify that old and new attempts do not overlap.
- Verify that provider taints are not removed.

### Phase C: GPU Reset

1. The regional production Installer already sets by default
   `GPU_FAULT_NODE_ALLOW_GPU_RESET=true`,
   `GPU_FAULT_NODE_ALLOW_FABRIC_RESET=true`,
   `GPU_FAULT_NODE_ALLOW_SERVICE_QUIESCE=true` and
   `GPU_FAULT_NODE_ALLOW_FABRIC_MANAGER_RESTART=true`.
2. Check Fleet readiness, artifact SHA and config digest.
3. Test on a single drained node without workloads inside a maintenance window.
4. Check that the node allowlist contains
   `QUIESCE_GPU_SERVICES/RESET_GPU/RESTORE_GPU_SERVICES`.
5. Confirm that `gpu-fault-quiesce-*.timer` can restore services independently when both the control plane and kubelet are unavailable.
   The control plane sets `GPU_FAULT_AGENT_MAINTENANCE_WINDOW_SECONDS=420` and the node sets
   `GPU_FAULT_QUIESCE_FAILSAFE_SECONDS=420`; the former must not exceed the latter.
6. Check that the control-plane allowlist also contains the three operations above; do not enable only the node-side switch.
7. Verify the complete order:
   `STOP_WORKLOADS -> QUIESCE_GPU_SERVICES -> VERIFY_NO_GPU_CLIENTS ->
   RESET_GPU -> RESTORE_GPU_SERVICES -> VALIDATE_GPU ->
   RESTORE_SCHEDULING -> RESTART_WORKLOAD`.
8. Make the reset and the explicit restore fail separately, confirming the fail-safe timer is kept and retries; after a successful recovery
   the timer is cancelled and the state file deleted.
9. Verify the three gates -- GPU, host and fabric -- after the reset.

#### Phase C.0: XID 74 NVLink Workflow

1. Confirm the Runtime Profile has `diagnosticBundleCapture`,
   `nvlinkDiagnostics`, `gpuReset` and `supportEscalation` enabled. The owner of
   `nvlinkDiagnostics` and `gpuReset` must be `gpu-fault-node-agent`.
2. The control-plane allowlist must contain `COLLECT_DIAGNOSTIC_BUNDLE`,
   `RUN_NVLINK74_WORKFLOW`, `RESET_GPU`, `QUIESCE_GPU_SERVICES`,
   `VERIFY_NO_GPU_CLIENTS`, `RESTORE_GPU_SERVICES` and `ESCALATE_SUPPORT`.
   Pre-install on every GPU node the NVIDIA-provided Field Diagnostic compatible with the current GPU/driver,
   then compute the executable's hash:

```bash
sudo sha256sum /opt/nvidia/fielddiag/fielddiag
```

   Configure the following parameters before deployment. The command does not go through a shell; the arguments must match the NVIDIA
   Field Diagnostic CLI the customer actually installed, and this solution assumes no fixed argument format of the vendor tool:

```bash
# Managed regional sites have the reviewed Installer Job template (deploy/node/run-hyperpod-installer-job.sh) generate the parameters from
# GPU_FAULT_ENABLE_NODE_FIELD_DIAGNOSTIC, GPU_FAULT_FIELD_DIAGNOSTIC_COMMAND/_SHA256,
# GPU_FAULT_MEMORY_FIELD_DIAGNOSTIC_COMMAND/_SHA256, GPU_FAULT_FIELD_DIAGNOSTIC_TIMEOUT_SECONDS;
# nothing is run on the node bypassing the release. Row-remap failure uses a separate whole-card memory diagnostic command
# and does not require or fabricate a Link ID. The manual compatibility path has the following parameter shape; the other required release/node parameters are omitted:
install-gpu-fault-collector.sh <other checked parameters> \
  --allow-field-diagnostic \
  --field-diagnostic-command '/opt/nvidia/fielddiag/fielddiag --gpu {gpu_uuid} --link {link_id}' \
  --field-diagnostic-sha256 '<64-character SHA-256 from the previous step>' \
  --memory-field-diagnostic-command '/opt/nvidia/fielddiag/fielddiag --gpu {gpu_uuid} --pci {pci_bdf}' \
  --memory-field-diagnostic-sha256 '<64-character SHA-256 of the memory diagnostic executable>' \
  --field-diagnostic-timeout 1800
```

   The regional Job template `deploy/node/run-hyperpod-installer-job.sh` generates these parameters from
   `GPU_FAULT_ENABLE_NODE_FIELD_DIAGNOSTIC` and the command/SHA/timeout inputs.
   `GPU_FAULT_ENABLE_FIELD_DIAGNOSTIC` is only a variable of the old single-cluster `deploy/hyperpod/deploy.sh` (Appendix A);
   neither the control-plane nor the Node Agent process recognises it. The installer adds `RUN_NVLINK74_WORKFLOW` and the configured
   `RUN_FIELD_DIAGNOSTIC` to the Node Agent allowlist, registers the
   `nvlinkDiagnostics` and `memoryDiagnostics` capabilities respectively, and includes both commands and their digests in
   the Agent heartbeat gate. Installation fails when a command is missing, the path is not absolute, the SHA-256 does not match, or the timeout
   is outside 60-7200 seconds. When no memory command is configured, the row-remap failure workflow
   stays BLOCKED for lack of a `memoryDiagnostics` owner and never replaces the node directly.
3. Kernel log lines that need count-based decisions must contain both an explicit Link ID and the seven
   hexadecimal fields after XID 74. For example, the following is only a user-space software chain replay and does not create a real NVLink fault:

```bash
printf '%s\n' \
  '<3>NVRM: Xid (PCI:0000:b9:00): 74, pid=1234, name=python, Link 3, 0x100 0x0 0x0 0x0 0x0 0x0 0x0' \
  | sudo tee /dev/kmsg >/dev/null
```

4. `0x100` means bit 8 of the first register. The XID Catalog requires a GPU reset or node reboot
   to clear this error, so the first event directly executes
   `MARK_UNSCHEDULABLE -> STOP_WORKLOADS (if training is active) ->
   QUIESCE_GPU_SERVICES -> VERIFY_NO_GPU_CLIENTS -> RESET_GPU ->
   RESTORE_GPU_SERVICES -> VALIDATE_GPU/FABRIC -> RESTORE_SCHEDULING`.
   `CHECK_MECHANICALS` must not block the first reset. When the reset fails, the failure escalation chain enters
   `REBOOT_NODE`; only when neither reset nor reboot can clear it, or it keeps recurring, does it enter manual hardware inspection/support.
   Repeats on the same Link/bit may add a diagnostic bundle and Field Diagnostic; the diagnostic parameters must contain the Link ID,
   bit count and seven registers, but the diagnostic cannot replace the reset.
5. Replay an unknown bit, all zeros, secondary-only and bit 18 of the fourth register separately, confirming the node stays
   fail closed, generates the `xid74-support-zh-v3` fixed-template notification and executes no single-GPU reset.
6. Replay safe-only or corrected-threshold-only bits, confirming the disposition is
   `MONITOR_ONLY` and the current training attempt is not stopped.
7. Replay ECC/parity three times consecutively on the same Link and the same bit, confirming that at count 1 and 2 it only monitors,
   and that once the count reaches the threshold it executes reset/diagnostics rather than only sending support; after switching Link or bit the count
   must start again from 1. Resubmitting the same event ID must not increase the count.
8. `/dev/kmsg` replays verify only the parser, policy, workflow and Agent; they must not be described as the NVLink
   hardware really producing a fault. Real destructive acceptance still needs a maintenance window, checkpoints and a rollback plan.

#### Phase C.1: H200 SXID Recovery

1. The full-reset in this section is only for ordinary Fatal Trunk and the `10003/19084` explicitly named by the Catalog;
   B200/B300 do not apply. Table 23 Always-Fatal executes a node reboot on HyperPod bare metal,
   not this section's full-reset.
2. The Node Agent sets simultaneously
   `GPU_FAULT_NODE_ALLOW_GPU_RESET=true`,
   `GPU_FAULT_NODE_ALLOW_SERVICE_QUIESCE=true` and
   `GPU_FAULT_NODE_ALLOW_FABRIC_RESET=true`.
3. The Node Agent allowlist and the control-plane allowlist add
   `COLLECT_DIAGNOSTIC_BUNDLE`, `RESET_ALL_GPUS_NVSWITCHES`, and the Runtime Profile configures the owner of
   `diagnosticBundleCapture` and `fabricReset` as
   `gpu-fault-node-agent`.
4. The execution order must be:
   `COLLECT_DIAGNOSTIC_BUNDLE -> STOP_WORKLOADS -> QUIESCE_GPU_SERVICES ->
   VERIFY_NO_GPU_CLIENTS ->
   RESET_ALL_GPUS_NVSWITCHES -> RESTORE_GPU_SERVICES -> VALIDATE_GPU ->
   VALIDATE_FABRIC -> RESTORE_SCHEDULING -> RESTART_WORKLOAD`.
   After a successful quiesce, each node's Agent generation is pinned into the persisted workflow evidence.
   When stopping kubelet makes the heartbeat/lease expire, only that workflow may continue within the maintenance window;
   a generation change, an Agent being drained/revoked, or exceeding the window aborts the whole multi-node barrier.
   The reset email may fail to send the first time while network services are quiesced; after `RESTORE_GPU_SERVICES`
   succeeds the same notification ID is retried automatically. Acceptance must confirm the final state is `SENT`; checking only that
   the notification body was generated is not enough.
5. The reset request must carry the node's complete GPU UUID inventory; the Agent re-runs
   `nvidia-smi --query-gpu=uuid --format=csv,noheader,nounits` and requires the sets to be exactly identical.
6. Stop the workflow on any GPU client, inventory mismatch, Fabric Manager quiesce failure or feature switch
   not enabled; do not restore scheduling or training directly.
7. A user-space write of a Fabric Manager-format SXID to `/dev/kmsg` can only verify the software chain from Collector to recovery workflow,
   and does not mean the NVSwitch hardware really produced a fault. Destructive acceptance must be run in a maintenance window,
   with a checkpoint available and the ability to resubmit training.
8. The production SXID main path is `gpu-fault-kernel-collector.service`: it reads the official SXID summary lines produced by
   the NVIDIA NVSwitch driver in `/dev/kmsg` and delivers them to
   `/v1/collector-events/nvidia-kernel`. `Severity` and `Data` continuation lines do not trigger handling on their own.
9. `gpu-fault-fabric-manager-collector.service` is only a supplementary source. It first reads
   `LOG_FILE_NAME` and `LOG_USE_SYSLOG` from `/usr/share/nvidia/nvswitch/fabricmanager.cfg`.
   The NVIDIA defaults are `/var/log/fabricmanager.log` and `0` respectively;
   the installer configures that path automatically when `--fabric-manager-log-paths` is not passed, `LOG_USE_SYSLOG=0` and the file already exists.
   An explicit command-line argument takes precedence. For a newly discovered file, and for missing or corrupt
   state, the Collector automatically records the current device, inode and EOF and collects only appends after that, avoiding replaying historical SXIDs as new faults after a
   first Node Runtime installation or a lost checkpoint. With existing valid state it continues from the unconfirmed
   offset. An event is formed only when the actual log sample conforms to the official SXID convention.

#### Phase C.2: Explicitly Enabling Driver/Firmware Remediation

Do not configure SXID remediation lists by default. Only after the site has confirmed from NVIDIA/AWS support conclusions the correspondence between specific SXIDs and target
versions, and has validated the commands in a maintenance environment, perform the following steps:

```bash
export GPU_FAULT_SXID_DRIVER_REMEDIATION_CODES=11001
export GPU_FAULT_TARGET_DRIVER_BRANCH=575
export GPU_FAULT_DRIVER_REMEDIATION_COMMAND='/opt/site/bin/remediate-driver --branch {target}'
export GPU_FAULT_DRIVER_REMEDIATION_SHA256="$(sha256sum /opt/site/bin/remediate-driver | cut -d' ' -f1)"

export GPU_FAULT_SXID_FIRMWARE_UPDATE_CODES=11001
export GPU_FAULT_TARGET_FIRMWARE_VERSION=92.10.14
export GPU_FAULT_FIRMWARE_UPDATE_COMMAND='/opt/site/bin/update-firmware --version {target}'
export GPU_FAULT_FIRMWARE_UPDATE_SHA256="$(sha256sum /opt/site/bin/update-firmware | cut -d' ' -f1)"
export GPU_FAULT_FIRMWARE_VERIFY_COMMAND='/opt/site/bin/current-firmware-version'
export GPU_FAULT_FIRMWARE_VERIFY_SHA256="$(sha256sum /opt/site/bin/current-firmware-version | cut -d' ' -f1)"
export GPU_FAULT_DIAGNOSTIC_S3_URI='s3://REPLACE_BUCKET/gpu-fault'
```

The control-plane side recognises only `GPU_FAULT_SXID_DRIVER_REMEDIATION_CODES` / `GPU_FAULT_TARGET_DRIVER_BRANCH` and
`GPU_FAULT_SXID_FIRMWARE_UPDATE_CODES` / `GPU_FAULT_TARGET_FIRMWARE_VERSION`: regional sites write them into
the source Manifests, render them into the generated ConfigMaps via `make deployment-contracts-update` and release them with the checked release.
The node-side switches are installer parameters: the Installer Job template (`deploy/node/run-hyperpod-installer-job.sh`) reads
`GPU_FAULT_ENABLE_NODE_DRIVER_REMEDIATION` / `GPU_FAULT_ENABLE_NODE_FIRMWARE_UPDATE` and the
command/SHA inputs above, generating `--allow-driver-remediation`, `--driver-*`, `--allow-firmware-update`,
`--firmware-*`; when installing a node by hand pass these parameters directly to `install-gpu-fault-collector.sh`.
These node parameters do not replace the Profile, allowlist, signing and pre-action gates. The old single-cluster deployment entry point
(`deploy/hyperpod/deploy.sh deploy` distributing both sides at once) is kept only in Appendix A. After deployment check `/v1/fleet/agents`: the allowlist,
artifact SHA and config digest of every target node must agree. The order after the policy matches is:

`COLLECT_DIAGNOSTIC_BUNDLE -> STOP_WORKLOADS -> QUIESCE_GPU_SERVICES ->
VERIFY_NO_GPU_CLIENTS -> [REMEDIATE_DRIVER] -> [UPDATE_SOFTWARE_FIRMWARE] ->
RESET -> RESTORE_GPU_SERVICES -> VALIDATE_GPU -> VALIDATE_FABRIC ->
RESTORE_SCHEDULING -> RESTART_WORKLOAD`.

Any target version mismatch, SHA mismatch, GPU client not exited, upload failure or version verification failure must
fail closed. A failed driver/firmware remediation keeps the isolation and directly creates a support workflow, no longer automatically
resetting/rebooting, to avoid continuing destructive actions on a partially updated state. Do not perform real upgrade acceptance with synthetic records via
`/dev/kmsg`; upgrade acceptance should use isolated maintenance nodes and approved site test events.

When only the Catalog `UPDATE_SWFW` (currently XID 78) needs to be executed without configuring an SXID mapping, keep
`GPU_FAULT_SXID_FIRMWARE_UPDATE_CODES` empty, but the control plane must configure
`GPU_FAULT_TARGET_FIRMWARE_VERSION` (workflow compilation requires `UPDATE_SWFW requires
GPU_FAULT_TARGET_FIRMWARE_VERSION`); the node needs `--allow-firmware-update` and the same set of target,
update/verify commands and SHA-256. The installer refuses to install when any input is missing.
`GPU_FAULT_ENABLE_FIRMWARE_UPDATE` belongs only to the old single-cluster `deploy/hyperpod/deploy.sh`; neither the control-plane nor the node process
recognises it, and it is not a capability switch of the running processes.

Non-destructive Kernel Collector software chain injection example:

```bash
printf '%s\n' \
  '<4>nvidia-nvswitch3: SXid (PCI:0000:c1:00.0): 28006, Non-fatal, Link 46 MC TS crumbstore MCTO (First)' \
  | sudo tee /dev/kmsg >/dev/null
```

The command writes a user-space synthetic record to the real `/dev/kmsg`; it does not modify NVSwitch hardware state, and the
result must not be described as the NVIDIA driver or NVSwitch really producing an SXID. Destructive full-reset software chain
replay uses SXID `10003`, for which the Catalog specifies full-reset, and may only be run in a maintenance window under all the gates of this section.
`12020` is Always-Fatal and should enter the node reboot workflow; it cannot be used to verify
`RESET_ALL_GPUS_NVSWITCHES`.

### Phase D: Node Lifecycle (Provider Replace Always Disabled)

The current `config/runtime-profile.regional-hyperpod-safe.example.yaml` gives both `nodeReboot`
and `nodeReplace` to `gpu-fault-hyperpod-adapter` as OWN. D.1/D.2 below are acceptance sub-phases that must
each leave evidence, not two independent environment switches. When only D.1 is approved, a new Runtime
Profile version must be released keeping `nodeReplace` as `OBSERVE`; do not manufacture an apparent "reboot-only" configuration by turning off the spare coordinator,
because that also removes the safety lock refusing fallback to the provider replace API.

<a id="阶段-dhyperpod-rebootprovider-replace-恒禁用"></a>
#### Phase D.1: HyperPod Reboot

- Enable only when `NodeRecovery=None` and the Runtime Profile's `nodeReboot` is explicitly `OWN`.
- Set only
  `GPU_FAULT_ENABLE_HYPERPOD_ADAPTER=true`,
  `GPU_FAULT_ALLOW_HYPERPOD_REBOOT=true` and
  `GPU_FAULT_ALLOW_HYPERPOD_REPLACE=false`.
- You must run `unset GPU_FAULT_ALLOW_HYPERPOD_MUTATION`; never set this old master switch in a regional
