| `GPU_FAULT_STORE_IO_MAX_IN_FLIGHT` | `64` | Total store calls allowed executing and waiting per API process |
| `GPU_FAULT_STORE_IO_ADMISSION_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` | `2` | Time to wait for a slot when store I/O is saturated; on timeout returns 503 with `Retry-After` |
| `GPU_FAULT_POSTGRES_POOL_MIN_SIZE` | `1` | Lower bound of resident PostgreSQL connections per API Pod |
| `GPU_FAULT_POSTGRES_POOL_MAX_SIZE` | `8` | PostgreSQL connection cap per **uvicorn process**; the cluster total cap is this value × `--workers` × replicas, and it should not be less than `GPU_FAULT_STORE_IO_WORKERS` |
| `GPU_FAULT_POSTGRES_POOL_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` | `2` | Maximum wait to obtain a database connection |
| `GPU_FAULT_POSTGRES_POOL_MAX_IDLE_SECONDS` | `300` | Idle connections are closed after this time, falling back to `POOL_MIN_SIZE`; `0` means no reclamation |
| `GPU_FAULT_POSTGRES_POOL_MAX_LIFETIME_SECONDS` | `3600` | Maximum connection lifetime; also reclaims connections pointing at the old writer after an Aurora failover |
| `GPU_FAULT_POSTGRES_STATEMENT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` | `60` | Server-side `statement_timeout` and `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout`; bootstrap DDL exempts itself |
| `GPU_FAULT_REQUEST_BUDGET_SECONDS` | `15` | End-to-end budget of ordinary HTTP requests, also bounding ingress waits, store I/O admission waits and pool checkout; `0` means unlimited |
| `GPU_FAULT_FAULT_REQUEST_BUDGET_SECONDS` | `30` | Separate end-to-end budget of the fault path; must cover the reserved fault ingress/store I/O waits so it is not cut off by the ordinary 15-second budget; `0` means unlimited |
| `GPU_FAULT_POSTGRES_AUTO_SCHEMA_INIT` | `false` | Off by default; development environments must explicitly set `true`; in production DDL is run by `gpu-fault-store-migrate --ensure-schema` or the standalone schema ensure Job, and business Pods always stay `false`, avoiding deadlocks between DDL and hot writes |
| `GPU_FAULT_POSTGRES_HOT_STATE_MODE` | `dedicated` | Storage mode of high-frequency state; before upgrading an old database the `legacy → dual → dedicated` migration must be completed; new deployments default to dedicated tables |
| `GPU_FAULT_GPU_METRICS_BATCH_RETENTION_SECONDS` | `86400` | Retention of the GPU metrics batch dedicated table |
| `GPU_FAULT_HOT_STATE_TERMINAL_RETENTION_SECONDS` | `2592000` | Retention of the terminal attempt observation and training progress dedicated tables |
| `GPU_FAULT_HOT_STATE_LATEST_RETENTION_SECONDS` | `2592000` | Retention of the GPU latest dedicated table for long-unupdated entries |
| `GPU_FAULT_GPU_FINDING_HISTORY_RETENTION_SECONDS` | `2592000` | GPU finding history is kept 30 days by default; the periodic task deletes in batches |
| `GPU_FAULT_DCGM_EXPORTER_MODE` | `auto` | `auto/existing/managed/disabled`, see A.2 |
| `GPU_FAULT_DCGM_EDGE_FILTER_ENABLED` | `true` | The node side sends only the initial baseline, health summaries, fault candidate edges/recoveries and error counter deltas |
| `GPU_FAULT_DCGM_HEALTH_SUMMARY_SECONDS` | `300` | Full health summary period when normal and unchanged |
| `GPU_FAULT_DCGM_EDGE_CONFIRMATION_SAMPLES` | `3` | Maximum consecutive confirmation samples reported for a persistent candidate fault |
| `GPU_FAULT_DCGM_VIOLATION_DUTY_CYCLE_THRESHOLD` | `0.05` | Threshold for judging edges of `*_violation_total_us` by duty cycle (Δµs ÷ actual elapsed µs), range `(0,1]`; tiny throttling counter growth below that duty cycle is no longer reported as a change and only goes out with the health summary |
| `GPU_FAULT_DCGM_HISTORY_MAX_POINTS` | `20` | Cap on in-node memory history samples attached to a fault trigger package |
| `GPU_FAULT_HOST_EDGE_FILTER_ENABLED` | `true` | Host baseline, candidate anomalies and recoveries are reported immediately; healthy samples are compressed on the node side |
| `GPU_FAULT_HOST_HEALTH_SUMMARY_SECONDS` | `300` | Host health summary period; persistent candidate anomalies are still reported per sampling period |
| `GPU_FAULT_HOST_HISTORY_MAX_POINTS` | `20` | Cap on in-node memory history points attached to Host candidate batches |
| `GPU_FAULT_DCGM_METRICS_URL` | `http://127.0.0.1:9400/metrics` | Node-local endpoint; supports `{node_ip}` and `{node_name}` |
| `GPU_FAULT_DCGM_REQUIRED_METRICS` | The set of critical fault fields | Comma means all required, `|` means alternative fields; trim only after confirming the control plane does not depend on them |
| `GPU_FAULT_NVSWITCH_TOPOLOGY_MAX_AGE_SECONDS` | `300` | Maximum age of the SXID port topology snapshot |
| `GPU_FAULT_NVSWITCH_TOPOLOGY_COMMAND` | Empty | Node-local topology query command that must output canonical JSON; the command is not run through a shell |

The current script requires the namespace to be fixed at `gpu-fault-system`.

#### Database Upgrade Procedure for Existing Deployments

This section is only for upgrading an existing deployment; it is not a parameter-setting step of a first deployment. Before the production rollout, run schema
ensure and disable startup DDL on all actually running control-plane roles:

```bash
gpu-fault-store-migrate --ensure-schema
kubectl -n gpu-fault-system set env \
  deployment/gpu-fault-api-ha \
  deployment/gpu-fault-control-worker \
  GPU_FAULT_POSTGRES_AUTO_SCHEMA_INIT=false
```

The repository provides `deploy/migrations/postgres-schema-ensure-job.yaml` to run the same step before rolling the Deployments.
The current schema version is 18 (`control-state-legacy-copy-reset-without-truncate`),
with `src/gpu_fault/schema_migrations.py` as the source of truth. v15 to v18 contain the remote-command/workflow
dedicated tables and separate cutover guards; do not fall back to old-style state migrations or cleanups that modify only `gpu_fault_objects`.
Large indexes are built online with `gpu-fault-store-migrate --build-indexes-concurrently`; the read-only validation at replica startup
now refuses to start on **missing, INVALID (left behind by an interrupted concurrent index build) or definition-drifted indexes**,
and the `gpu-fault-admin` preflight reports `indexes.missing` / `indexes.invalid` /
`indexes.drifted` accordingly; drifted indexes are dropped first and then rebuilt concurrently.

The high-frequency state dedicated-table migration must be run in the following order; you cannot jump from `legacy` straight to `dedicated`:

```bash
# 1. Create the dedicated tables and indexes
gpu-fault-store-migrate --ensure-schema

# 2. Copy the historical records from before the rollout
gpu-fault-store-migrate --backfill-hot-state

# 3. Switch the control plane to dual and roll all non-zero-replica roles
kubectl -n gpu-fault-system set env \
  deployment/gpu-fault-api-ha \
  deployment/gpu-fault-control-worker \
  GPU_FAULT_POSTGRES_HOT_STATE_MODE=dual

# 4. Backfill the rollout-window delta; every missing_or_mismatched in the output must be 0
gpu-fault-store-migrate --backfill-hot-state
gpu-fault-store-migrate --hot-state-status

# 5. Switch to dedicated and verify the dedicated tables keep growing while the old kinds stop growing
kubectl -n gpu-fault-system set env \
  deployment/gpu-fault-api-ha \
  deployment/gpu-fault-control-worker \
  GPU_FAULT_POSTGRES_HOT_STATE_MODE=dedicated

# 6. Finally purge the old kinds; the command internally runs the consistency gate again
gpu-fault-store-migrate --purge-legacy-hot-state
```

The state check uses timestamp monotonicity for latest/attempt/training: the dedicated table may be newer than the old table but not
older; immutable GPU metrics batches require identical payloads. Any missing key or dedicated-table timestamp
lagging behind refuses the purge.

<a id="52-选择-dcgm-exporter-模式"></a>
### A.2 Choosing the DCGM Exporter Mode

Keeping the default is recommended:

```bash
export GPU_FAULT_DCGM_EXPORTER_MODE=auto
```

The four modes behave as follows:

| Mode | Behaviour |
|---|---|
| `auto` | Keeps managing when this solution's DaemonSet already exists; otherwise probes the endpoint node by node. Reuses when all are compatible, deploys this solution's exporter when none exist; stops on a mixed state, missing fields or port conflict |
| `existing` | Creates no exporter; requires the endpoint of every HyperPod node to be reachable and contain the required fields |
| `managed` | Uses this solution's DaemonSet and node port `9400`; stops when an existing endpoint or occupied port is found before first creation |
| `disabled` | Deletes this solution's DaemonSet and switches node metrics collection to `nvidia-smi`; the XID/SXID collectors are unaffected |

Reusing an exporter provided by HyperPod or another platform:

```bash
export GPU_FAULT_DCGM_EXPORTER_MODE=existing
export GPU_FAULT_DCGM_METRICS_URL='http://{node_ip}:9400/metrics'
RUN_E2E=false deploy/hyperpod/deploy.sh deploy
```

The probe Job is pinned to run on every target node. `{node_ip}` and `{node_name}` in the URL are replaced before installing the node
collector. All nodes must adopt the same result, preventing some nodes from using the official exporter and others
this solution's exporter.

By default the following fault-decision-critical fields are validated:
```text
DCGM_FI_DEV_ROW_REMAP_FAILURE
DCGM_FI_DEV_ECC_DBE_VOL_TOTAL
DCGM_FI_DEV_PCIE_REPLAY_COUNTER
DCGM_FI_DEV_NVLINK_CRC_FLIT_ERROR_COUNT_TOTAL
  or DCGM_FI_DEV_NVLINK_ERROR_DL_CRC
```

This can be overridden via `GPU_FAULT_DCGM_REQUIRED_METRICS`, but validating only HTTP 200 or arbitrary
`DCGM_FI_DEV_*` fields is not acceptable. Fresh XID events are obtained by the KernelLogCollector from `/dev/kmsg`;
`DCGM_FI_DEV_XID_ERRORS` is an optional persistent-state supplement and is therefore not listed as a default hard requirement of the exporter.

The `acceleratedComputeMetrics` of the HyperPod observability add-on may only remote-write via OTel to
Amazon Managed Service for Prometheus. Without a reachable Prometheus endpoint exposed per node, the current
`DcgmMetricsCollector` cannot treat AMP as an `existing`
endpoint; in that case use the managed fallback of `auto`, or implement AMP query ingestion separately.

When switching from this solution's `managed` to an external exporter, first configure the non-default external URL, then run
the `existing` deployment. The script validates the external endpoint first and deletes this solution's DaemonSet only on success. Do not configure
this solution's own `127.0.0.1:9400` as `existing`.

<a id="53-执行部署"></a>
### A.3 Running the Deployment

```bash
deploy/hyperpod/deploy.sh deploy
```

The script completes in order:

1. Updates the kubeconfig and validates the EKS API.
2. Discovers the HyperPod `NodeRecovery`.
3. Creates the namespace, execution token and node-action secret.
4. Creates a least-privilege IRSA when the self-built lifecycle needs it.
5. Creates or takes over the encrypted Aurora Serverless v2, writer and cross-AZ reader tagged with the current `site-id`.
6. Builds the wheel, node installer package and ConfigMaps.
7. Deploys the 3-replica control plane, Service, RBAC and PDB.
8. Registers the Runtime Profile.
9. Deploys the Completion Watcher and reuses, creates or disables the DCGM Exporter per the selected mode.
10. Runs a privileged installation Job on every HyperPod node, installing the systemd collectors/Agent.
11. Checks Aurora, API, DCGM, Agent, version and config digest.
12. Runs the passive-recovery E2E that requests no GPU and performs no reset/reboot/replace.

<a id="54-观察部署"></a>
### A.4 Observing the Deployment

```bash
kubectl -n gpu-fault-system get pods -o wide
kubectl -n gpu-fault-system get deployment,daemonset,pdb
kubectl -n gpu-fault-system logs deployment/gpu-fault-api-ha --tail=200
kubectl -n gpu-fault-system logs deployment/gpu-fault-completion-watcher --tail=200
# The single-cluster deploy.sh path keeps the gpu-fault-api-canary compatibility Service.
kubectl -n gpu-fault-system port-forward service/gpu-fault-api-canary 8080:8080
curl http://127.0.0.1:8080/healthz
```

The health result should be `executor=active`, `dispatcher=enabled`, `agent_registry=enabled`.

Repeat verification:

```bash
RUN_E2E=false deploy/hyperpod/deploy.sh validate
deploy/hyperpod/deploy.sh e2e
```

<a id="55-runtime-profile-选择"></a>
### A.5 Runtime Profile Selection

The selection table, field replacement requirements and the configuration/package-data boundary are in
[`config/README.md`](../../config/README.md).

- `config/runtime-profile.regional-hyperpod-safe.example.yaml`: used by the regional CPU control plane;
  workload/scheduler actions are handed to the GPU cluster Executor; the regional production installer guarantees the Node Agent,
  signing key and fleet readiness converge, so `diagnosticBundleCapture`, `gpuReset`,
  `fabricManagerRestart` and `fabricReset` are `OWN/node-action`.
  Node capabilities whose tools or switches are not enabled, such as `nvlinkDiagnostics`, `memoryDiagnostics`, `driverRemediation` and
  `softwareFirmwareUpdate`, stay `OBSERVE`;
  `nodeReboot` is executed by the provider reboot API; `nodeReplace`, although sharing the
  `gpu-fault-hyperpod-adapter` owner, allows only
  the `HEALTHY_WARM_SPARE_ONLY` local switch. reboot is controlled by
  `GPU_FAULT_ALLOW_HYPERPOD_REBOOT`; provider replace is always disabled,
  `GPU_FAULT_ALLOW_HYPERPOD_REPLACE` must stay false, and the warm-spare path is controlled by
  `GPU_FAULT_ENABLE_HYPERPOD_SPARE_FAILOVER` and the healthy spare gates.

Register after changing `cluster_id` and `profile_version`:

```bash
curl -fsS -X POST \
  -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  --data-binary @<(python3.12 -c \
    'import json,sys,yaml; print(json.dumps(yaml.safe_load(open(sys.argv[1]))))' \
    config/runtime-profile.regional-hyperpod-safe.example.yaml) \
  http://127.0.0.1:8080/v1/runtime-profiles
```

If the registration response contains warnings, the related `OWN/DELEGATE` capabilities have been downgraded to `OBSERVE`, and actions must not be opened further.

The regional control plane shares the Runtime Profile by `profile_version` and registers it only once. `cluster_id` uses
a stable identifier as the registration anchor and must not be the HyperPod provider name; the first cluster ID may be reused, but the anchor stays unchanged
after that cluster is decommissioned and is not required to still exist in the current registry;
`profile_version` must agree with the collector, Completion Watcher and training submission parameters:

```bash
CLUSTER_ID='<stable-regional-cluster-id>'
PROFILE_JSON="$(
  python3.12 - "${CLUSTER_ID}" <<'PY'
import json
import pathlib
import sys
import yaml

path = pathlib.Path(
    "config/runtime-profile.regional-hyperpod-safe.example.yaml"
)
payload = yaml.safe_load(path.read_text())
payload["cluster_id"] = sys.argv[1]
print(json.dumps(payload, separators=(",", ":")))
PY
)"
# The regional role-split path uses the gpu-fault-api Service, not the canary Service.
kubectl --kubeconfig "${CPU_KUBECONFIG}" -n gpu-fault-system \
  port-forward service/gpu-fault-api 18080:8080
# Run in another terminal:
curl -fsS -X POST \
  -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  --data-binary "${PROFILE_JSON}" \
  http://127.0.0.1:18080/v1/runtime-profiles | jq .
unset PROFILE_JSON
```

The returned `cluster_id` must equal the registration anchor, `profile_version` and the capability owners must match
the regional deployment policy, and `warnings` must be empty. The Profile is saved in Aurora and shared immediately by the three API Pods and
all GPU clusters referencing that version, with no rollout needed. A content change within the same version must use a new version instead.

<a id="13-过渡单集群验收清单非生产"></a>
### A.6 Transitional Single-Cluster Acceptance Checklist (Non-Production)

> This section is only for maintaining historical single-cluster environments and pre-migration Canaries; it does not constitute production go-live acceptance. New production environments must
> use REG-8 "Regional deployment acceptance checklist" of §5.3 and the regional end-to-end acceptance cases.

This section applies to the **transitional single-cluster deployment** (`GPU_FAULT_DEPLOYMENT_MODE` not set to `regional`): the control plane and the
GPU cluster share one EKS, the API is reached in plaintext on 8080 inside the cluster, and the default-deny authorisation middleware is not enabled, so
the `curl` below carries no token and `/v1/fleet/agents` may also omit `cluster_id`.

**Do not use this checklist for the regionally separated deployment**; use REG-8
"Regional deployment acceptance checklist" of §5.3 instead: there the control plane is exposed only through the TLS NLB on 443 (`http://` fails to connect),
all operator calls must carry `X-GPU-Fault-Execution-Token`, `/v1/fleet/agents` must carry the
`cluster_id` scope, and three checks that do not exist for single cluster are added: executor functional readiness, unclaimed command age and executor internal
error count.

```bash
curl -fsS http://<control-plane>/healthz
curl -fsS http://<control-plane>/v1/fleet/agents
curl -fsS -X POST -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  -d '{"cluster_id":"<cluster>","node_ids":["<node>"]}' \
  http://<control-plane>/v1/fleet/readiness
curl -fsS \
  http://<control-plane>/v1/collector-status/<cluster>?node_id=<node>
curl -fsS \
  http://<control-plane>/v1/gpu-metrics/<cluster>/<node>/latest
```

Kubernetes:

```bash
kubectl -n gpu-fault-system get pods
kubectl -n gpu-fault-system get pdb
kubectl auth can-i patch nodes \
  --as system:serviceaccount:gpu-fault-system:gpu-fault-control-plane
kubectl auth can-i delete nodes \
  --as system:serviceaccount:gpu-fault-system:gpu-fault-control-plane
```

The last command must return `no`.

Acceptance criteria:

- The 3 replicas are spread sensibly and the PDB is effective.
- The Store connection uses the writer endpoint and TLS.
- All node Agent heartbeats are fresh with consistent versions/digests.
- Each node's GPU and host collectors have recent successful records; the NodeLogCollector stays disabled per default policy.
- The DCGM fields contain the metrics required by `deploy/dataplane/dcgm-counters.csv`.
- Duplicate events/terminals create no second incident or restart.
- Missing allocation and unknown XIDs trigger no destructive operation.
- After a workflow lease takeover the old replica cannot keep writing.
- The non-destructive E2E passes completely.

<a id="14-过渡单集群故障排查非生产"></a>
### A.7 Transitional Single-Cluster Troubleshooting (Non-Production)

This section covers only faults of the historical single cluster/Canary. Faults specific to the production regionally separated deployment (startup guard CrashLoopBackOff,
remote commands stuck PENDING/LEASED, reading `status_source`, dual-path distribution of node credentials) are in
REG-14 "Regional mode troubleshooting" of §5.3.

#### API Cannot Start

Check the three mandatory items of active mode:

```bash
kubectl -n gpu-fault-system describe pod <api-pod>
kubectl -n gpu-fault-system logs <api-pod>
```

- The Store URL must start with `sqlite:///`, `postgresql://` or `postgres://`.
- The execution token is at least 32 characters.
- The operation allowlist must not be empty and the enum values must be correct.

#### Agent Readiness Fails

Check:

```bash
curl --fail --silent --show-error \
  --cacert /etc/gpu-fault/node-agent.crt \
  https://<node>:9099/healthz
sudo journalctl -u gpu-fault-node-agent -n 200
curl --fail --silent --show-error \
  --cacert /etc/gpu-fault/control-plane-ca.crt \
  https://<control-plane>/v1/fleet/agents?cluster_id=<cluster>
```

Focus on comparing the Agent version, wheel SHA, policy version, profile, config digest, Node UID, boot ID and generation.

#### No DCGM Data

```bash
curl -fsS http://<node>:9400/metrics | grep DCGM_FI_DEV_ | head
sudo journalctl -u gpu-fault-metrics-collector -n 200
# Only in managed DCGM Exporter mode:
kubectl -n gpu-fault-system logs daemonset/gpu-fault-dcgm-exporter --tail=200
```

If the exporter lacks the project fields, mount `deploy/dataplane/dcgm-counters.csv`. Do not treat the `nvidia-smi` fallback, which has no NVLink/PCIe data, as complete DCGM.

#### Workflow Stays WAITING

Query:

```bash
curl http://<control-plane>/v1/workflows/<request-id>
```

Check the waiting step, `adapter_operation_id`, whether old Pods are still active/terminating, whether the HyperPod observer can map the NodeLogicalId, and whether the Agent heartbeat satisfies compatibility.

#### Workflow BLOCKED

This is a safe state. Complete the allocation, workload state, capability owner, collector freshness or Adapter per `blocked_reasons`; do not bypass the evidence gate by widening the allowlist.

#### Administrators Must Not Call HyperPod Provider Replace

This solution **never calls** `BatchReplaceClusterNodes` (see §2.2 constraint 2 and
[High-Level Design](high-level-design.md) §2.1); the constraint holds for automation and administrators alike. Any
`BatchReplaceClusterNodes`, `BatchDeleteClusterNodes` or equivalent provider replace
operation is an anomaly, not a break-glass path.

`REPLACE_NODE` in a workflow means only
`HEALTHY_WARM_SPARE_ONLY`: a local switch from the managed, same-specification, healthy customer-owned node pool.
Without a qualified spare, keep the faulty node isolated and enter the manual degraded new attempt of §8.2 or support
escalation; do not destroy the faulty instance to gamble on the provider temporarily allocating new GPU capacity.
##### When an Alternative Remediation May Start: Preconditions

**The only correct precondition is that the workflow's `MARK_UNSCHEDULABLE` step has `SUCCEEDED`.**

It is not "wait for the workflow to reach a terminal state", nor "wait for the reboot attempt to fail". The reason: isolation is **step 2** of every repair
path, not a failure fallback.
In `src/gpu_fault/orchestration/workflow_builder.py::WorkflowBuilder.catalog_operations`,
`RESET_GPU`/`REBOOT_NODE`/`REPLACE_NODE` share one branch whose first statement is
`operations.append(MARK_UNSCHEDULABLE)`; the `CHECK_MECHANICALS`,
`UPDATE_SWFW`, `RESET_ALL_GPUS_AND_NVSWITCHES` and
`ESCALATE_OPERATOR` branches likewise isolate first.

How to query:

```bash
curl -H "X-GPU-Fault-Execution-Token: $TOKEN" \
  http://<control-plane>/v1/workflows/<request-id> \
  | jq '.step_executions[] | select(.operation=="MARK_UNSCHEDULABLE")
        | {status, details}'
```

The node appearing in `details.isolated_nodes` means the isolation has been persisted. At that point the node simultaneously has
(written atomically by one `patch_node` in `_isolate()` of
`src/gpu_fault/adapters/kubernetes/node_operations.py::KubernetesNodeOperationsMixin._isolate`):

- The taint `gpu-fault.io/quarantined=<incident-derived value>:NoSchedule`
- `spec.unschedulable=true`
- The annotations `gpu-fault.io/incident-id`, `gpu-fault.io/fencing-token`,
  `gpu-fault.io/previous-unschedulable`

⚠ **cordon + taint only blocks new scheduling and does not evict existing Pods.** What really stops existing GPU workloads is
`STOP_WORKLOADS`, and it **is inserted only when `workload_state=ACTIVE`**
(`WorkflowBuilder.catalog_operations`). So the strict precondition is:

> `MARK_UNSCHEDULABLE` has SUCCEEDED **and** the node has no existing GPU workloads
> -- i.e. `STOP_WORKLOADS` has also SUCCEEDED, or planning judged IDLE and never inserted that step.

Re-check once with `kubectl` before acting; do not rely on the workflow alone:

```bash
kubectl get pod -A --field-selector spec.nodeName=<node> \
  -o custom-columns='NS:.metadata.namespace,NAME:.metadata.name,PHASE:.status.phase' \
  | grep -v Succeeded
```

##### No Need to Avoid the Warm Spare Allocation Window

Early documentation suggested "avoiding the window in which `spare_coordinator.allocate()` has taken a spare but not yet
`release()`d"; that advice **was wrong and has been withdrawn**. Code facts:

- `SPARE_RESERVATION_ANNOTATION = "gpu-fault.io/spare-reservation"` stores the
  **incident_id**, not a node reference (`hyperpod_spares.py:576-588`);
- Spare identification is **node-local**:
  `src/gpu_fault/hyperpod_spares.py::HyperPodSpareCoordinator.allocate`
  filters by the spare's own
  `gpu-fault.io/spare` label / `_declared_spare_names`.

The faulty node and the spare are two independent objects; keeping the faulty node isolated, sending it for repair or permanently decommissioning it does not break an existing
spare reservation, nor does it constitute a reason to call provider replace. The successful warm-spare path
**deliberately does not call** `release()`; only
the exception branch of `src/gpu_fault/adapters/hyperpod/lifecycle.py::HyperPodLifecycleStepAdapter.execute` releases the reservation,
because after success the spare has become a working node. The only residual effect is the capacity waste of "consuming one more spare",
which is not a window that needs avoiding.

##### Permitted Remediation Steps

```bash
# 1. Confirm the preconditions (see above).
# 2. Record the HyperPod instance id and EC2 instance id for later reconciliation.
kubectl get node <node> -o jsonpath='{.metadata.labels}' | tr ',' '\n' | grep -i instance

# 3. Only one of two is allowed:
#    A. Continue this solution's HEALTHY_WARM_SPARE_ONLY workflow;
#    B. When spares are insufficient run §8.2, creating a reduced or remapped new attempt.
#
# Calling any provider replace/delete cluster-node API is forbidden.
false "provider replace is prohibited; use warm spare or §8.2"
```

##### Cleaning Up Fleet Records After a Node Disappears Through External Repair or Permanent Decommissioning

After a node disappears, the control plane's agent record **does not disappear automatically**. The stale record still takes part in the
comparison set of the fleet consistency gate (`fleet.py:666-680`), misjudging "the whole fleet has upgraded" as
"N nodes still run the old value", distorting the `NODE_STALE` / `PIN_AHEAD_OF_FLEET` classification,
and all subsequent node actions FAIL at the gate.

Reconcile first, then clean up:

```bash
curl -s -H "X-GPU-Fault-Execution-Token: $TOKEN" \
  "http://<control-plane>/v1/fleet/agents?cluster_id=$CLUSTER_ID" \
  | jq -r '.[] | "\(.node_id) \(.lifecycle_state) gen=\(.generation)"'
kubectl get nodes -o name | sed 's|node/||'
```

For node_ids that exist only on the fleet side, run drain -> revoke:

```bash
BODY='{"expected_generation":<current generation>,
       "transition_id":"revoke-<node>-<date>",
       "reason":"node replaced; stale fleet record"}'

curl -X POST -H "X-GPU-Fault-Execution-Token: $TOKEN" \
  -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d "$BODY" \
  "http://<control-plane>/v1/fleet/agents/$CLUSTER_ID/<node>/drain"

curl -X POST -H "X-GPU-Fault-Execution-Token: $TOKEN" \
  -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d "$BODY" \
  "http://<control-plane>/v1/fleet/agents/$CLUSTER_ID/<node>/revoke"
```

⚠ Three pitfalls:

1. **You must drain before revoke, and the `transition_id` of both requests must be identical.**
   `fleet.py:787-793`: a state other than `DRAINING` or a mismatched `transition_id` returns
   `409 agent must be drained by the same transition`. A direct revoke always fails.
2. `expected_generation` is an optimistic lock and must be the **current** `generation`.
3. **Check `lifecycle_state` first: if it is already `REVOKED`, leave it alone.** A node automatically revoked by the workflow's
   `REPLACE_NODE` step has a `transition_id` like
   `workflow-<id>/<idx>/REPLACE_NODE/hyperpod-lifecycle`; draining it returns
   `409 agent already belongs to another transition`, which is normal and needs no handling.

##### Adding an Independently Repaired and Re-accepted Node to the Spare Pool

`health_reasons()` (`hyperpod_spares.py:462`) rejects a "schedulable" candidate spare with the reason
`"unreserved spare is schedulable"`. So a new node entering the pool needs **both the label and the cordon**; with only the label and no cordon,
that spare is judged unhealthy and never selected.
The supported declaration action is `gpu-fault-admin config spare`; **do not hand-run `kubectl label` /
`kubectl cordon`** -- manual operations leave no baseline, and there is nothing to restore exactly at release time:

```bash
# Read-only check: reports item by item what this node still lacks (Ready, HyperPod health, reservation,
# pool state, isolation residue, active GPU Pods, exactly one ACTIVE Node Agent, same topology as the fault node),
# changes nothing; exit code 1 when items are unmet
gpu-fault-admin config spare --state-dir /secure/gpu-fault --node <new-node> \
  [--fault-node <fault-node>] [--cluster-id <cluster>]

# Declare: writes only the spare label and spec.unschedulable=true,
# first writing the pre-declaration label/cordon baseline to <state-dir>/warm-spares/<new-node>.json before changing the node
gpu-fault-admin config spare --state-dir /secure/gpu-fault --node <new-node> \
  --declare --reference <CHANGE_ID> --confirm DECLARE_WARM_SPARE_CORDON

# Release: restores exactly per the recorded baseline (a node already cordoned before the declaration stays cordoned)
gpu-fault-admin config spare --state-dir /secure/gpu-fault --node <new-node> \
  --release --reference <CHANGE_ID> --confirm RELEASE_WARM_SPARE_UNCORDON
```

The command **does not write** `gpu-fault.io/spare-pool-state`: that annotation belongs to the control plane, and the spare pool accepts it when missing too.
A "labelled but schedulable" half-declared spare (for example when a validated restore lifted
the isolation together with the cordon) may be `--declare`d again to complete the cordon; a node already labelled and already cordoned
is refused as "already declared". Repeated declaration is refused while an unreleased record exists; the only exception is an interrupted declaration whose patch never took effect
and whose baseline still matches the node's present state (the record has no `declared_state`): the next `--declare`
archives it into the record's `superseded_interrupted_declarations` and simply re-declares, without a prior
`--release`. A resourceVersion conflict when modifying the node (the node concurrently updated by kubelet heartbeats etc. between the check and the modification)
re-reads and retries with the new version, at most 3 times, and only when the spare label, cordon,
`spare-reservation` annotation and isolation ownership are all unchanged; otherwise it refuses rather than overwriting. Release refuses nodes carrying
`spare-reservation`, with pool state `ALLOCATED`, still carrying the isolation taint/ownership annotation, or not declared by
this command (no unreleased record). An unreachable control-plane Store is a named refusal, never a silent pass.
Console output lands, like the other mutating verbs, in `<state-dir>/logs/mutating/config-*.log`.

##### Manual Cleanup of Isolation Residue (Only When the Workflow Never Reached `RESTORE_SCHEDULING`)

When a workflow succeeds, `RESTORE_SCHEDULING` automatically cleans up the isolation state -- measured: the taint,
`unschedulable` and the three `gpu-fault.io` isolation annotations all disappear, with no human intervention.
**Only when the workflow FAILED / BLOCKED midway and will never reach that step** do you need to manually reproduce the semantics of
`_restore()` in
`src/gpu_fault/adapters/kubernetes/node_operations.py::KubernetesNodeOperationsMixin._restore`:

```bash
kubectl patch node <node> --type=merge -p '{
  "metadata": {"annotations": {
    "gpu-fault.io/incident-id": null,
    "gpu-fault.io/fencing-token": null,
    "gpu-fault.io/previous-unschedulable": null
  }},
  "spec": {"unschedulable": false, "taints": []}
}'
```

⚠ `"taints": []` clears **all** taints. If the node still has business taints unrelated to this solution,
you must instead read the taints array, filter out only the entries with `key == gpu-fault.io/quarantined`
and write it back
(which is what `src/gpu_fault/adapters/kubernetes/node_operations.py::KubernetesNodeOperationsMixin._restore`
does).
The target value of `unschedulable` should be taken from the `gpu-fault.io/previous-unschedulable` annotation;
this solution writes `false` by default.

⚠ **Do not delete the four `gpu-fault.io/installer-*` annotations**
(`installer-config-digest`, `installer-node-uid`, `installer-state`,
`installer-version`) -- they belong to the node installer and are unrelated to isolation.

##### Known Gap: No Notification When Isolation Completes

The current implementation **sends no notification** at the moment isolation succeeds -- `_isolate()`
(`src/gpu_fault/adapters/kubernetes/node_operations.py::KubernetesNodeOperationsMixin._isolate`) contains no
`save_notification_if_absent`.
The first email the administrator receives is the XID investigatory action email sent **synchronously** at ingest time
(`src/gpu_fault/app/`, **before** `dispatcher.wake()`), when isolation has not yet executed.
**So "the node is already isolated when the notification arrives" does not currently hold; the order is reversed.**

warm-spare replace itself already has two kinds of notifications:

- The success path in `src/gpu_fault/adapters/hyperpod/lifecycle.py::HyperPodLifecycleStepAdapter.execute` calls
  `_notify_warm_spare_replaced()` of
  `src/gpu_fault/adapters/hyperpod/notifications.py::HyperPodNotificationMixin._notify_warm_spare_replaced`,
  sending the replacement-complete email;
- When spares are insufficient, `src/gpu_fault/hyperpod_spares.py::HyperPodSpareCoordinator._alert` saves a capacity alert whose body states
  the workflow is still blocked and the faulty node stays isolated.

What is still missing is the `MARK_UNSCHEDULABLE` completion notification shared by all repair paths. So the warm-spare
success email cannot serve as a substitute for "notify as soon as the node is isolated".

Until that gap is fixed, **do not rely on emails to decide whether you may act**; you must actively query the
`MARK_UNSCHEDULABLE` step state as at the start of this section.

Improvement direction (two trigger points whose semantics must stay separate):

| Trigger point | Notification content | Scope |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `MARK_UNSCHEDULABLE` SUCCEEDED | "The node is isolated, no new workloads will be scheduled onto it, repair in progress" -- pure status notice, **without** a replace recommendation | All repair paths |
| official action is `REPLACE_NODE` **and** `MARK_UNSCHEDULABLE` SUCCEEDED | "This decision requires a warm-spare switch; without a qualified spare perform manual degradation or support escalation; provider replace is forbidden" | warm-spare replace path only |

The two conditions are a **conjunction**: the former decides whether to send the warm-spare/manual-degradation recommendation, the latter decides when
it is safe to send. **"Isolation succeeded" alone must not be the trigger of that recommendation** -- `RESET_GPU` isolates too,
and if "node replacement needed" were notified on isolation success, a node that needs only a GPU reset and self-heals within tens of seconds would be misreported as
needing replacement capacity.

"A fault a restart can fix is not misreported as replace" is guaranteed by the escalation ladder itself (one-way; both single-node and multi-node records
first climb within the record, `src/gpu_fault/execution/branch_escalation.py`, and only when a rung is refused by the budget,
the outcome is unknown or the rungs are exhausted does
`src/gpu_fault/orchestration/escalation.py::HardwareEscalationService.classify`
take over with a successor workflow):

```
RESET_GPU failed    → next_action = REBOOT_NODE
RESTART_NODE failed → next_action = REPLACE_NODE
REPLACE_NODE failed → ESCALATE_OPERATOR
```

So an official action of `REPLACE_NODE` has only two origins: (a) a hardware fault the policy directly judges to need
a warm-spare switch (a restart is useless); (b) a **new incident** escalated after a reboot was really attempted and failed
(`event_id = "replace-after-<original workflow id>"`) -- by then it is also proven that a restart
does not solve the problem. And a workflow whose official action is `RESET_GPU`/`REBOOT_NODE` never
plans a `REPLACE_NODE` step at all.

The minimal implementation change: `ADVISORY_OPERATION_PRIORITY` at `notification_service.py:42-46`
already ranks `REPLACE_NODE` as the highest priority (30), and
`src/gpu_fault/notification_preview.py::NotificationPreviewService.preview`
already implements "pick the highest-priority step to notify", but the whole path is blocked by
`step.execution_owner.startswith(MANAGED_HYPERPOD_OWNER_PREFIX)` --
`MANAGED_HYPERPOD_OWNER_PREFIX = "hyperpod-managed-"`, while under the hard constraint
`NodeRecovery=None` the node action owner is `gpu-fault-hyperpod-adapter`, the prefix does not
match, and `try_preview()` silently returns `None` (measured injection response
`advisory_notification_id: null`). If that notification is added, this solution's adapter owner should be added to
the checked whitelist, and a new template version explicitly stating "warm-spare only, provider replace forbidden" should be used;
the priority selection and `save_notification_if_absent` deduplication can be reused, and the old provider-replace wording
must not be revived.
